# Changelog

Per-release changes for the UAIP plugin. Before reading the entries below, please skim the **Versioning Policy** section so you know what each version number means (breaking change vs additive vs fix).

---

## Versioning Policy

UAIP follows [Semantic Versioning 2.0.0](https://semver.org/).

### Reading version numbers

A version number is `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`.

| Segment | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **MAJOR** (e.g. `1.x.x` → `2.x.x`) | Breaking changes. May require updating command names, parameter shapes, or config files on upgrade |
| **MINOR** (e.g. `1.0.x` → `1.1.x`) | Backward-compatible additions — new commands, new capabilities, new optional parameters. Existing calls keep working |
| **PATCH** (e.g. `1.0.0` → `1.0.1`) | Bug fixes only. No impact on the public API |

### Current phase: 1.x.y (Fab Pro released)

The current version is **1.2.0**. UAIP is **released on Fab as the Pro product** ([listing](https://www.fab.com/listings/0eedf909-00ac-4d95-b109-8fda51800fff)). For the changes since 1.0.0 — the first entry of the `1.x.y` series — see the release list below.

- Standard SemVer rules now apply strictly: breaking changes require a MAJOR bump (e.g. `2.0.0`)
- New commands / new capabilities ship as MINOR (e.g. `1.0.x` → `1.1.0`)
- Bug fixes ship as PATCH (e.g. `1.0.0` → `1.0.1`)

### Pre-1.0 history: 0.x.y series

Versions `0.9.0` and `0.9.1` were demo-only releases distributed via GitHub Releases ahead of the Fab Pro submission. Their entries are preserved below for historical reference.

### Pre-release tags

Pre-release tags are not used in routine development. The only exception is an optional `1.0.0-rc.1`-style RC (release candidate) build distributed via GitHub Releases immediately before Fab submission. An RC build ships **identical code** to what will be submitted to Fab — if no issues are found, the same code is re-tagged as `1.0.0`.

### Deprecation policy (rarely removed)

UAIP maintains a single source tree across UE versions using version macros (not per-version branches). Because of this, **commands shipped to users are not removed once released**.

- Commands scheduled for retirement are marked `Stability: "Deprecated"` but **keep working**
- `uaip_describe_command` exposes the `DeprecationMessage` and `MigrationTarget` (recommended replacement)
- The only exception: when Epic removes an underlying engine API and UAIP can no longer maintain the implementation, the affected command may be removed during a MAJOR bump

This guarantees that older command names — which may be baked into an AI agent's training data or working memory — keep functioning long-term.

### Demo and Pro share the same version number

The free demo (GitHub Releases) and Pro (Fab) **always share the same version number**. Both are built from the same source with only feature gating differences, so HealthCheck's `UAIPVersion` field returns the same value either way.

---

## Releases

> Unreleased and upcoming changes are tracked on the [`next` branch changelog](https://github.com/Naotsun19B/UnrealAIIntegrationPlatform-Document/blob/next/docs/en/changelog.md).

### Unreleased

Changes that have shipped in the plugin repository but are not yet released on Fab.

#### UAIP Plugin

**Added**

- **New command `DismissMenus`** (`UAIPEditorUIAutomation` module, `UAIP.Editor.UIAutomation`, now 17 commands; also in the demo build): closes the open menus and popups and waits until they are gone. Without arguments it closes every open menu; with a `WidgetRef` it closes the menu that holds that widget and the submenus opened from it. It does not move the keyboard focus, is refused only while a drag is in progress, waits up to 10 frames and 1 second, and reports the state before and after (`MenusOpenBefore`, `MenuWindowCountBefore`, `MenuWindowCountAfter`, `MenuStackOpenAfter`, `FramesWaited`, `KeyboardFocusWidgetType`). A `WidgetRef` of `""`, a non-string or a malformed value is `InvalidParams`; a widget that is destroyed or in no menu is `NotFound` with `Result.Reason: WidgetDestroyed` / `WidgetNotInApplicationMenu`; a shutdown during the wait is `ExecutionFailed` with `Reason: DeliveryCancelledByShutdown`. Menus that are not registered in the application's menu stack are not closed. It needs `EditorUIAutomation`; to close a menu opened from inside a modal dialog, add it to `[UAIP.CommandPump] StarvedTickAllowList`. The description of `PressKey` now points to it, because Escape can be consumed by the widget that receives it. See [Commands Reference](commands.md#uaipeditoruiautomation) and [Configuration](config.md#uaipcommandpump--running-commands-while-a-modal-dialog-is-up-off-by-default).
- **`SnapshotUI` can return what is typed in input fields (`IncludeFieldValues`)** (`UAIPEditorUIAutomation` module): with `IncludeFieldValues: true` the node of each input text carries `HintText` and `Value` (256 characters, `ValueTruncated`), and a password field, or a field whose password state cannot be determined (a multi-line input box), carries `ValueWithheld` instead, with `ValueWithheldCount` and `PasswordValuesWithheldReason` in the snapshot. The default is `false`, and `LabelContains` never looks at a value. The plain value of a password field is returned only when **both** the new safety setting `AllowPasswordFieldRead` (`[UAIP.SafetyPolicy]`, default `False`, launch flag `-uaip-policy-allow-password-field-read`, read at startup) **and** the new DefaultDenied capability `EditorWidgetPasswordTextRead` allow it; it then appears only in the response (`Result.PasswordFieldValues`, at most 16 values of 256 characters), never in the saved snapshot, the log or the completion notification. `SnapshotUI` does not require the capability itself, so without it the command still succeeds with the values withheld. `AllowRuntimePasswordFieldRead` (launch flag `-uaip-policy-allow-runtime-password-field-read`) is the counterpart for the game viewport. `QueryCapabilities` reports both as `OperationalConstraints.IsPasswordFieldReadAllowed` and `OperationalConstraints.IsRuntimePasswordFieldReadAllowed`. See [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#editor-operations) and [Configuration](config.md#safetypolicy-cli-flags).
- **Better labels in `SnapshotUI`** (`UAIPEditorShared` module): a widget with no name and no accessibility text now has its type name as `Label` instead of `None`, and a rich text block (`SRichTextBlock`) has an approximation of what it shows, with the tags removed. A rich text block is now in the default roster of `SnapshotUI` (role `tb`), so node counts, the numbering of refs and the point where `MaxNodes` is reached can change. `DumpSlateTree`'s `AccessibleText` follows the same rules. The label of a password field, or a field whose password state cannot be determined, is now always its hint text or its type name, never its accessibility text. A button's label, which is built from its children, does not improve when the child is rich text; the `Label` is not the string `[Label=...]` is matched against.
- **A refused UI operation says why** (`UAIPRuntimeInput` / `UAIPEditorUIAutomation` modules): when `ClickWidget`, `HoverWidget` or `PressKey` is refused with `PolicyViolation` (`DragInProgress`, `ModalActive`, `ForeignMenuActive`), the response `Result` and the saved report now both carry a `GuardDiagnostics` object — the type and window kind of the target, whether it belongs to a menu in the application's menu stack, where the keyboard focus is, how many menu windows are open, and the title of the modal window. Only type names, window kinds and the modal title are reported, never widget text. A widget now counts as "inside the open menu" when it belongs to a menu in the application's menu stack (or is inside a menu-type window), not when it is within 50 parents of one, so a widget deep inside a menu and a menu that opens without a window of its own (a combo box in the PIE game view) are no longer refused as "outside the menu".
- **New ini setting `[UAIP.UIAutomation] GuardWriteCommands`** (default `False`; read once at startup; no launch flag): when `True`, `InputText`, `FillForm`, `SetCheckboxState` and `SetComboSelection` are checked once, when the request is accepted, in the same way as the click. `FillForm` is all or nothing; a refusal names the first refused field as `Result.RejectedFieldName`, which holds that field's `WidgetRef`. See [Configuration](config.md#uaipuiautomation--guarding-the-write-commands-off-by-default).
- **`SnapshotUI` no longer returns an empty artifact for a very deep tree** (`UAIPEditorUIAutomation` module): a widget that would nest deeper than 30 levels of emitted widgets is left out together with what is below it, `Traversal.OutputNestingLimitReached` is `true`, `Traversal.OutputNestingSkippedCount` counts the widgets left out, and `Traversal.Complete` is `false`. If the JSON still cannot be produced the command fails with `ExecutionFailed`, and the refs of the previous snapshot stay valid.
- **New capability `ScenarioSensitiveValueToUndeclaredParam` (DefaultDenied)** (`UAIPScenario` module): lets a scenario pass a sensitive value to a parameter that the receiving command has not declared as a destination for one (see **Changed**). ⚠️ **Granting it fully releases the protection of sensitive values for the scenarios of the session that holds it:** the receiving command may write the value into its response, artifacts and log, and a later step may carry it on. Only the replacement of the step's `ErrorMessage` and the masking of the recorded request remain. It is not granted by default; see [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#scenario-execution).
- **Mass Entity support** (new `UAIPRuntimeMass` and `UAIPEditorMass` modules): AI agents can now observe a running Mass simulation and read and assemble the assets it is built from. 13 commands in two domains, none of them in the demo build.
  - `UAIP.Runtime.Mass` (6 commands, editor only): `DumpMassEntitySettings`, `GetMassEntityCount`, `DumpMassEntityArchetypes`, `DumpMassProcessorGraph`, `DescribeMassEntity` and `GetMassEntityFragments` — the engine's Mass settings, entity counts and archetypes (with paging and element filters), the processor graph per phase, the element types one entity carries, and the values of its fragments. Everything requires `RuntimeInspect`; `GetMassEntityFragments` also requires the new DefaultDenied capability `RuntimeMassFragmentValueRead`, because those values are project data. Five of them accept `TargetNetRole` / `TargetPIEInstance`.
  - `UAIP.Editor.Mass` 🧩 (7 commands, requires the `MassGameplay` plugin): `ListMassEntityTraits`, `DumpMassEntityConfig` and `ValidateMassEntityConfig` read (`EditorInspect`), and `AddMassEntityConfigTrait`, `RemoveMassEntityConfigTrait`, `SetMassEntityConfigTraitProperty` and `ConfigureMassSpawner` edit. Each edit is one undoable step and is refused during PIE. The editing commands require the new DefaultDenied capability `MassAssetEdit`; the new `MassCustomTypeEdit` is required in addition to bring in a trait or spawn data generator from outside the engine's Mass modules, and the new `MassProjectCodeExecution` wherever a call makes project code run.
  - **⚠️ Granting `MassProjectCodeExecution` lets a project's own trait code run inside the editor** — in a private world created for one call and in the editor world — and a trait that stops in an unexpected state can bring the editor down. It does not stop the engine from running custom trait code in reaction to generic commands (starting PIE, deleting an actor, saving, Undo / Redo), and it is judged per trait class. Loading a config asset that is not loaded yet also loads the StateTrees it refers to, and the engine's ordinary loading steps can run on StateTree nodes the project wrote in C++; the capability does not stop that either.
  - With the editor's Typed Elements Data Storage (TEDS) disabled, loading `UAIPRuntimeMass` makes the editor load the `MassEntity` module, so Mass subsystems can be created in editor-side worlds. This stays inside the editor; with TEDS enabled (the default) there is no new side effect.
  - While a Mass Debugger tab is open, the temporary world used to evaluate a config makes one engine-internal `ensure` fail and print a call stack. The command still succeeds.
  - An element name that matches more than one type (`GetMassEntityCount`, `DumpMassEntityArchetypes`, `GetMassEntityFragments` and `ListMassEntityTraits`) is refused with `InvalidParams`, and the failed response's `Result` lists the full type paths it may mean (`AmbiguousName`, `Candidates` — sorted, at most 10 — and `TotalCandidateCount`), so the call can be repeated with one of them.
  - In `GetMassEntityFragments`, a struct nested deeper than 4 levels of expansion is returned by its type name only, and `Limitations` says so with `StructDepthTruncated` (one entry per type, `Detail` is the type path).
  - In every bounded list of both domains, `<List>Truncated` means more entries exist beyond everything the response returned (`Data` and artifact together), with the same value in both; `Data` also carries `<List>DataEntriesTruncated`, which is true when it lists fewer entries than the artifact. An artifact that holds every entry leaves `Truncated` false however short `Data` is. `ArtifactTruncated`, `NextStartIndex` and `<List>TotalCount` are unchanged.
  - See [Runtime](commands.md#uaipruntimemass), [Editor](commands.md#uaipeditormass-) and [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#mass-observation-and-editing).

- **Chaos destruction observation** (new `UAIPRuntimeChaosDestruction` module): AI agents can now observe the Chaos destruction of a running game. Adds 8 commands under `UAIP.Runtime.ChaosDestruction` — `ListGeometryCollections` and `DumpGeometryCollectionState` (which Geometry Collection components the world holds, how many pieces are broken, and the state, transform and velocity of each piece), `DumpChaosFieldState` (the physics fields that act on the world), `StartDestructionCapture` / `GetDestructionEventLog` / `StopDestructionCapture` (record destruction events into a capture owned by the calling session and read them back as a time series, even after the play session ended; a stopped capture stays readable for 30 minutes), `CaptureGeometryCollectionStrain` (the strain each piece carries on the next physics step) and `EnableForcedDestructionNotify` (turn on the game's destruction notifications so that a capture can record the engine's own event data). Everything requires `RuntimeInspect`; `EnableForcedDestructionNotify` also requires the new DefaultDenied capability `ChaosDestructionForceNotify`, because it changes the running game: the game's own listeners receive the events too, and the solver's event filters it raises cannot be lowered until the play session ends (a notification itself is restored when the capture stops). `EnableForcedDestructionNotify` and `ChaosDestructionForceNotify` are not provided in the demo build; the other seven commands are. See [Commands Reference](commands.md#uaipruntimechaosdestruction) and [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#runtime-chaos-destruction).

- **Navigation observation** (new `UAIPRuntimeNavigation` module): AI agents can now check the navigation mesh and path finding of a world, in the editor or during play. Adds 8 commands under `UAIP.Runtime.Navigation` — `DumpNavigationSystemState` (navigation data, generation settings, bounds, areas and costs, build state and the search node ceilings), `FindPath` and `IsLocationReachable` (a path between two points, or only whether the end can be reached, with a three-valued `Reachable`, the reason, and the conditions the query actually used; `AgentActor` runs the query as an AI would), `DumpNavMeshTiles`, `QueryNavModifiers`, `GetPathFollowingState` (what the path following component of an AI is doing, play world only), `WaitForNavigationBuild` (waits until the navigation system is quiescent; a passive wait) and `RebuildNavigation` (queues a regeneration of the navigation mesh of a play world and answers at once; not provided in the demo build). Seven commands need only the DefaultAllow `RuntimeInspect`. `RebuildNavigation` needs the new DefaultDenied capability `RuntimeNavigationRebuild`, and a path query above 8192 search nodes needs the new DefaultDenied `RuntimeNavigationExtendedSearch`. All eight accept `TargetNetRole` / `TargetPIEInstance`. See [Commands Reference](commands.md#uaipruntimenavigation) and [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#runtime-navigation).

- **Game viewport UI observation and operation** (`UAIPRuntimeObservation` / `UAIPRuntimeInput` / `UAIPEditorUIAutomation` modules): AI agents can now read and operate the UI of a running game's viewport (UMG, Slate, HUD). Adds 7 commands. Under `UAIP.Runtime.Observation`, 4 read commands: `DumpGameViewportWidgets` (the widget tree of the game viewport), `FindGameViewportWidgets` (find widgets by condition), `DumpGameViewportWidgetTexts` (the texts the widgets show) and `DumpHUDState` (the HUD and its canvas). Under `UAIP.Runtime.Input`, 3 operation commands: `ClickGameViewportWidget`, `PressGameViewportUIKey` and `TypeGameViewportText`. All seven accept `TargetNetRole` / `TargetPIEInstance`. The four read commands are available in the demo build; the three operation commands are not. Adds 4 capabilities: `RuntimeUIInputInjection` (required by all three operation commands), `RuntimeUIEditorReservedKeyInput` (keys the editor reserves for itself), `RuntimeWidgetTextRead` (reading widget texts) and `RuntimeWidgetPasswordTextRead` (reading the text of a password field), all DefaultDenied. Adds 2 settings: `AllowRuntimePasswordFieldWrite` under `[UAIP.SafetyPolicy]` (default `False`; also the launch flag `-uaip-policy-allow-runtime-password-field-write`; without it `TypeGameViewportText` refuses a password field) and `EnableHUDCanvasStats` under `[UAIP.RuntimeObservation]` (default `False`; no launch flag; takes effect after the editor or game is restarted; without it `DumpHUDState` leaves out the canvas draw statistics). **Changes to commands that already existed:** a Runtime command that needs a play world now answers `NotAllowed` when there is none (Observation, Input, World, GAS, Assertion and Execution used `ExecutionFailed` before; Niagara used `PreconditionFailed`), so a scenario no longer retries such a step by itself and a scenario that relied on the retry to wait for PIE must wait explicitly; `DumpWorldState`, `DumpActorState`, `DumpComponentState`, `DumpRuntimeLog`, `CapturePerformanceSnapshot` and `SearchLoadedClasses` now require `RuntimeInspect`, `CaptureViewportImage` requires `RuntimeCapture` and `CheckpointCapture` requires `RuntimeInspect` as well (all DefaultAllow, so only a configuration that denies one of them notices); those commands now also run under `ReadOnly` and while a modal dialog is open; `CaptureViewportImage` captures the world that `TargetNetRole` / `TargetPIEInstance` select instead of another PIE instance's screen; and `PlayerIndex` of the new commands is the local player's number, not the player controller's. **Editor UI automation:** `ClickWidget`, `AcceptDialog` and `CancelDialog` of `UAIP.Editor.UIAutomation` now report `PartialDelivery` (`DownOnly`) when only the press of a click reached the widget. See [Commands Reference](commands.md#compatibility-what-changed-for-commands-that-already-existed), [UAIP.Runtime.Observation](commands.md#uaipruntimeobservation), [UAIP.Runtime.Input](commands.md#uaipruntimeinput) and [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#runtime-game-viewport-ui).

- **Data Layer runtime state control** (`UAIPRuntimeWorld` module): AI agents can now drive World Partition Data Layer streaming in a *running* play world and verify the result, instead of only authoring Data Layers in the level asset. Adds 5 commands under `UAIP.Runtime.World` — `SetDataLayerRuntimeState` (set one instance to `Activated` / `Loaded` / `Unloaded`, optionally recursing into every descendant and reporting each refused one with a reason token), `ListDataLayerRuntimeStates` (every instance with its type, both runtime states, parent and network constraints; the array is in the artifact and the response carries `Count`), `GetDataLayerRuntimeState` (one instance in full, including which ancestor is holding its effective state down), `WaitForDataLayerStreaming` (poll until streaming settles — `TimeoutSeconds` defaults to 30 with a ceiling of 60, `DataLayerInstanceIds` takes at most 64 entries, and a value outside either bound is `InvalidParams` before the wait starts) and `ListPlayWorlds` (every play world the engine holds, with an empty list rather than an error when nothing is playing). Adds 1 DefaultDenied capability, `RuntimeDataLayerControl`, required by `SetDataLayerRuntimeState` alone; the four observation and wait commands need no capability. Data Layer identifiers are the same ones `UAIP.Editor.WorldPartition` uses. A level without World Partition answers a Degraded Success with `bIsWorldPartitionEnabled: false` on the reads, rather than an error. Two streaming completion flags are reported side by side (`bStreamingCompleted`, scoped to the Data Layers in question but blind to spatially loaded cells; `bWorldStreamingCompleted`, world-wide but not narrowable) because neither covers the other.
- **Target play world selection for Runtime commands** (`UAIPRuntimeShared` module plus every Runtime domain module): a networked PIE session runs a server and one or more clients in the same editor process, and **102 Runtime commands** now accept two optional parameters saying which of them to act on — `TargetNetRole` (`"Standalone"` / `"Server"` / `"Client"`, case-insensitive; `"Server"` matches dedicated and listen alike) and `TargetPIEInstance` (the index `ListPlayWorlds` reports). Omitting both reproduces the previous behaviour exactly, so **no existing call changes meaning**. A role matching several worlds with no instance given, or a role and an instance that disagree, is `InvalidParams` with every candidate listed; a target that matches nothing is `NotAllowed`. Only play sessions inside the same editor process can be selected — a separately launched dedicated server is never a candidate. Toolset bridge commands (`Toolset.*`) deliberately do not take these parameters, because a bridge's schema is generated from what the engine toolset declares. `CheckpointCapture`, which is composed of `CaptureViewportImage` and `DumpWorldState`, forwards the target to both of them, so one checkpoint's screenshot and state dump always describe the same world. See [Commands — Choosing which play world a Runtime command acts on](commands.md#choosing-which-play-world-a-runtime-command-acts-on).
- **`RunEditorPythonScript` can interrupt a script that runs too long** (`UAIPEditorExecution` module): a new optional `AbortAfterSeconds` parameter (integer, `0`-`1800`; `0` disables it; omitting it or passing `null` uses the ini default) raises an exception inside the script on the game thread once the deadline passes and repeats it until the script stops, so a runaway loop no longer holds the editor for good. An interrupted call answers `Timeout` with `Data.Aborted: true` and `Data.NotExecuted: false`; **the script has run partially and its edits are kept**, so check the editor state before running it again. `Data` also reports `AbortAfterSeconds`, `AbortSource` (`Argument` or `Config`), `DeadlineExceeded`, `AbortSignalsSent` and `ElapsedSeconds`. It is best effort: a script blocked inside a native call is interrupted only when that call returns, and a script that catches the exception is not stopped. The abort works only on the supported Python version (3.11); on another version, a call that asks for one is refused with `AbilityUnavailable`. It cannot run inside another Python execution on the game thread (`NotAllowed`), and before Python has initialized it answers `PreconditionFailed`. Adds a `[UAIP.Python]` ini section with `DefaultAbortAfterSeconds` (default `0`, so **existing behavior is unchanged unless you set it**); it is read once at startup and cannot be overridden from the command line. See [Configuration](config.md#uaippython--default-abort-deadline-for-runeditorpythonscript).
- **`RunFunctionalTest` and `WaitForCondition` now report `Data.TimedOut: true` when they run out of time** (`UAIPRuntimeExecution` / `UAIPRuntimeAssertion` modules): a caller can tell a deadline expiry from other failures by the flag instead of by parsing the message. `WaitForCondition` sets it on both its paths, with and without progress notifications. The `ErrorCode` is unchanged.

- **UAIP's own automation tests that depend on test-only types now register and run in the shipped plugin too** (`UAIPCore` and every domain module that has test-only types): about 1,900 tests (roughly 17% of the suite) use reflection types that exist only for testing. These types used to be built only in the development repository, so on your machine those tests were either not registered or always skipped. They are now compiled into the plugin you install. In normal use they stay out of sight: they do not appear in the editor's type pickers, Blueprint parent-class and variable-type menus, or class lists, and they are left out of the results of UAIP's type-listing commands. They become visible only while a test that uses them is running, and are hidden again when the test ends, whether it passed or failed. The tests appear in the Automation test list and run like any other test. Two leftovers to know about: after running tests in an editor session, a type menu the engine cached during the run can still show a test-only type until you restart the editor; and a few pickers that list every struct without filtering (for example the DataRegistry item-struct picker) show a test-only struct that the engine offers no way to hide. Commands and capabilities are unchanged. Directly naming a test-only type in a command still resolves it, and bringing it into an asset is still subject to the usual capability check. **To leave the test-only types out of the build**, add the following to your project's `Config/DefaultUAIP.ini` (add the `[UAIP.Build]` section and the line if they are not there — a file carried over from an older version has neither), then rebuild:

  ```ini
  [UAIP.Build]
  bCompileTestFixtures=False
  ```

  The tests that depend on these types are then not registered; every other test runs as before. **This works only when the plugin is in your project's `Plugins/` folder and you build it from source**, and it takes a rebuild — restarting the editor is not enough. A precompiled plugin (for example one installed under the engine) cannot leave them out, and the setting is ignored there. If the setting and the build disagree, the editor logs a warning at startup that names the value, what the binary was built with, and what to do. See [Configuration](config.md#uaipbuild--compiling-the-test-only-types).
- **New capability `GauntletUnrestrictedArguments` (DefaultDenied)** (`UAIPRuntimeExecution` module): `RunGauntletTest` needs it for the arguments that change what the test targets or how the child process behaves — the parenthesized per-test parameter syntax, dangerous keys, keys that start with the test name, and values such as absolute paths, UNC paths and URLs. It is not granted by default; see [Safety](safety.md). Characters that cannot be passed safely on an external process command line stay refused even with it.
- **`SelectMenuItem` now accepts labels that contain `...`** (for example `Edit/Project Settings...`): a label shown by the menu listing can be used as it is in `MenuPath`. A path segment that is only `..` and control characters are still refused with `InvalidParams`, and the error text now names the key as `MenuPath`.
- **New command `GetRestartStatus`** (`UAIPEditorWorkspace` module, `UAIP.Editor.Workspace`): reports the state of a `RestartEditor` attempt — `Scheduled`, `CloseRetrying`, `Declined` (with `DeclineReason`, `DeclineDetail`, `Remedy` and the error code of the outcome) or `ExitStarted`. It takes an optional `AttemptId` (the most recent attempt when omitted), is read-only and requires `EditorLifecycle`. Every answer, `NotFound` included, carries `ProcessId`, the id of the editor process that answered. See [Commands](commands.md#uaipeditorworkspace).
- **`HealthCheck` is documented as returning `MinBridgeVersion`**, the oldest MCP Bridge version the plugin accepts (the response has carried it since the bridge version check was introduced; the reference had left it out).
- **New ini setting `[UAIP.EditorRestart] CloseDeadlineSeconds`** (default `60`, range `10`-`600`): how long an accepted `RestartEditor` may wait for the editor to close before it is declined with `CloseTimedOut`.
- **CLI request size limits are documented and counted the same way on every path** (`UAIPTransportCLI` module): a request is limited to 1 MiB (1,048,576 bytes), counted as UTF-8 bytes on the inline, file, stdin and stream paths alike, so a non-ASCII character such as Japanese counts as 3 bytes. The operating system limits the command line to 16,383 characters, so pass a large request with `-uaip-request-file=` or `-uaip-scenario-file=`. In stream mode, a line over the limit is rejected with `InvalidParams` and no `SessionId`, and the stream keeps running.
- **The CLI transport accepts absolute file paths** (`UAIPTransportCLI` module): `-uaip-request-file=`, `-uaip-scenario-file=` and `-uaip-response-file=` may now be absolute as well as relative to the project root, as long as the path falls under the allowed directory (`Saved/UAIP/Requests/` for request and scenario files, `Saved/UAIP/Responses/` for the response file). A path outside it is still refused with `InvalidParams`.
- **SaveGame slot operations** (new `UAIPRuntimeSaveGame` module, `UAIP.Runtime.SaveGame`): AI agents can now list, inspect, read, back up, prepare, delete and restore the game's save slots, so a test can start from a particular save — or from no save — and put things back afterwards. Adds 10 commands: `ListSaveGameSlots`, `GetSaveGameInfo` and `ListSaveGameSnapshots` (names, sizes, times, header metadata such as `Status`, `Format`, `PackageFileUEVersion` — a string such as `"UE4=<n> UE5=<m>"` — and the content hash, without reading any value or loading any class; a class name cut to 256 characters is marked `SaveGameClassNameTruncated`, and a damaged snapshot is still listed with `Status: "Corrupt"` so it can be deleted), `DumpSaveGame` (the property values as a JSON artifact), `DeleteSaveGame`, `CopySaveGameSlot` and `WriteSaveGame` (create a slot from a class and JSON values, or patch an existing slot), and `SnapshotSaveGame`, `RestoreSaveGame` and `DeleteSaveGameSnapshot`. Adds 4 capabilities: `RuntimeSaveGameInspect` (DefaultAllow) for the three inspection commands, and the DefaultDenied `RuntimeSaveGameContentRead`, `RuntimeSaveGameWrite` and `RuntimeSaveGameUnvalidatedWrite`. Nothing is ever loaded: the SaveGame class and the objects a slot refers to must already be loaded. Every slot UAIP writes is validated up to its SaveGame header, except when an unchanged snapshot goes back to the slot it was taken from, or when `SkipValidation` (which needs `RuntimeSaveGameUnvalidatedWrite`) is given. Slot changes are refused during PIE and under `DisableSave`. ⚠️ A dump artifact holds the save's contents as they are, with no secret masking, and stays until artifact garbage collection removes it; snapshots hold the raw bytes and stay until `DeleteSaveGameSnapshot` removes them, so `RuntimeSaveGameWrite` alone can reach the contents through a snapshot file. Adds a `[UAIP.SaveGame]` ini section with `bAllowWritesDuringPIE` (default `False`) and `MaxSaveFileBytes` (default 64 MiB, range 1 MiB to 1 GiB). See [Commands](commands.md#uaipruntimesavegame) and [Safety](safety.md#runtime-savegame-slots).
- **AI perception observation** (new `UAIPRuntimeAI` module, `UAIP.Runtime.AI`): AI agents can now find out, during play, what an AI character sees, hears and remembers, and get step-by-step clues to why it does not see something — the questions behind "the enemy does not notice the player", which until now could only be answered by watching the editor's debug display. Adds 6 read-only commands: `DumpAIPerceptionState` (one AI's perception component — its sense configs and, for every perceived actor, the state of each stimulus: `Sensing`, `LostButRemembered`, `Expired` or `Unknown`), `GetPerceivedActors` (the actors an AI senses right now, or also still remembers, optionally restricted to one sense or to hostile actors), `GetActorPerceivers` (the reverse lookup: which AIs hold stimuli about an actor, and whether that actor is a sight target at all), `DumpAIPerceptionSystemState` (whether the perception system is usable in the world and why not, which senses run, sight query counts and every perception component), `ExplainSightPerception` (recomputes "can this AI see that actor" step by step from the current state and names the first step that fails — a non-authoritative diagnosis that says whether it agrees with the engine's last result) and `DumpAIPerceptionSenseConfigs` (every sense config property with its value, including the ones a project-defined config declares, within size bounds). All six accept `TargetNetRole` / `TargetPIEInstance`, and each saves its full result as a JSON artifact. A perception system that is unavailable in the observed world (a client world, AI disabled in the world settings, a custom AI system) is answered with a success that names the reason and the incomplete sections, never with a silent empty list. Adds 1 capability, `AIPerceptionConfigDump` (DefaultDenied), required by `DumpAIPerceptionSenseConfigs` in addition to `RuntimeInspect`; the other five need only the DefaultAllow `RuntimeInspect`, and are available in the demo binary. ⚠️ An artifact `DumpAIPerceptionSenseConfigs` produced stays after the capability is taken away. See [Commands](commands.md#uaipruntimeai) and [Safety](safety.md#runtime-ai-perception).
- **Behavior tree and Blackboard observation** (`UAIPRuntimeAI` module, `UAIP.Runtime.AI`): AI agents can now find out, during play, which node of a behavior tree an AI is running, which decorators pass, what its Blackboard holds and how the tree moved over time — the questions behind "the enemy does not chase the player", which until now could only be answered by watching the editor's behavior tree debugger. Adds 8 commands. Four observe the current state: `ListBehaviorTreeAgents` (the actors running a tree), `GetActiveBehaviorTreeNode` (the active node and its path to the root), `DumpBehaviorTreeState` (the stack of tree instances with the active node, the active decorators and services and the parallel tasks, the descriptions and decorator verdicts that are safe to give by default, the reason and the required permission for every one left out, and the Blackboard key definitions) and `GetBlackboardValues` (the value of every key). Three record how the tree moves over time: `StartBehaviorTreeCapture`, `GetBehaviorTreeEventLog` and `StopBehaviorTreeCapture` (the tree starting and stopping, pausing and resuming, subtrees being entered and left, the active node and its activity, decorators, services and parallel tasks; read once per tick, so a recording cannot show several moves within one frame or why a move happened, and it says so in `Limitations`). The eighth, `DescribeBehaviorTreeWithProjectCode`, describes what the others must leave out because producing it runs project code (the descriptions of project and Blueprint nodes, the values of project-defined key types). It is not read-only, and is not part of the demo binary. All commands accept `TargetNetRole` / `TargetPIEInstance` except the log and stop commands, which take a `CaptureId`. The first seven are in the demo binary. See [Commands — Behavior tree and Blackboard observation](commands.md#behavior-tree-and-blackboard-observation).
- **StateTree observation** (new `UAIPRuntimeStateTree` module, `UAIP.Runtime.StateTree`, requires the `StateTree` and `GameplayStateTree` plugins): AI agents can now see, during play, which states of a StateTree are active, which tasks run and which delayed transitions are waiting, and record why the tree moved. Adds 7 commands: `ListStateTreeComponents`, `GetActiveStateTreeState` and `DumpStateTreeState` (the current state), `DumpStateTreeInstanceData` (the values of the tree's parameters, the active states' parameters and the active nodes' instance data) and `StartStateTreeCapture`, `GetStateTreeEventLog` and `StopStateTreeCapture` (states entered, exited, completed and selected, the transitions taken with their trigger and source, and the events sent — payloads are recorded as a type name only). A component class the project derived is not read, because it cannot be verified that it runs its tree the way the engine component does. A recording needs an engine build that provides StateTree debug notifications (the Development and DebugGame editor); while one runs, StateTree execution is slightly slower. Not part of the demo binary. See [Commands — UAIP.Runtime.StateTree](commands.md#uaipruntimestatetree-).
- **Three new DefaultDenied capabilities for the AI observation above**: `BlackboardValueRead` (`GetBlackboardValues`, `DescribeBehaviorTreeWithProjectCode`) reads Blackboard values; `AIBehaviorProjectCodeExecution` (`DescribeBehaviorTreeWithProjectCode`, not in the demo binary) runs project code to describe a tree; `StateTreeInstanceDataDump` 🧩 (`DumpStateTreeInstanceData`) reads StateTree parameter and node values. None is granted by default; enable them in `[UAIP.SafetyPolicy]`. Without them the other commands still work, and what they leave out is listed with the reason and the permission that would produce it. **Be aware of what the default-allowed commands still show:** whether a Vector, Rotator, Object or Class key is set, the pass/fail verdict of decorators on the active path, and the execution path itself (active nodes, active states, transitions) — together with the tree's structure these allow inferring values. This is accepted as the least information needed to diagnose an AI's decision; see [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#runtime-ai-behavior-trees-and-blackboards).
- **Behavior tree nodes now carry `ExecutionIndex`** (`UAIPEditorBehaviorTree` module): `GetBehaviorTreeNodeList` and `GetBehaviorTreeSubtree` report, for every node, the number the node has when the asset runs as a top-level tree — the same number the runtime commands report for it — so a node seen during play can be found in the asset, without PIE and without saving. It is `null` with `ExecutionIndexUnavailableReason` (`NotInCompiledTree` or `ContextDependentInjectedDecorator`) for a node the runtime tree does not reach and for a root decorator of the asset. `GetBehaviorTreeNodeProperties` lists a different, transient `ExecutionIndex` property that is filled in only after the tree was opened in the BehaviorTree editor; its description now says so. It is not the `node_index` of the Toolset bridge commands.
- **Animation state observation** (new `UAIPRuntimeAnimation` module, `UAIP.Runtime.Animation`): AI agents can now observe the animation state of a character in a running game, without reading the Animation Blueprint debugger by eye. Adds 7 read-only commands: `DumpAnimInstanceState` (the update state, state machines, blend weights, montages and curves of the main, linked and post process anim instances of one skeletal mesh component, in one capture), `GetAnimStateMachineStates` (the active state of each state machine, the time spent in it and the transitions in progress), `GetAnimBlendWeights` (the final blend weight of the playing assets, blend spaces and layered blends), `GetActiveMontages` (the montages that are active, a montage that is blending out included), `GetAnimCurveValues` (the animation curves with their values and kinds), `DumpAnimInstanceVariables` (the values of the variables the project's own anim instance classes declare) and `GetMotionMatchingState` (which pose the last Motion Matching search chose and its total cost). All seven accept `TargetNetRole` / `TargetPIEInstance`, and each saves its full result as a JSON artifact. `GetMotionMatchingState` is registered only while the PoseSearch plugin is enabled. Adds 1 capability, `AnimInstanceVariableDump` (DefaultDenied), required by `DumpAnimInstanceVariables` in addition to `RuntimeInspect`, because the variables of a project's own classes can hold any project data; the other six need only the DefaultAllow `RuntimeInspect`. Six of the commands are available in the demo build (`GetMotionMatchingState` is not), and `AnimInstanceVariableDump` stays DefaultDenied there too. ⚠️ An artifact `DumpAnimInstanceVariables` produced stays after the capability is taken away. See [Commands](commands.md#uaipruntimeanimation) and [Safety](safety.md#runtime-animation).

- **Subsystem observation** (`UAIPRuntimeObservation` module): AI agents can now see which subsystems are alive in a running game and — when you allow it — read the values inside one. Adds 2 commands under `UAIP.Runtime.Observation`: `ListSubsystems` (the subsystems of the engine, the game instance, the play world and the local players, one row per instance with its class, owner and status, sorted into one fixed order and paged with `StartIndex` / `MaxEntries`; with `IncludeNotCreated` it also lists the loaded classes that have no instance under an owner; the `Engine` scope works without a play world) and `DumpSubsystemState` (the property values of one subsystem instance, saved as a JSON artifact). Adds the capability `SubsystemStateDump` (DefaultDenied), required by `DumpSubsystemState` in addition to `RuntimeInspect`: the values are whatever the project stores, secrets included, a property that a worker thread is rewriting cannot be read safely, and an artifact that was already produced stays after the capability is taken away. Allow it with `+AllowedCapabilities=SubsystemStateDump` under `[UAIP.SafetyPolicy]`; the demo build's default configuration does not grant it. Both commands accept `TargetNetRole` / `TargetPIEInstance` (not with the `Engine` scope alone) and are available in the demo build. See [Commands](commands.md#subsystems-2) and [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#runtime-subsystems).

- **Network and replication observation** (new `UAIPRuntimeNetwork` module, `UAIP.Runtime.Network`): AI agents can now inspect the network side of a running game — its net drivers, its connections and how an actor replicates —, compare what a client received with what the server holds, and reproduce a poor connection. Adds 7 commands: `GetNetDriverState` (the net drivers of a world with their settings, traffic totals and packet emulation settings, and whether a client is still connecting), `ListNetConnections` (the connections of one net driver, paged, with round trip time, jitter, loss, traffic and the kind of remote address), `DumpActorReplication` (an actor's replication settings, its replicated properties with their conditions and, on the server, what is known about sending it to each connection), `DumpReplicatedPropertyValues` (the values of an actor's replicated properties as the server or a client holds them), `CompareReplicatedProperties` (each replicated property compared between the server and a client as `Equal`, `Different` or `NotComparable` with a reason; it never claims that the actor is in sync, and a `Different` is not proof of a bug), `SetNetworkEmulation` (change the packet emulation — latency, loss, jitter, duplication, reordering — of a net driver or of one connection; the settings before the first change are saved) and `ResetNetworkEmulation` (put them back). All seven accept `TargetNetRole` / `TargetPIEInstance`. Adds 3 DefaultDenied capabilities: `RuntimeNetConnectionAddressRead` (the raw IP address and port, for `ListNetConnections` with `IncludeRemoteAddress`), `RuntimeReplicatedPropertyValueRead` (`DumpReplicatedPropertyValues` and `CompareReplicatedProperties`; the values can include the replicated `UniqueId` of a `PlayerState` and the names of players) and `RuntimeNetworkEmulationControl` (`SetNetworkEmulation` and `ResetNetworkEmulation`; restoring needs it too, so reset before taking it away). `GetNetDriverState`, `ListNetConnections` and `DumpActorReplication` need only the DefaultAllow `RuntimeInspect`. ⚠️ An artifact produced while the address or value capability was granted stays after the capability is taken away. `GetNetDriverState`, `ListNetConnections`, `DumpActorReplication` and `RuntimeNetConnectionAddressRead` are available in the demo build; the value, comparison and emulation commands are not, and the emulation commands are not registered in Shipping builds either. See [Commands](commands.md#uaipruntimenetwork) and [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#runtime-network-and-replication).

- **CommonUI observation** (new `UAIPRuntimeCommonUI` module, `UAIP.Runtime.CommonUI`, requires the `CommonUI` plugin): AI agents can now see, during play, which activatable widgets a CommonUI game has pushed and which one is in front, what CommonUI does with input, and the texts its actions are shown with. Adds 4 commands: `DumpActivatableStacks` (every activatable widget container — stack or queue — with the widget it shows, whether it is between two widgets, and its entries in the order they were added, plus the activated widgets in no container), `GetInputState` (for each local player, the input mode the action router applies, whether it lets game input through and why, the front activated widget on UE 5.8 and later, the actual focus, the input type and, with `IncludeActions`, the bound actions and their keys), `GetActionDisplayNames` (the three display texts of each bound action, written to the artifact only) and `DescribeActivatableWidgetsWithProjectCode` (the input configuration and the focus target an activatable widget asks for, which only project code can produce). The first three run no project code and say which fields they left out in `ProjectCodeFields`; every widget carries `WidgetPath`, and a widget in the game layer also `TypePath` and `Fingerprint`, so it can be passed to `FindGameViewportWidgets` (`WithinWidgetPath`) and to the game viewport UI operations. All four accept `TargetNetRole` / `TargetPIEInstance`; their `PlayerIndex` is the local player's number, not the player controller's. `GetActionDisplayNames` needs `RuntimeWidgetTextRead` (DefaultDenied) in addition to `RuntimeInspect`. Adds 1 capability, `CommonUIProjectCodeExecution` (DefaultDenied), required by `DescribeActivatableWidgetsWithProjectCode`, which is not read-only. Installing the integration also adds CommonUI's verdict to the gate of the input injection commands (see Changed). Not part of the demo binary. See [Commands — UAIP.Runtime.CommonUI](commands.md#uaipruntimecommonui-) and [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#runtime-commonui-observation).

- **New command `EvaluateInputGateWithProjectCode`** (`UAIPRuntimeInput` module, `UAIP.Runtime.Input`): reports the verdict the gate of the input injection commands reaches for a player controller (`PlayerIndex`, default 0), evaluated exactly as an injection evaluates it — including the project's overrides of the game viewport client — without injecting anything. It succeeds when the gate blocks too. Adds 1 capability, `InputProjectCodeExecution` (DefaultDenied), required in addition to `RuntimeInspect`; the command is not read-only. Not part of the demo binary, where the capability is registered and marked `NotInThisEdition`. See [Commands — The injection gate](commands.md#the-injection-gate) and [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#runtime-game-input-gate).

- **`DumpInputState` reports why it inferred the input mode and what the injection gate decides** (`UAIPRuntimeInput` module): new fields `InputModeEvidence` (the game viewport and player controller values the input mode was inferred from, and the last input mode debug string in development builds), `PlayerControllerPath` (the object path of the player controller `PlayerIndex` resolved to, so a caller can tell which player controller a dump describes), `ViewportClientClass`, `ViewportClientIsVerifiedEngineClass`, `GameInputGate` (the gate's `Decision`, the judge that decided it, the viewport read status and the installed contributor's verdict; for a project viewport client class it names `InputProjectCodeExecution` and `EvaluateInputGateWithProjectCode`) and `Limitations`. When the Enhanced Input state cannot be read on the running engine version, `EnhancedInputUnavailableReason` says why. See [Commands — DumpInputState output](commands.md#dumpinputstate-output).

- **`FindGameViewportWidgets` can search inside one widget** (`UAIPRuntimeObservation` module): the new `WithinWidgetPath` restricts the search to the subtree of the widget at that object path (for example a `WidgetPath` the CommonUI commands return). It is not a condition by itself, so a request with it alone is still `InvalidParams`. A path that does not resolve is `NotFound`. The walk and its limits still start at the root of the game layer, and the value is echoed back. See [Commands](commands.md#uaipruntimeobservation).

- **New SafetyPolicy setting `AllowProjectNetDriverNetModeQuery`** (`UAIPRuntimeShared` module; default `False`; launch flag `-uaip-policy-allow-project-net-driver-net-mode-query`): the Runtime commands now work out a play world's net mode without calling the net driver's overridable server check, so a project net driver's code no longer runs inside a read-only command. The answer is unchanged unless the project's net driver changes what being a server means; for such a driver, turning the setting on restores the engine's own answer, and read-only Runtime commands then run that project code (a warning is logged once). Takes effect after a restart. See [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#safetypolicy-settings).

- **Sensitive response values can be declared at a nested position** (`UAIPCore` module): a command can now mark a field inside an array of its response `Data` as sensitive, not only a top-level field. The positions are dot paths in which `[]` stands for every element of an array — for example `Connections[].RemoteAddress` or `Components[].Properties[].Value` — and `DescribeCommand` reports them under `SensitiveResponseData` (the key is unchanged; a plain name is still a top-level field). The completion notification replaces the value at each position with `"<redacted>"`; the response returned to the caller and the artifacts are unchanged. `ListNetConnections` now hides each `RemoteAddress` this way, and `DumpReplicatedPropertyValues` hides each property `Value`. A malformed path masks the whole `Data` and is logged as an error when the command is registered. In a scenario, a step that takes such a value (or the array or object holding it) from an earlier step's response is recorded masked. See [Commands — Sensitive values and where they are masked](commands.md#sensitive-values-and-where-they-are-masked).
- **New command `SetEditorWindowState`** (`UAIPEditorWorkspace` module, `UAIP.Editor.Workspace`, also in the demo build): minimizes the editor main window, or restores it from the minimized state, and returns once the window has settled. `State` is `Restored` or `Minimized`. `Restored` does not bring the editor in front of an application the user is working in (Windows keeps it behind, and the keyboard focus stays there; the window can be captured all the same), and a window that was maximized before it was minimized is maximized again. A request for the state the window is already in returns at once with `Changed: false`. It waits up to 2 seconds, and is refused with a `Reason` (`Headless` / `BlockedByModal` / `Hidden` as `NotAllowed`, `NoWindow` as `PreconditionFailed`) when there is nothing to act on. The reason a capture was refused (see Changed) tells an AI when to use it.
- **New capability `EditorWindowStateControl` (DefaultDenied)** (`UAIPEditorWorkspace` module): `SetEditorWindowState` needs it. It is registered in the demo build too and is not marked unavailable there; add `+AllowedCapabilities=EditorWindowStateControl` to `[UAIP.SafetyPolicy]` to use it.
- **`DumpEditorState` reports the state of the main window** (`UAIPEditorObservation` module): the new `MainWindowState` carries `State` (`Normal` / `Minimized` / `Maximized` / `Hidden` / `NoWindow`; a window that has never been shown reports `Hidden`), `Size`, `RestoredSize` and `CaptureBlocker` (`None` when a capture can be taken, otherwise the `Reason` a capture would be refused with). `MainWindowSize` is unchanged: it is the size of the first top-level window, which is not always the main frame, and its description now says so.
- **Window size mismatches are detected, synchronized and reported** (`UAIPRuntimeObservation` / `UAIPEditorWorkspace` / `UAIPEditorObservation` modules): after a restore from the minimized state, the size Slate holds for a window could stay different from the size the OS reports, and the capture was a broken image. Every capture command now compares the two sizes before it captures and tries to synchronize them. If that does not fix it, the capture is refused with the new `Reason` `WindowSizeMismatch` (`NotAllowed`); the remedy is to minimize and restore the main window again with `SetEditorWindowState`, or to ask the user to change the size of any other window. `SetEditorWindowState` also synchronizes after `Restored` and, when it cannot be fixed, answers `NotAllowed` / `Reason: WindowSizeMismatch` with `OsClientSize` and `SlateSize`. `DumpEditorState` gains `SizeCheck`, `SizeMismatched`, `OsClientSize` and `SlateSize` in `MainWindowState`, and a root `WindowMessageRecording` (the recent window size messages from the OS: the last 64 of each regular Slate window such as the main window, up to 8 windows, so notification toasts cannot push them out, plus the last 64 of all other windows together; each message has `IsPriorityWindow` and a `MessageSizeMeaning` of `ClientArea`, `WholeWindow` or `ProposedWholeWindow` saying what `MessageSize` measures, and the overview gains `PriorityWindowCapacity`, `MaxPriorityWindowCount`, `PriorityMessageCount` and `SharedMessageCount`). `DumpEditorState` only reads and never synchronizes, so `CaptureBlocker` can say `WindowSizeMismatch` for a mismatch that a synchronization would have fixed. Recording can be turned off with `RecordWindowMessages` under `[UAIP.RuntimeObservation]` (default `True`, read once at startup, so it takes effect after a restart). The remedy for a background tab now also explains the `OwnerTabId` of `OpenTabById`. See [Capture refusals](commands.md#capture-refusals) and [Configuration](config.md).
- **`QueryCapabilities` marks the capabilities the running edition does not ship** (`UAIPCore` module): with `IncludeUnavailable: true`, an entry of `RegisteredCapabilities` can carry `UnavailableReason: "NotInThisEdition"`. Only the free demo build marks anything. A marked capability is never granted, whether it is named in `+AllowedCapabilities`, declared by a command provider or DefaultAllow, and naming it in `AllowedCapabilities` logs a startup warning (`... is not available in this edition of UAIP. The grant has no effect.`). See [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#capabilities-the-demo-build-does-not-ship).
- **The demo guide now states what the demo restricts and what it does not** ([Demo Version Guide](demo.md#what-the-demo-does-and-does-not-restrict)): no semantic editing commands; saving, shutting down and restarting stay because observation depends on them; UI automation can change and save assets; and why `SaveAsset` is left out while `SaveAllPackages` stays. It also says why `OpenPasswordTestWindow` is in the demo (it only opens a window and changes nothing).
- **Override any parent or interface function from a Blueprint** (`UAIPEditorBlueprint` module): `AddGraphNode` accepts `NodeType: "Event"` with the event name in `FunctionName` (for example `ReceiveDestroyed`), the way the Override menu of the My Blueprint panel adds an event node; an existing override is returned instead of a second node. The new `AddFunctionOverride` command (`UAIP.Editor.BlueprintEditing`) overrides a function that returns a value, has output parameters or is const as a function graph with the parent's entry and result nodes and a call to the parent implementation, compiled unless `SkipCompile` is `true`; asking again returns the existing graph with `AlreadyExisted: true`. Each command refuses the other's kind of function with `InvalidParams` naming the command to use. Both require `BlueprintGraphEdit`; no new capability. See [Variables & graph](commands.md#variables--graph-11).
- **Control Rig runtime-asset format (UE 5.8 default) is supported** (`UAIPEditorControlRig` / `UAIPEditorAssets` / `UAIPEditorSequencer` modules): the Control Rig commands accepted only the legacy Blueprint format and stopped with "Invalid asset path or asset class." on the new default. Every `UAIP.Editor.ControlRig.*` native command now accepts both formats with the same parameters, and so do the Sequencer commands `FindOrCreateControlRigTrack`, `BakeToControlRig` and `AddMixerChildTrack`.
  - Class paths: runtime asset `/Script/ControlRig.ControlRigRuntimeAsset` (UE 5.8 default), legacy `/Script/ControlRigDeveloper.ControlRigBlueprint`. The previously documented `/Script/ControlRig.ControlRigBlueprint` never resolved on UE 5.8 and is corrected.
  - `CreateAsset` must be given the class that matches the project's `RigVM.BlueprintIndependentAssets` setting; a mismatch is `InvalidParams` with the right class and the setting to change. UAIP never changes the setting.
  - `Toolset.Editor.ControlRig.*` rejects a runtime-asset Control Rig with `UnsupportedOperation` and names the equivalent `UAIP.Editor.ControlRig.*` command. `Toolset.Editor.ControlRig.Create` keeps creating the legacy format whatever the setting says.
  - `AddVariable` returns the actual variable name and recompiles; on a runtime-asset Control Rig `IsReadOnly` is accepted but has no effect (an engine limitation).
  - `BakeToControlRig` on a runtime-asset Control Rig reads `Tolerance` as a fraction of the value range, and refuses reduction with `PreconditionFailed` when the sequence is not open in the Level Sequence editor (pass `Tolerance: 0` to skip reduction).
  - `CreateAsset` with `ClassName=/Script/Engine.Blueprint` and a `FactoryParams.ParentClass` that derives from `UControlRig` is refused with `InvalidParams` before anything is created; the message names the Control Rig class to use as `ClassName` (the generic Blueprint factory made a plain Blueprint there, without the `ControlRigBlueprintCreate` check).
  - `Toolset.Editor.SequencerControlRig.*`: on UE 5.8 the 37 commands that use `ControlRigPath` reject a runtime-asset Control Rig with `UnsupportedOperation` before delegating (the delegated toolset finds the rig by its Blueprint class). The other 26 commands are unchanged; `ZeroControlRigTransforms` is one of them, because it accepts `ControlRigPath` for compatibility but does not use it.
  - See [Control Rig asset formats](commands.md#control-rig-asset-formats).

**Changed**

- **⚠️ Breaking change — the six generic property reads return the same error codes as the writes** (`UAIPEditorProperty` module): `GetActorProperty`, `GetAssetProperty`, `GetBlueprintDefault`, `GetDataTableRow`, `GetProjectSetting` and `GetWorldSetting` used to refuse a bad property path with `ExecutionFailed` whatever the reason. They now return `NotFound` for a property that does not exist and `InvalidParams` for a malformed or unreachable path, like their `Set*` counterparts. A target that cannot be found is still `ExecutionFailed`. `GetDataflowNodeProperty` / `SetDataflowNodeProperty` (`InvalidParams`) and `GetSectionProperty` (`ExecutionFailed`) keep their codes. See [UAIP.Editor.Property](commands.md#uaipeditorproperty).
- **⚠️ Breaking change — a fixed-size array is read whole, and a struct one needs an index mid-path** (`UAIPEditorShared` / `UAIPEditorDataflow` modules): reading a fixed-size array without an index (`Values`) now returns every element as a JSON array instead of element 0 alone, and that array can be written back unchanged. A path that steps through a fixed-size array of structs without an index (`Arr.Member`, including Sequencer channels such as `Translation.Times`) is refused with `InvalidParams` and names `Arr[0]` to `Arr[N-1]`, instead of silently using element 0. Dataflow node properties follow the same rules. See [Writing references, structs and containers](commands.md#writing-references-structs-and-containers).
- **⚠️ Breaking change — a scenario can no longer pass a sensitive value to a parameter that is not declared for it** (`UAIPScenario` module). A `${...}` template may copy a sensitive part of an earlier step's response (for example the `PasswordFieldValues` of `SnapshotUI`) only into a parameter that the receiving command declares as a destination: `Text` of `InputText` and of `TypeGameViewportText`, and the `Value` of each entry of `Fields` of `FillForm` (not the entry's `WidgetRef`). A scenario that passed such a value anywhere else used to run; its step now fails without running, with `PolicyViolation` and `Result.Reason: SensitiveValueToUndeclaredParam`, a fixed message that holds neither the value nor the expression, and no retry. **The key is compared exactly, case included:** with `Text` declared, a sensitive value placed in `text` is refused. Fix the scenario to use the declared parameter, or grant `ScenarioSensitiveValueToUndeclaredParam` (see **Added**). The `ErrorMessage` of a step that took a sensitive value is now a fixed sentence in the scenario result and in the completion notification, and the text given to `InputText` and the values given to `FillForm` are now masked in the completion notification and audit records. See [Sensitive values and where they are masked](commands.md#sensitive-values-and-where-they-are-masked).
- **⚠️ Breaking change — `DumpGameViewportWidgetTexts` needs `AllowRuntimePasswordFieldRead` to return the plain value of a password field** (`UAIPRuntimeObservation` module). The capability `RuntimeWidgetPasswordTextRead` alone used to be enough. It is now required **together with** `AllowRuntimePasswordFieldRead=True` under `[UAIP.SafetyPolicy]`. A call that relied on the capability alone does not fail: the values are withheld and a new limitation, `PasswordTextWithheldBySafetyPolicy`, says how many fields were withheld. With the setting on and the capability missing, the limitation is still `PasswordTextWithheld`. Set the option and restart the editor to get the old behaviour back. See [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#runtime-game-viewport-ui).
- **⚠️ `Toolset.Editor.SlateInspector.SnapshotUI` hides the lines of input fields** (`UAIPEditorUIAutomation` module): the label of every line that belongs to an input field — password or not — and of every line whose ref starts with `tb` is replaced with `"(withheld)"`, because the engine renders the text being typed as the label. A line that cannot be tied to a widget is withheld too. A result of an unexpected shape fails with `InternalError`. To read the value of a field, use the native `SnapshotUI` with `IncludeFieldValues`.
- **⚠️ Public API change — `ISlateAppStateReader` has five new pure virtual functions** (`UAIPRuntimeInput` module, `SlateInput/ISlateAppStateReader.h`): `GetUserIndexForKeyboard()`, `FindWidgetWindow(Widget)`, `IsWidgetInApplicationMenu(Widget)`, `GetVisibleMenuWindowCount()` and `GetActiveModalWindowTitle()`. The plugin's own implementation and test mocks implement them. **If your own code derives from `ISlateAppStateReader`, it no longer compiles until it implements these five.** The interface also now routes the guard's reads that used to go straight to Slate, so a mock sees them. In `FSlateInteractionGuard`, the new `CheckSafeToInteractWithDiagnostics` returns the refusal with its diagnostics; `CheckSafeToInteract` remains and gives the reason only. `ICommandHandler` has a new virtual function `GetSensitiveValueSinkPaths()` whose default returns an empty list, so existing handlers are not affected.
- **⚠️ `CreateAsset` refuses an occupied path, discards what it could not finish, and drops the generic Blueprint factory from specific Blueprint kinds** (`UAIPEditorAssets` module; applies to every asset class). An asset already at `AssetPath` (saved or not) is `InvalidParams` with the existing class as `Result.ExistingClassName` when known; nothing is overwritten and no overwrite dialog appears, so to recreate an asset delete it first. When the created asset is of a different class than requested, or post-processing fails, the asset is deleted before the error is returned; if it cannot be deleted, `Result.RemainingAssetPath` names where it remains. The generic Blueprint factory no longer appears among the candidates (and in `ListFactoriesForClass`) for specific Blueprint kinds, so more kinds can be created without `FactoryClassName`; `/Script/Engine.Blueprint` itself still uses it. Conversely, `ClassName=/Script/Engine.Blueprint` with a `FactoryParams.ParentClass` that the engine maps to a dedicated Blueprint kind (for example an Animation Instance subclass) is now refused with `InvalidParams` before anything is created, naming the class to use instead in the message and as `Result.RequiredClassName`; it used to create that kind without the checks that kind has. A parent that needs no dedicated kind, and an omitted `ParentClass`, are created as before. See [Commands Reference](commands.md#uaipeditorassets).

- **⚠️ Breaking change — a generic property write to a Mass asset now needs `MassAssetEdit` and `MassProjectCodeExecution`** (`UAIPEditorMass` module). Writing a property of a Mass entity config, a spawner, a trait, a spawn data generator, or one of the structs that carry their settings, through a generic property command used to need only the generic capability (`PropertyEdit`, plus `PropertyReferenceEdit` / `PropertyStructuredEdit` for references and nested objects). It now also requires `MassAssetEdit` and `MassProjectCodeExecution`, always. The structs covered are those the Mass plugins (MassGameplay, MassAI, MassCrowd) declare and that can be reached from the properties of a config, a spawner, a trait or a generator; a struct used only by Mass project settings (movement styles, crowd lane density, look-at priorities and the like) or only by StateTree nodes is not covered, while a struct used both by a trait and by another asset (a data table row, for example) is covered whichever way it is written. A write that reaches a struct the project declares itself is not caught. A procedure that wrote such a property with `PropertyEdit` alone must add the two capabilities. See [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#mass-observation-and-editing).

- **⚠️ Breaking change — `SetTimeScale`, `TeleportActor`, `PossessActor` and `ExecuteConsoleCommand` (`UAIP.Runtime.World`) no longer act on the level open in the editor.** These four resolved their target world without filtering by world type, so with no play session running they picked up the first world they found — the level being edited — and really did apply the change there. They are now refused outside a play session and change nothing. The refusal also changed shape, from `ExecutionFailed` with `No active game world found.` to **`NotAllowed` with `PIE is not running.`** These four are commands addressed to a running game; use the matching `UAIP.Editor.*` commands to change the level being edited. Callers pattern-matching on the old error code or the old message text must be updated.
- **`TimeoutSec` on `RunEditorPythonScript` is now described accurately**: the parameter has never interrupted a script — it is validated but its value is not used. The schema description now says it is accepted for compatibility and has no effect, and points to `AbortAfterSeconds` for cutting a long-running script short. Behavior is unchanged.
- **⚠️ Breaking change — `RunGauntletTest` (`UAIP.Runtime.Execution`) now validates `TestName` and `TestParams`, and some inputs that used to pass are refused or need a permission.** Callers that used the old forms must be updated.
  - **Now refused with `InvalidParams`** (the process is not started, and `GauntletUnrestrictedArguments` does not lift these): non-alphanumeric characters in `TestName` and in values; in `TestName`, `/` and `:`, symbols outside parentheses, names that start with a digit, and nested parentheses; in keys, a leading `-` and characters such as `.` (a key is a letter followed by letters, digits and `_`); in values, spaces, quotes and symbols such as `,` `=` `(` `)` `;` `@`; and a value that is not a JSON string (a number, `true` / `false` or `null` — write `"600"`, not `600`).
  - **Now requires `GauntletUnrestrictedArguments`** (otherwise `CapabilityNotAvailable`; these used to pass with the default permission): the parenthesized per-test parameter syntax; dangerous keys; keys that start with the test name; values that are absolute paths, UNC paths or URLs; values that start with `-` and a letter.
  - **Project or engine paths that contain `&` `%` `!` `^` and similar symbols** now fail with `ExecutionFailed` and the process is not started. The launch script cannot handle them safely, so no permission lifts this (the launch most likely did not work correctly in such a location before).
  - **`TestParams` now reach Gauntlet as `-Key=Value`.** Before, they may not have arrived as intended.
- **`RunGauntletTest` keeps parameters out of logs and artifacts, and its launch failures are told apart**: a parenthesized `TestName` appears as `(<redacted>)` in logs, error text and artifacts, and the launch failure messages now differ by cause (a path that holds an unusable symbol, another Gauntlet test already running, a launch script or shell that cannot be found, a process that failed to start).
- **⚠️ Breaking change — 13 commands now refuse to run when the safety policy sets `DisableSave`** (`UAIPEditorAssets`, `UAIPEditorEnhancedInput`, `UAIPEditorWorldPartition` and `UAIPEditorGameFeatures` modules): `DeleteAsset`, `DeleteFolder`, `ForceDeleteFolder`, `RenameAsset`, `MoveAsset`, `MoveFolder`, `FixAssetRedirectors`, `FixUpRedirectorsInFolder`, `DeleteInputAction`, `DeleteMappingContext`, `DeleteDataLayerAsset`, `DeleteHLODs` and `DeleteGameFeaturePlugin`. Deleting, moving or renaming removes or rewrites files on disk, but these commands used to carry on regardless of `DisableSave`. They now answer `PolicyViolation` ("Disk write operations are disabled by the current safety policy.") and change nothing, **before** the PIE check and the editor-state checks, so a call made during PIE also answers `PolicyViolation` instead of `NotAllowed`. Callers that relied on these commands working under `DisableSave=True` must stop doing so. Commands that already refused under `DisableSave` are unchanged. See [Safety](safety.md#disablesave-and-the-commands-that-delete-move-or-rename).
- **`RestartEditor`: `Success` now means the request was accepted, and the outcome is reported** (`UAIPEditorWorkspace` module). The response carries `AttemptId`, `State`, `RestartScheduled`, `CloseDeadlineSeconds` and `ProcessId`. A restart that cannot happen right now is refused at once with a reason and a remedy — `PreconditionFailed` during a lighting build, an Interchange import, a main window recreation or (in an editor a person operates) an open modal dialog; `NotAllowed` when the editor is already exiting; `Conflict` when another attempt is in progress. A restart declined after acceptance is reported through `GetRestartStatus`, and the editor puts back the settings it changed and the unsaved changes it was about to discard (before, a declined restart left the "discard unsaved changes silently" setting in place). A `SaveAll` that fails to save some packages now answers `ExecutionFailed` and does not restart (before, it answered `Success`). Callers should read the outcome from `GetRestartStatus` rather than assume the editor restarted. See [Commands](commands.md#uaipeditorworkspace).
- **`ShutdownEditor`, and `QuitGame` when it asks the editor itself to exit, cancel a `RestartEditor` attempt that is still in progress**, so the exit is not turned into a restart. A `ShutdownEditor` refused by the safety policy cancels nothing.
- **`DeleteGameFeaturePlugin` only deletes GameFeatures inside the project's `Plugins/GameFeatures/` folder**, and refuses (`NotAllowed`) anything else — a plugin outside that folder, the folder itself, a plugin with code modules or nested plugins, or one containing a link such as a junction. It unloads the plugin's assets before deleting, and can delete a GameFeature that was never registered.
- **⚠️ A test that skips is now reported as `Skipped`, not `Pass`** (`RunAutomationTest`, `RunAutomationSpec` and `RunRuntimeAutomationTest`; `UAIPExecutionShared`, `UAIPEditorExecution` and `UAIPRuntimeExecution` modules): a test that succeeded and wrote a skip line (`TEST-SKIP[<Reason>]: <Detail>`) used to count as `Pass`. It now has `Result: "Skipped"`, so `Summary.Pass` is lower and `Summary.Skip` counts it. Callers that read a skipped test as a pass — for example by comparing `Summary.Pass` with the number of tests — must be updated. A test that recorded an error stays `Fail`, even if one of its messages looks like a skip line. See [Commands](commands.md#automation-test-report-contract).
- **Warnings no longer fail a test and are reported in their own list**: a warning a test emitted used to be mixed into its failure messages. It now goes to `WarningMessages` and does not change `Result`, and skip lines go to `SkipMessages`. Each of `FailMessages`, `SkipMessages` and `WarningMessages` is written only when non-empty, and the log artifact lists `FAIL:`, `SKIP:`, `WARN:` and `LOG:` entries in that order. See [Commands](commands.md#automation-test-report-contract).
- **CLI refusals now report an `ErrorCode` that matches the cause** (`UAIPTransportCLI` module): a refusal used to be reported as `InternalError` whatever the reason. It is now `InvalidParams` for a bad request (more than one request source, a path outside the allowed directories or with a disallowed extension, an unreadable or oversized file, malformed JSON, an invalid `Command` / `SessionId` / `Params`), `NotFound` for a `SessionId` that does not exist, `PolicyViolation` when the role gate blocks the transport, and `Timeout` when a one-shot command does not finish within 120 seconds. `InternalError` remains for a scenario module that is not available and for a response file that cannot be written. The process exit codes are unchanged (`0` on success, `1` when the command ran and failed, `2` for a refusal or for a response file that could not be written). Callers that matched `InternalError` on a CLI refusal must be updated. See [API](api.md#33-cli-response).
- **The HTTP 404 for a session or an artifact that does not exist now carries `ErrorCode: NotFound`** (`UAIPTransportHTTP` module): `DELETE /uaip/sessions/{sessionId}`, `GET /uaip/artifacts/{artifactId}` and `GET /uaip/sessions/{sessionId}/artifacts` used to answer `CommandNotFound`, which names a command that is not registered. The HTTP status (404) and the message text are unchanged. Callers that matched `CommandNotFound` on these responses must be updated. WebSocket is unchanged.
- **`QuitGame` (`UAIP.Runtime.World`) now answers `ExecutionFailed` in an editor where no game is running**: it used to answer `Success: true` without doing anything. The editor stays open. `QuitGame` quits the running game, not the editor, so use `ShutdownEditor` (`UAIP.Editor.Workspace.ShutdownEditor`) to close the editor. During PIE it still stops the PIE session. Callers that relied on `Success: true` from an editor with no game running must be updated. See [Commands](commands.md#uaipruntimeworld).

- **`DumpActorState` and `DumpComponentState` (`UAIP.Runtime.Observation`) now answer `NotFound` when the actor or component cannot be found**, where they answered `ExecutionFailed`. The message is unchanged. A scenario retries a step by itself only after `ExecutionFailed` or `PreconditionFailed`, so **a step with `RetryCount` that relied on the retry to wait for an actor to appear is no longer retried** and fails at once. Put a wait command in front of it instead.

- **⚠️ Breaking change for extension authors — `ICommandHandler::GetSensitiveResponseDataKeys()` is renamed `GetSensitiveResponseDataPaths()`** (`UAIPCore` module), because it now returns positions (paths) rather than field names. The field of `FCommandDescriptor` is renamed from `SensitiveResponseDataKeys` to `SensitiveResponseDataPaths` as well. The `DescribeCommand` key `SensitiveResponseData` is unchanged. To migrate, rename the override and keep the values: a top-level field name is a valid path as it is. ⚠️ **A function still named `GetSensitiveResponseDataKeys()` that is not marked `override` can compile without an error and is then never called, so the fields it declared are no longer masked.** Mark the function `override` so that the compiler reports the old name. Commands that declare no sensitive response values need no change. See [Architecture — Extension points](architecture.md#9-extension-points).

- **⚠️ The message of a missing required parameter is now the same form in every command, and it tells the caller how to check the schema** (all modules): a missing required parameter used to be reported in a different wording by almost every command, and most of them did not say where to look. It is now `Missing required parameter: '<Key>'. Check the ParameterSchema returned by UAIP.Core.DescribeCommand.` (the wording for a missing-or-invalid value, `Parameter '<Key>' is missing or is not <a type>. ...`, is unified the same way), and the refusals found by the shared schema check name the command: `... Call UAIP.Core.DescribeCommand with Name '<command name>' and check its ParameterSchema.` A required parameter missing inside a nested object now also shows its position, written as in a type mismatch (`'/Parent/Key'`). The `ErrorCode` stays `InvalidParams` and the conditions for a refusal are unchanged, so a client that branches on `ErrorCode` is not affected. **A client that compares `ErrorMessage` as a string (for example, one that matches the old wording of a missing parameter) is affected and needs to be updated**; branch on `ErrorCode` instead. The schema is read with `uaip_describe_command(CommandName=...)` over MCP, and with `UAIP.Core.DescribeCommand` (`Params.Name`) over HTTP / WebSocket / CLI.
- **⚠️ Breaking change — the editor capture commands refuse a target that is not drawn, and the error code of the refusal changed** (`CaptureActiveWindowImage`, `CaptureEditorTabImage`, `CaptureGraphViewportImage`, `CaptureViewportImageAnnotated`; `UAIPEditorObservation` module). A minimized window used to be reported as `ExecutionFailed` with a wrong cause ("no rendering device"). The commands now check that the target will be drawn before they capture, and refuse with a `Reason` and a message that says what to do: `NoWindow` and `NotRenderable` answer `PreconditionFailed`; `Headless`, `DebuggerPaused`, `NotInVisibleTree`, `BlockedByModal`, `Minimized` and `Hidden` answer `NotAllowed`.
