See [Capture refusals](commands.md#capture-refusals). **A scenario's `RetryCount` does not retry `NotAllowed`** (it retries only `ExecutionFailed` and `PreconditionFailed`), so a step that used to be retried while the window was minimized now fails at once; restore the window first (`SetEditorWindowState`, or ask the user). A capture that was allowed but failed afterwards is still `ExecutionFailed`, now without a `Reason` and with a message that says which step failed.
- **`CaptureViewportImageAnnotated` no longer returns a stale frame**: with its window minimized it used to answer success with the last image it had drawn. It now refuses like the other capture commands.
- **⚠️ Breaking change — `CaptureViewportImage` and `CheckpointCapture` refuse a game viewport that is not drawn** (`UAIPRuntimeObservation` module). A window that is minimized, hidden or behind a modal window used to end in `ExecutionFailed`. `CaptureViewportImage` now checks that the viewport will be drawn before it captures, and refuses with a `Reason` (in `Result.Reason`) and a message that says what to do, using the same vocabulary and error codes as the editor capture commands (`NoWindow` and `NotRenderable` answer `PreconditionFailed`; the others answer `NotAllowed`). `CheckpointCapture` passes the refusal through: it answers the same `NotAllowed` or `PreconditionFailed`, copies the `Reason`, and its message is `CheckpointCapture: CaptureViewportImage failed: <the message of CaptureViewportImage>`. **A scenario's `RetryCount` does not retry `NotAllowed`**, so a step that used to be retried while the window was minimized now fails at once; restore the window first. A capture that was allowed but failed afterwards is still `ExecutionFailed`, with no `Reason`. See [Capture refusals](commands.md#capture-refusals).
- **The game viewport UI operations are refused by the state of the host window** (`ClickGameViewportWidget`, `PressGameViewportUIKey` and `TypeGameViewportText`; `UAIPRuntimeInput` module). The three commands are new in this release (see Added), so no existing call changes. Before sending anything they check the window that hosts the game and answer with `Reason: HostWindowUnavailable`: a window that does not exist yet is `PreconditionFailed`, a hidden or minimized window is `NotAllowed`, with a message for each state. A minimized window is `NotAllowed`, so a scenario's `RetryCount` does not retry it. See [Capture refusals](commands.md#capture-refusals).
- **A child window is refused when its parent is not drawn**: the engine does not draw a child window while its parent is minimized or hidden, so a capture of a floating window is refused with the parent's state (`Minimized` or `Hidden`) even if the child itself is not minimized. While a modal window is open, only the modal window, its children, tooltips and notifications can be captured.
- **The editor capture commands no longer leave the renderer holding a pending screenshot**: capturing a target that is not drawn could leave a screenshot request pointing at memory the caller had already released, and the editor could crash after the target was shown again. The commands refuse such a target before asking for the screenshot, and a refusal clears any request that was left behind.
- **Demo build: capabilities and Toolset errors read the same way as in Pro** (`UAIPCore` module). The demo now registers the capabilities of the commands it leaves out, marked `NotInThisEdition` (see Added), including those of AI behavior-tree project code and Chaos destruction force notify, which it used to leave unregistered. A Toolset bridge command that cannot run now starts its error with the integration and its state (`This command runs through the Toolset integration 'UAIPCoreToolsetRegistry', which is not loaded (state: NotInThisEdition). ...`), where the message used to give only the remedy.
- **A folder parameter accepts the content root itself** (`SearchAssets`, `ListAssetRedirectors`, `GetAssetSizeMap`, `GetAssetSizeMapByClass`, `StartAssetAudit`, `RunAssetAudit`, `FindUnreferencedAssets`, `FindCircularReferences`, `FindBrokenReferences` and `StartValidationJob`; `UAIPEditorAssets` and `UAIPEditorValidation` modules). `/Game` is accepted as it is `/Game/` or a folder beneath it. A trailing `/` is dropped, so `ListAssetRedirectors` now echoes `FolderPath` without it. A refused path reports the parameter and the value (`'PackagePath' value '…': <reason>`); the error code is still `InvalidParams`.
- **`GetSelectedAssets`, `SelectAssets`, `GetContentBrowserPath` and `SetContentBrowserPath` report that they are unavailable when the Content Browser is not loaded** (`UAIPEditorAssets` module): `ListCommands` shows `Available: false` (`UnavailableDetail: ExecutionEnvironment`) instead of listing them as available and refusing the call.
- **The descriptions of `ListActorComponents` and `GetActorComponentProperty` say that `ComponentId` is opaque and already escaped**: pass it unchanged to the other component commands and never build or edit it by hand. The description of `ListIntegrations` says that `MissingPlugins` lists the plugins that enabling would fix, and that it is empty for `NotInThisEdition`. The description of `OpenPasswordTestWindow` says it only opens a window and changes nothing, and several descriptions no longer name commands that the demo does not include.
- **⚠️ Breaking change — `DumpInputState` (`UAIP.Runtime.Input`) changed in several ways a strict client notices.**
  - **`Result` is now an object** (`PlayerIndex`, `InferredInputMode` and the gate's `Decision` and `Source`) where it was `null`.
  - **A `PlayerIndex` no player controller has is now `NotFound`**, listing the indices that exist, where it was `ExecutionFailed`. A scenario retries a step by itself only after `ExecutionFailed` or `PreconditionFailed`, so a step that relied on that retry now fails at once.
  - **Arguments are checked strictly and before the play world is looked up**: a negative `PlayerIndex` or an `IncludeActionStates` that is not a boolean is `InvalidParams`, even when no play session is running.
  - **`InferredInputMode` can differ for a project game viewport client.** The command no longer runs project code: it reads the engine's implementation of `IgnoreInput`, `GetMouseCaptureMode` and `HideCursorDuringCapture`, so a project viewport client class that overrides them may now be reported differently. `GameInputGate` says so, and `EvaluateInputGateWithProjectCode` gives the overridden answer.
  - **`ActiveMappingContexts` is read from the data Enhanced Input evaluates**, not through the subsystem's overridable functions, so a project that overrides those functions now sees the contexts input processing really applies. The order is unchanged (`Priority` descending, then `Path` ascending). When that data cannot be read on the running engine version, `EnhancedInputState` is `"Unavailable"` and `EnhancedInputUnavailableReason` says why, instead of an empty list.

  See [Commands — DumpInputState output](commands.md#dumpinputstate-output).
- **⚠️ The refusal of the input injection commands names the judge that blocked** (`InjectInputKey`, `InjectEnhancedInputAction`, `InjectLegacyAction`, `InjectLegacyAxisInput`, `InjectLegacySpeechInput`; `UAIPRuntimeInput` module). The `NotAllowed` message now says which judge blocked the input — `ViewportInference`, or an installed contributor such as `CommonUIRouter` — and why. The error code is unchanged; a client that compares the message text must be updated. The `PolicyViolation` for `BypassInputMode=true` without `AllowInputModeBypass` is unchanged. With the CommonUI integration installed, injected game input is also blocked while CommonUI's action router holds the game in menu mode, as a person's input is. The audit record of an injection carries `GateSource`, `GateContributorReason` and `GateViewportReadStatus`. See [Commands — The injection gate](commands.md#the-injection-gate).

**Fixed**

- **A refused property write no longer marks the package as unsaved** (`UAIPEditorProperty` / `UAIPEditorLevel` modules): the generic `Set*` commands and `SetActorComponentProperty` used to leave a saved asset, actor or Blueprint marked as modified whenever a write was refused — by a missing capability, a property that does not exist, or any other reason — although the value itself did not change. The package now keeps the unsaved flag it had before the call.
- **One element of a fixed-size array can be read and written, and a whole write checks every element** (`UAIPEditorShared` module): a path such as `Values[1]` used to be refused; it now reads or writes that element alone. Writing a whole fixed-size array of bone references or gameplay tags used to validate only element 0, so an invalid value in a later element was written; every element is now validated, and nothing is written if one is refused.
- **⚠️ Numeric ini values and command-line flags are now read strictly** (all `ReadInt32` / `ReadFloat` settings): these were parsed leniently, so `10x` was read as `10`, `abc` and an empty value as `0`, `1.5` for an integer key as `1` and `010` as `10`. Such a value is now **ignored with a warning and the key keeps its default**, exactly like an out-of-range value. Accepted forms are an optionally signed decimal integer (`10`, `-3`, `+5`, and `0`), and for float keys also `1.0` and `1e3`; `0x10`, `010`, `00`, `nan`, `inf` and a value with spaces inside are rejected, while leading and trailing spaces are tolerated. **If a key that used to work stops taking effect after updating, look for a warning in the Output Log** — this matters most for keys whose lower bound is `0` (`[UAIP.Transport] AutoStartPort`, `[UAIP.PendingInteraction] ReacquireCooldownSeconds`), where a malformed value used to silently mean `0`. Keys affected: `[UAIP.ArtifactGC]` (`MaxAgeHours`, `MaxSessionCount`, `CleanupIntervalSeconds`, `MaxRebuildScanFiles`), `[UAIP.PendingInteraction]` (`AwaitingDeadlineSeconds`, `ResultRetentionSeconds`, `ReacquireCooldownSeconds`), `[UAIP.Session]` (`MaxCommandLogEntries`, `SessionLifetimeHours`, `MaxAllowedLifetimeHours`, `MaxScanFiles`), `[UAIP.Jobs]` (`AuditStepBudgetMs`, `ValidationStepBudgetMs`), `[UAIP.CommandNotification]` (`DurationSeconds`, `MaxConcurrentNotifications`, `ThrottleWindowSeconds`) and `[UAIP.Transport]` (`MaxConcurrentPassiveWaits`, `AutoStartPort`, `AutoStartPortScanCount`). The out-of-range behavior is unchanged; the documentation that said such a value was "clamped" was wrong and now says it is ignored.
- **⚠️ `[UAIP.PythonExtension] CommandsDir` now takes effect when written in `Config/DefaultUAIP.ini`**: the value was read from `DefaultGame.ini`, so the setting documented under `DefaultUAIP.ini` was never applied and the default directory was always scanned. It is now read from `DefaultUAIP.ini` first, with `DefaultGame.ini` as a fallback that logs a warning asking you to move the value, and a warning when the two disagree. **If you had set `CommandsDir` in `DefaultUAIP.ini` and it appeared to do nothing, the scanner will now start looking in that directory** — which can register Python commands from a directory that was never scanned before.
- **`RunFunctionalTest` now accepts names that contain `..`, and its description is corrected**: the parameter is the actor's display name, not an asset path as the description said. Control characters and `\` `?` `#` are still refused.
- **Cookbook recipes corrected**: recipes that failed when run as written were fixed.
- **`CreateGameFeaturePlugin` now creates a plugin that the engine treats as a real GameFeature** (`UAIPEditorGameFeatures` module). Before, the plugin was created directly under `Plugins/`, was not registered as a GameFeature, and the path in the response did not match where it was created. It is now created under the project's `Plugins/GameFeatures/` (an `OutputPath` outside that folder is refused with `InvalidParams`), gets a GameFeature data asset, is registered in the same session and taken to `InitialState` (default `Active`), and is registered again after a restart. The response reports the real `PluginPath`, the `PluginUrl`, the reached `State` and `Warnings` (for example, a missing asset-manager setting that could leave the data out of a packaged build). If a step fails partway, the failure answer reports how far it got. **⚠️ Plugins an older version created directly under `Plugins/` are not GameFeatures: `DeleteGameFeaturePlugin` refuses them, so delete those folders by hand.**
- **`RestartEditor` no longer reports success and then silently fails to restart.** The cases that used to end that way are now either refused up front or reported as a declined attempt with a reason; the MCP Bridge waits for the outcome (see the MCP Bridge entry below).
- **The CLI now checks the `-uaip-response-file=` path before it runs anything** (`UAIPTransportCLI` module): a path outside `Saved/UAIP/Responses/` was only refused after the command (or scenario) had already run, so the editor could be changed by a request that was then reported as refused. It is now refused first, with `InvalidParams` on stdout and exit code 2, and nothing is run. A response file whose path is allowed but that cannot be written is still a failure after the command ran: `InternalError` on stdout and exit code 2, even if the command succeeded.
- **Packaging the plugin with BuildPlugin no longer floods the log with C# type-conflict warnings (CS0436)**: building the plugin's build rules used to print a large number of these warnings; they are gone.
- **`ListIntegrations` now lists in `MissingPlugins` only the plugins that were actually found not enabled at build time, and a build left out because of the engine version no longer names any plugin.** For a `CompiledOut` integration, `MissingPlugins` used to come from a hand-written list that could name plugins the integration does not depend on. It now contains exactly the plugins that were not enabled when the plugin was built. When an integration is left out only because the engine version is not supported, `MissingPlugins` is empty, and the `CommandNotFound` hint tells you the engine version the integration requires instead of asking you to enable a plugin. No response keys or `State` values were added.
- **The completion notification for a command that cannot be resolved now masks the response `Data` too** (`UAIPCore` module): when the command a request names cannot be found (an unknown name, or a command unregistered while it ran), the notification masked every parameter but passed the response `Data` through. It now masks every top-level value of `Data` as well, keeping the field names. This also covers the responses UAIP itself makes for such a request (`CommandNotFound`, or a timeout of a command that never ran). The response returned to the caller is unchanged.
- **The startup log reports an optional integration that could not be loaded once** (`UAIPCore` module): the report was repeated for the same integration.
- **The warning for an unknown capability name in `Config/DefaultUAIP.ini` no longer fires for a capability that registers late** (`UAIPCore` module): the check now runs after the optional integrations have finished loading, so a correct name that a later-loaded module registers is no longer reported as "not registered in any module; check for typos". A name that exists nowhere is still reported.
- **The demo zip no longer contains paths with `\`** (`Tools/DemoPipeline`): a zip made with PowerShell's `Compress-Archive` stored the entries with `\` separators, which extraction tools that do not treat `\` as a separator turn into file names instead of folders. The zip is now made with the Windows `tar.exe`, and a check that no entry uses `\` and that the zip is not empty runs after it.
- **A capture of a widget inside a tab that is behind another tab now says so, and `CaptureEditorTabImage` brings such a tab to the front** (`UAIPRuntimeObservation` / `UAIPEditorObservation` modules): opening a Blueprint editor sends the level editor behind it, and a viewport inside the level editor tab used to be refused with `NoWindow` and a message telling you to wait, which never helped. A new [`Reason`](commands.md#capture-refusals), `InBackgroundTab` (`NotAllowed`), names the case, and the message tells you to bring the tab to the front with `OpenTabById` (needs `EditorTabSpawn`) or to ask the user; waiting does not change it. The `NoWindow` message carries the same advice for the cases the check cannot detect. `CaptureEditorTabImage` now brings a tab that is behind to the front together with the editor tab that holds it, then waits up to 2 seconds for it to be drawn before it captures (the tab flashes; the editor's OS window is not raised above other applications). A tab that is already in front is captured immediately, and the other capture commands do not move tabs.
- **`SetSlotProperties` now compiles the Widget Blueprint, so the change reaches widgets created at runtime** (`UAIPEditorUMG` module): the slot was edited in the asset but not compiled, while the response said no follow-up compile was needed (`bRequiresFollowupCompile: false`), so a widget created in PIE kept the old layout. The new optional `bSkipCompile` parameter only marks the Blueprint modified and then reports `bRequiresFollowupCompile: true`, for a batch of edits followed by one `CompileWidgetBlueprint`.
- **`PressGameViewportUIKey` says why a key did not reach the game** (`UAIPRuntimeInput` module): with `ReachedGameViewport: false` the response now carries `ReachedGameViewportReason: HandledBeforeGameViewport` — the focused widget, or one that contains it, handled the key, so the game's own input never saw it. The command succeeds either way, and a success alone used to read as a key the game acted on.
- **⚠️ 32 `Toolset.Editor.SequencerControlRig.*` bridges now send the arguments the delegated toolset declares** (`UAIPEditorSequencer` module, UE 5.8): they were checked one by one against the AnimationAssistantToolset functions they delegate to. The toolset quietly ignores an argument it does not know and uses its default for an argument of the wrong shape, so most of these mismatches only showed up when a required argument was missing.
  - **A required `Frame` was missing, so the call always failed** (21 commands): the 8 typed `Get...Value` and 8 typed `Set...Value` commands, `GetWorldTransform`, `SetWorldTransform`, `SetControlRigSpace`, `GetControlRigTransformInSequencer` and `SetControlRigTransformInSequencer`. They now take a required `Frame` (an integer, the frame number in the sequence's display rate).
  - **The transform was sent nested, so it would have been written as zero**: `SetEulerTransformValue`, `SetWorldTransform` and `SetControlRigTransformInSequencer` now send each location and rotation component as its own argument. ⚠️ `SetWorldTransform` and `SetControlRigTransformInSequencer` no longer accept `Scale`, because the toolset has no scale argument for them and always writes a unit scale.
  - **Argument names**: `SetPriorityOrder` now sends the priority as `order`. `DeleteControlRigAnimLayer` and `DuplicateControlRigAnimLayer` now send the layer index as `index`. Neither had reached the toolset before.
  - **Lists of controls and layers**: ⚠️ `BakeControlRigSpace` and `SnapControlRig` take the controls as `ControlNames` (a non-empty array of strings) instead of `ControlName`. ⚠️ `SnapControlRig` no longer accepts `BakeMode` or `SnapSettings`, which the toolset does not have. Instead it takes the optional booleans `KeepOffset`, `SnapPosition`, `SnapRotation` and `SnapScale`. ⚠️ `MergeControlRigAnimLayers` takes a required `Indices` (a non-empty array of layer indices); it used to send no indices at all.
  - **`ImportFBXToRig`** without `SelectedControls` sends an empty list, which the toolset reads as every control. The toolset requires the argument, so the call used to fail.
  - **`ZeroControlRigTransforms`**: ⚠️ `IncludeChannels` is now a boolean (default `true`), as the toolset declares it. `SelectionOnly` is sent only when given, so its default is now the toolset's `true`. `AssetPath` and `ControlRigPath` are optional and not used, because the toolset acts on the rigs in the active Sequencer.
  - **The anim layer commands** (`GetControlRigAnimLayers`, `AddControlRigLayerFromSelection`, `DeleteControlRigAnimLayer`, `DuplicateControlRigAnimLayer`, `ReorderControlRigAnimLayers`, `MergeControlRigAnimLayers`) act on the LevelSequence open in the active Sequencer. `AssetPath` is now optional and not used.
- **`ForceDeleteFolder` reports its outcome in `Result`** (`UAIPEditorAssets` module): a folder that is missing, a folder over the 50-item limit and a partial deletion all answer `Success`, and only the artifact said which it was. `Result` now carries the same `Status` (`Deleted`, `PartiallyDeleted`, `NotFound` or `TooManyAssets`). Once deletion has run, it also carries `DeletedAssetCount` and `FailedAssetCount`. The error code and the artifact are unchanged.
- **`AddVariableNode` (Control Rig) finds the rig's member variables** (`UAIPEditorControlRig` module): it looked only at the graph's local variables, so a node for a variable added with `AddVariable` failed with "Internal operation failed.". It now looks at local variables, then member variables. A name that is neither is `NotFound` ("Variable not found.").
- **`AddVariable` (Control Rig, legacy format) refuses a type path that names no type** (`UAIPEditorControlRig` module): an object path such as `/Script/Nowhere.NoSuchType` used to create a variable with no type. It is now `InvalidParams`, and no variable is added. On UE 5.8, the message for a type the engine cannot hold now says to name the type by its object path, for example `/Script/CoreUObject.Vector`.
- **Toolset bridge commands whose Toolset returns a boolean or a number now succeed** (`UAIPCore` module): such a command always answered `ExecutionFailed` with "Failed to parse Toolset response as JSON.", even though the Toolset call had done its work — for example, `Toolset.Editor.SequencerControlRig.SetFloatValue` wrote the value and still reported a failure. The value now arrives under `Result.Result` (for example `true` or `0.37`). This applies to every `Toolset.*` bridge; a result that is an object or an array is unchanged.
- **Closing a sequence after its Control Rig was recompiled no longer crashes the editor** (`UAIPEditorSeqControlRig` module): recompiling a legacy-format Control Rig Blueprint replaces the rig used by a Sequencer Control Rig track with a new instance that the engine leaves without its binding to the mesh. Closing that sequence then crashed the editor, whether the recompile came from a UAIP command that edits the rig (for example `AddVariable`) or from the editor's Compile button. UAIP now hands the binding over to the new instance. Rigs in the runtime-asset format were never affected. The new [`[UAIP.Sequencer]` `CarryControlRigBindingOnRecompile`](config.md#uaipsequencer--keeping-a-sequencer-control-rig-bound-across-a-recompile) setting (default `True`) turns it off.

#### MCP Bridge 1.3.0 — Unreleased

The bridge is versioned and released independently of the plugin; see the version numbering above.

**Added**

- **Repair mode: running the installer that is already deployed inside the bridge now rebuilds only what is broken.** When the installer finds that it is running from the deploy target itself, it prints "Repair mode: ...", keeps the deployed files, and rebuilds only `.venv/`, `config.json` (existing settings are kept) and the registration snippet. A repair is not an update: to move to a newer release, extract the new zip outside the deploy target and run its installer.
- **`-UAIPPluginDir` (`install.ps1` / `install.cmd`) and `--uaip-plugin-dir` (`install.sh`) name the folder that directly contains `UnrealAIIntegrationPlatform.uplugin`.** Use them when the plugin lives outside `Plugins` (for example under an `AdditionalPluginDirectories` entry) or when more than one copy is found. The bridge is deployed next to that folder.
- **The installer now refuses an unsafe deployment with a reason and an exit code (13 to 19), and deletes nothing when it does.** Each refusal is listed in [Troubleshooting](troubleshooting.md).
- **Extra editor command-line arguments: the `UAIP_EDITOR_EXTRA_ARGS` environment variable and `uaip_reload_config(EditorExtraArgs=[...])`.** The bridge appends them when it launches the editor — for example `-stomp2malloc` to hunt a memory corruption. They apply from the next launch, never to an editor already running, and stay in effect (crash relaunches included) until replaced; `[]` clears them. `uaip_get_editor_status` shows the list in effect as `Data.Config.EditorExtraArgs`. Flags the bridge manages itself (`-uaip-...`, `-unattended`, `-nopause`) are refused, and so are the SafetyPolicy flags (`-uaip-policy-...`), so that a client cannot lift its own restrictions; set those in `[UAIP.SafetyPolicy]` of `Config/DefaultUAIP.ini`. See [Configuration → Reloading config at runtime](config.md#reloading-config-at-runtime-uaip_reload_config).

**Fixed**

- **The installer no longer deletes its own files when it is run from inside the deploy target.** The 1.2.0 installer, run from the bridge it had deployed (for example after extracting the zip into `Plugins`), removed the very files it was running from. If you still use the 1.2.0 installer, run it from outside the deploy target.
- **The installer finds the UAIP plugin by the file `UnrealAIIntegrationPlatform.uplugin`, not by a fixed folder name.** A plugin folder with any other name used to be missed.
- **A reload that changes a setting needing a new editor no longer leaves the bridge unusable after `RestartEditor` or an MCP reconnect.** If you called `uaip_reload_config` after changing the editor path, the uproject path, the HTTP port, `enable_scenario` or `launch_attended` while the running editor was one the bridge had attached to (the editor that `RestartEditor` brings up, or one an MCP reconnect attached to), the reload reported success and then every later command was refused with `PORT_OCCUPIED`, `ShutdownEditor` included; only an MCP reconnect recovered it. The reload is now refused with nothing changed and the answer lists the steps (send `ShutdownEditor` only if an AI operation started the editor, otherwise ask its owner to close it or give up the change). An editor the bridge launched itself is still shut down by the reload, and an editor that refuses `ShutdownEditor` now makes the reload return that refusal instead of being ignored. A reload that changes the HTTP port is refused the same way instead of sometimes launching a second editor on the new port next to the old one. A reload is also refused while another tool call is running, and while a reload runs the other tool calls are turned away at once (if that goes on for about a minute and a half, the message tells you to reconnect the MCP session). Guest mode (`attach_only`) is not refused. See [Configuration → Reloading config at runtime](config.md#when-a-reload-would-have-to-stop-an-editor).
- **A reload in guest mode (`attach_only`) no longer says the editor will relaunch.** The answer was "Editor will relaunch on the next tool call." with `EditorRestartScheduled: true`, although a guest bridge never launches or stops an editor. It now says `EditorRestartScheduled: false`: if the bridge was connected, the connection is dropped without stopping the editor, the next tool call is refused once, and the one after that attaches to the editor on its port; if it was not connected, the new settings apply to the editor it attaches to next. A reload that also switches `attach_only` describes the next tool call under the new mode, and changing only `EditorExtraArgs` in guest mode says the arguments are not used.
- **Reload answers around guest mode (`attach_only`) are now more accurate.** A reload that changes only `attach_only` stops no editor and the next tool call is not refused; the answer says whether the bridge will launch the editor from now on or no longer will. The one-time refusal in guest mode now ends with "The editor was not stopped. The next call checks the editor answering on the port and connects to it again; retry as is." After leaving guest mode, if the editor answering on the port is not closed, the next tool call waits for the port to free up and is refused with "The config reload did not ask any editor to exit ... Close the editor holding the port, then retry." (it used to say the editor was asked to exit). When a guest bridge is disconnected and something answers on the port, `uaip_get_editor_status` now reports a `RecommendedAction` beginning `PROCEED:` (the next call checks it and connects; nothing is launched) instead of `CHECK CONFIGURATION:`.
- **`uaip_get_editor_status` now reports a reload that failed while applying the new configuration, as `Data.Config.LastReloadApplyFailure`.** When a reload fails partway (for example a file the bridge is made of was being edited and could not be read again), the bridge cannot tell whether it runs on the old settings, the new settings or a mix; `RecommendedAction` says to fix the cause and reconnect the MCP server. The next successful reload clears it. It is separate from `LastReloadError`, which means a `config.json` that could not be parsed and was not applied; when a later reload is refused for that reason, `RecommendedAction` carries both notes and says to fix `config.json` before reconnecting.

**Changed**

- **`uaip_execute` of `RestartEditor` now returns when the outcome is known, not when the restart is accepted.** When the editor returns an `AttemptId`, the bridge keeps sending progress to the client and polls `GetRestartStatus` while it waits, then answers once it has connected to the new editor, or the restart was declined (the editor's reason and remedy come back as the error), or the outcome could not be confirmed within the limit (until the editor closes: the editor's `CloseDeadlineSeconds` plus about 30 seconds, and up to 60 more once shutdown has begun; after it closes: `http_startup_timeout_seconds`). It verifies the identity of the new editor before reporting success. With an older editor that returns no `AttemptId`, or when the status command is unavailable or denied, the bridge behaves as before and returns at acceptance. A `RestartEditor` issued while the bridge is already waiting for a new editor is refused with an error instead of restarting the current one. The minimum plugin version is unchanged.
- **When the engine install path holds more than one UAIP plugin copy, the installer now refuses instead of picking one.** `install.sh` used to pick one without even a warning. Pass `--uaip-plugin-dir` / `-UAIPPluginDir` with the copy you want, or move the others out of `Engine/Plugins`.

#### uaip-result-view 0.1.0 — Unreleased

First public release, `0.1.0`. A Claude Code Mod that draws UAIP MCP tool calls in the transcript as a summary — which command ran, success or the error code and message, artifacts, scenario steps — instead of the first characters of the raw response JSON, with the main fields of `Result` behind a Details button (already open when the call failed). Only the drawing changes; the result the model reads is unchanged. For sessions that reach UAIP through the MCP Bridge; it does not need the Claude Code Plugin. Distributed as `UAIP-ResultView-<version>.zip` from this repository's Releases (tag `UAIPResultView-v<X.Y.Z>`), under the same marketplace `uaip-tools` as the Claude Code Plugin (plugin name `uaip-result-view`). Versioned `0.x` because the Claude Code Mod API it uses is early access. See [uaip-result-view](uaip-result-view.md).

The [Claude Code Plugin](claude-code-plugin.md)'s update steps now delete only `plugins/uaip`, so that the Mod extracted into the same folder survives an update.


### UAIP Plugin 1.2.0 — 2026-09-29

**UAIP 1.2.0 is now live on Fab.** [https://www.fab.com/listings/0eedf909-00ac-4d95-b109-8fda51800fff](https://www.fab.com/listings/0eedf909-00ac-4d95-b109-8fda51800fff)

[![Watch the update trailer on YouTube](https://markdown-videos-api.jorgenkh.no/youtube/5BqecMKK96c)](https://youtu.be/5BqecMKK96c)

A backward-compatible MINOR release. All changes below have shipped in the plugin repository (`.uplugin` `VersionName` is `1.2.0`) and passed Fab review.

**1.2.0 adds 351 commands and removes none** — 341 UAIP commands and 10 Toolset bridge commands, taking the totals from 877 / 411 (1288) in 1.1.0 to **1218 / 421 (1639)**. Every command name that existed in 1.1.0 still exists and still means the same thing. The largest additions are MetaHuman (56), GroomAsset (35), ChaosDestruction (29), LiveLink (25 across the runtime and editor modules), MotionMatching (23), Subsonic (22), **Chooser (20 — a new domain)**, UAF (19), ControlRig Dynamics (17), AnimSequence (13) and Insights Trace (11). See the [Commands Reference](commands.md) for the full per-domain breakdown.

#### UAIP Plugin

**Added**

- **LiveLink support — observation, client control, synthetic sources, presets, discovery and Take Recorder recording** (`UAIPRuntimeLiveLink` and `UAIPEditorLiveLink` modules, both **new**; **Pro only** — not available in the demo build): AI agents can now read which LiveLink Sources and Subjects exist and what is flowing through them, change which Subjects the client evaluates, bind a Subject to a placed actor, save and apply preset assets, discover and connect MessageBus providers, and record Subjects into a LevelSequence — none of which was reachable from UAIP before. Adds **23 commands** across two providers. Under `UAIP.Runtime.LiveLink` (12): read-only `ListLiveLinkSources`, `ListLiveLinkSubjects`, `GetLiveLinkSubjectFrame`, `GetLiveLinkSubjectStatus`, `ListLiveLinkRoles` (all `RuntimeInspect`); client state `SetLiveLinkSubjectEnabled`, `RemoveLiveLinkSource`, `AddLiveLinkVirtualSubject`, `RemoveLiveLinkVirtualSubject`; and synthetic sources `CreateLiveLinkSyntheticSource`, `PushLiveLinkSyntheticFrame`, `RemoveLiveLinkSyntheticSource`. Under `UAIP.Editor.LiveLink` (11): `GetLiveLinkPresetInfo`, `ApplyLiveLinkPreset`, `AddLiveLinkPresetToClient`, `SaveLiveLinkPreset`, `SetLiveLinkComponentSubject`, `DiscoverLiveLinkMessageBusProviders`, `ConnectLiveLinkMessageBusSource`, `StartLiveLinkRecording`, `StopLiveLinkRecording`, `CancelLiveLinkRecording`, `GetLiveLinkRecordingStatus`. Adds **12 DefaultDenied capabilities**: `LiveLinkClientControl`, `LiveLinkSourceDelete`, `LiveLinkSyntheticSource`, `LiveLinkFrameInjection`, `LiveLinkSourceInspectSensitive`, `LiveLinkNetworkDiscovery`, `LiveLinkSourceConnect`, `LiveLinkPresetApply`, `LiveLinkPresetAdd`, `LiveLinkPresetSave`, `LiveLinkComponentEdit`, `LiveLinkRecording`. **The synthetic Source commands are the notable part**: they register a minimal UAIP-owned Source and push frames into it, so the entire LiveLink path can be stood up and verified **in process, with no capture hardware, no LiveLink Hub and no network**. ⚠️ `UAIP.Runtime.LiveLink` has **no plugin requirement** — the client interface it uses ships in the engine's always-present `LiveLinkInterface` module — so its commands are always registered and report `Available: false` when the `LiveLink` plugin is off, rather than disappearing; `ListLiveLinkSources` answers `LiveLinkAvailable`, so one call settles whether LiveLink is usable. `UAIP.Editor.LiveLink` does require both `LiveLink` and `Takes` and is compiled out without them. ⚠️ `ApplyLiveLinkPreset` is destructive and **cannot be rolled back**: it removes every existing Source before rebuilding, and a run that does not finish leaves a partially removed configuration — the engine keeps no copy of the previous one. ⚠️ A pushed frame is only **enqueued**; the client drains the queue on its own next tick, so `IsSubjectValidImmediately` reads `false` on nearly every successful push and the Subject becomes readable one tick later. ⚠️ No frame rate is computed anywhere in this domain — `GetLiveLinkSubjectStatus` returns the raw arrival-time history the engine exposes and nothing more. Only one LiveLink configuration operation runs at a time: a preset apply or a recording refuses the mutating commands for its duration (`TooManyRequests` when waiting helps, `NotAllowed` when the other operation has to be ended first), while **reads and frame injection are never refused**. Virtual Subjects and connected Sources are real client configuration and **survive the session that created them**; only synthetic Sources and UAIP-started recordings are cleaned up automatically. Not covered: LiveLink Hub control and LiveLink Device authoring — the engine exposes no editor-side API for either.
- **Component editing for actors placed in the level** (`UAIPEditorLevel` module): the Blueprint / SCS side has had component commands since 1.0.0, but a component on an actor **already in the level** could not be listed, added, removed, re-attached, or have a property read or written at all. Adds 6 commands under `UAIP.Editor.Level` — `ListActorComponents` (read-only, `EditorInspect`, answerable during PIE), `GetActorComponentProperty` (read-only, `EditorInspect`, answerable during PIE, and the only one of the six that can read a component of any `Origin`), and `AddActorComponent` / `DeleteActorComponent` / `ReparentActorComponent` / `SetActorComponentProperty`, gated by the new DefaultDenied `ActorComponentEdit` capability and refused during PIE, where the change would be lost when play stops. Each write command is one undo step. `ListActorComponents` issues the `ComponentId` the other five take and reports each component's `Origin` (`Instance` / `SimpleConstructionScript` / `UserConstructionScript` / `Native`); only `Instance` components can be added, removed or re-attached here, and a refusal names the route that does own the others. Every write takes `ExpectedComponentClass` alongside the identifier, so an identifier gone stale is refused with `NotFound` rather than followed to whatever now bears the name. Each listing entry also reports what **adding** a component of that class would take (`Admission` / `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities`), which is how the class gate below can be inspected before a write is attempted. **`GetActorComponentProperty` is the only way to read a property that traverses the object reference an actor holds its components through** — `GetActorProperty` refuses that path — and its response includes `WriteRequirements` (what a write of that property would additionally need beyond `ActorComponentEdit`, split into what the session already holds and what is missing, and which of `SetActorComponentProperty`'s two value parameters it must arrive in). `SetActorComponentProperty` takes the same `Value` / `ValueJson` / `Operation` parameters as every other property writer, and is refused when the component's `Origin` is `UserConstructionScript`, since the next run of that script would discard the write. Renaming and duplicating an instance component are deliberately not provided — add the component again under the name you want.
- **`CreateAsset` can now produce a plain Actor Blueprint and a usable Anim Blueprint**: both used to be reachable only in name. Asking for `/Script/Engine.Blueprint` returned a Blueprint pinned to a view model base with no `SimpleConstructionScript`, which `AddBlueprintComponent` then refused; asking for `/Script/Engine.AnimBlueprint` **answered `Success` while producing an asset with no target skeleton** — one that cannot be opened and that silently renders nothing. Neither needed a new command: `CreateAsset` has an interceptor extension point, and the cause was that the view model interceptor claimed the generic `UBlueprintFactory` outright instead of only the factory it owns, while Anim Blueprints had no interceptor at all. Both now have one, each claiming its own factory class exactly. `FactoryParams.ParentClass` is optional for a Blueprint and defaults to `AActor`; `FactoryParams.TargetSkeleton` is **required** for an Anim Blueprint and its absence is refused with `InvalidParams` rather than answered with a broken asset. ⚠️ **Behaviour change**: `CreateAsset` for `/Script/Engine.Blueprint` without `FactoryParams` now yields an Actor Blueprint instead of a view model one — name `FactoryParams.ParentClass` explicitly (`/Script/ModelViewViewModel.MVVMViewModelBase`) to create a view model Blueprint.
- **Commands that produce several related artifacts in one call now also return them as a single bundle** (19 commands): a test run's report and log, the before/after snapshots of a map load or PIE start, a DataTable's pre-change snapshot and result, and Enhanced Input's audit record and result all used to sit side by side in `Artifacts[]` with **no way to tell from the response which was which** — callers had to rely on array position, and position moves for implementation reasons. These commands now return a manifest of the new `Bundle` artifact type alongside the individual artifacts. Each manifest item carries a role label (`Pre` / `Post` / `Report` / `Log` / `Audit` / `Result` / `Snapshot` / `Payload`) and a `Persisted` flag saying whether that artifact was actually stored. **The manifest goes last**, so the individual artifacts keep the indices they had before and callers that depended on position keep working. Saving the manifest is best effort: if only the manifest is lost, the individual artifacts stay on the response and the command still succeeds — you simply lose the role labels. ⚠️ **The artifact count goes up by one**, including in the editor's on-screen notification. Two commands whose outputs are only loosely related (`ExecuteConsoleCommand` and `CreateGameFeaturePlugin`) are deliberately excluded and still return individual artifacts only.

- **Asset validation, as a job or in one call** (`UAIPEditorValidation` module, **new**, requires the `DataValidation` plugin; **Pro only** — not available in the demo build): AI agents can now run the asset validators a project has registered, read what they found as structured results, and apply the fixes those validators offered. Adds 7 commands under `UAIP.Editor.Validation` — `ListValidators` (which validators are enabled, and whether material validation can run at all); `ValidateAssets` (up to 8 assets, synchronously, in a single engine call); `StartValidationJob` / `GetValidationJobStatus` / `GetValidationJobResult` / `CancelValidationJob` (a folder or a long list, validated a few assets at a time so the editor stays usable); and `ApplyValidationFix`. Adds 2 DefaultDenied capabilities: `AssetValidation` (running validators — project-supplied code the engine does not forbid from having side effects) and `AssetValidationFix` (rewriting an asset). UAIP defines no quality rules of its own; every judgement comes from `UEditorValidatorSubsystem`. Requires the `DataValidation` plugin to be enabled (the engine ships it enabled by default; UAIP compiles support for it in automatically on an editor build — see the optional-plugin loading change below). Material validation additionally needs `MaterialValidationPlatforms` configured plus an editor restart. A chunked job's result is not promised to equal validating everything in one call, and fixes only ever come from project-written validators — no validator the engine ships offers one.
- **AnimNotify editing** (`UAIPEditorAnimSequence` module, **new**; **Pro only** — not available in the demo build): AI agents can now add, remove, and edit AnimNotify / AnimNotifyState entries and notify tracks on `UAnimSequence` / `UAnimMontage` / `UAnimComposite` assets, and repair notify guids left unstable after a load. Adds 11 commands under `UAIP.Editor.AnimSequence` — read-only `GetAnimNotifyInfo`, `GetAvailableAnimNotifyClasses`, `GetAnimNotifyClassSchema` (require `EditorInspect`); track management `AddAnimNotifyTrack` / `RemoveAnimNotifyTrack`; notify placement and removal `AddAnimNotify` / `AddAnimNotifyState` / `RemoveAnimNotify`; event field edits `SetAnimNotifyEvent`; a generic per-notify-instance property writer `SetAnimNotifyProperty`; and guid repair `FixupAnimNotifyGuids`. Adds 2 DefaultDenied capabilities: `AnimNotifyEdit` (required by every edit command) and `AnimNotifyReferenceEdit` (required in addition to `AnimNotifyEdit` only when `SetAnimNotifyProperty` writes a property that is — or contains — a hard object/class reference). Built entirely on engine-shipped AnimNotify types — no optional plugin required. Every edit command is rejected while PIE or SIE is active.
- **`SynchronizePoseSearchDatabase`** (`UAIPEditorMotionMatching` module): AI agents can now explicitly merge `PoseSearchBranchIn` animation notify entries into a `UPoseSearchDatabase`'s `AnimationAssets`, since the engine has no reliable event to observe that merge happening on its own. Adds 1 command under `UAIP.Editor.MotionMatching` (requires `PoseSearchAssetEdit`). ⚠️ Calling it in the same request as the referencing animation asset's save can report zero merged entries, because the asset registry's referencer index rebuilds asynchronously after a save; re-issue the call once the save has settled.
- **MetaHuman character editing** (`UAIPEditorMetaHuman` module, **new**, requires the `MetaHumanCharacter` plugin; **Pro only** — not available in the demo build): AI agents can now author MetaHuman characters end to end — create the asset, set body / skin / eye / makeup / head model / face evaluation settings, sculpt the face, conform and fit to target meshes, manage the wardrobe, drive the preview viewport, request cloud rigging and high resolution texture synthesis, and run the asset build pipeline. Adds 56 commands under `UAIP.Editor.MetaHuman` — creation (`CreateMetaHumanCharacter`), body / skin / eyes (`GetBodyConstraints`, `SetBodyConstraints`, `GetBodyShape`, `SetBodyShape`, `GetSkinSettings`, `SetSkinTone`, `GetEyeSettings`, `SetEyeColor`), appearance detail (`SetSkinSettings`, `GetMakeupSettings`, `SetMakeupSettings`, `GetHeadModelSettings`, `SetHeadModelSettings`, `SetEyeSettings`, `GetFaceEvaluationSettings`, `SetFaceEvaluationSettings`), face sculpting (`GetFaceModelCoefficients`, `SetFaceModelCoefficients`, `GetFaceLandmarks`, `TranslateFaceLandmarks`, `CommitFaceState`, `ImportFaceFromDna`, `ImportFaceFromTemplate`, `ImportFaceFromIdentity`, `CompareFaceState`), conforming and fitting (`GetMeshDataForConforming`, `ConformBodyToTarget`, `ConformFaceToTargetMeshes`, `AlignToTargetMeshes`, `RefineVerticesToTarget`, `CommitPosedStateAsAPose`, `FitStateToTargetVertices`, `FitFaceFromBodyWithEyesTeethTemplate`, `FitFaceFromBodyWithEyesTeethDna`, `GetAsyncConformState`), the build pipeline (`RequestTextureSources`, `GetTextureSourceState`, `RequestAutoRigging`, `GetRiggingState`, `CanBuildMetaHuman`, `BuildMetaHuman`), preview (`GetViewportSettings`, `SetViewportSettings`, `RefreshCharacterPreview`), wardrobe (`ListWardrobeSlots`, `ListWardrobeItems`, `GetWardrobeItemInfo`, `AssignWardrobeItem`, `RemoveWardrobeItem`, `ReplaceWardrobeItem`, `GetWardrobeItem`, `SetWardrobeItem`, `SetWardrobeItemPipeline`, `ListItemPipelineClasses`), and session management (`ReleaseEditSession`). Adds 6 DefaultDenied capabilities: `MetaHumanAssetCreate`, `MetaHumanEdit`, `MetaHumanFileImport`, `MetaHumanTextureSynthesis`, `MetaHumanCloudRigging`, `MetaHumanBuild`. Editing commands open an edit session on demand and hold it open, so most reads in this domain are **not** read-only — they require `MetaHumanEdit` and are refused under a read-only safety policy; `GetViewportSettings` is the only exception and needs just `EditorInspect`. ⚠️ `RequestAutoRigging` **uploads the character's face data to Epic's cloud rigging service** and requires a signed-in Epic account and network access. ⚠️ `BuildMetaHuman` occupies the game thread for the entire build (seconds to minutes). The engine shows a progress dialog and keeps redrawing, so the editor stays usable to look at rather than going unresponsive, but no other command runs until the build returns — that is longer than the MCP bridge freeze detection (30 s) and the default command timeout (60 s), so raise `command_timeout_seconds` before calling it, and call `CanBuildMetaHuman` first. 14 of the 56 commands (`GetFaceModelCoefficients`, `SetFaceModelCoefficients`, `ConformFaceToTargetMeshes`, `AlignToTargetMeshes`, `RefineVerticesToTarget`, `GetAsyncConformState`, `CommitPosedStateAsAPose`, `FitFaceFromBodyWithEyesTeethTemplate`, `FitFaceFromBodyWithEyesTeethDna`, `GetMeshDataForConforming`, `AssignWardrobeItem`, `RemoveWardrobeItem`, `ReplaceWardrobeItem`, `RefreshCharacterPreview`) depend on UE 5.8 engine APIs; they are still registered on UE 5.7 but report `Available: false` and return `PolicyViolation` when called. Also adds 9 Toolset bridge commands under `Toolset.Editor.MetaHuman.*` (`BeginEdit`, `EndEdit`, `GetBodyShape`, `SetBodyShape`, `GetSkinTone`, `SetSkinTone`, `GetEyeColor`, `SetEyeColor`, `Create`), delegating to the `MetaHumanGenerator` Python toolset — these require UE 5.8+ with `MetaHumanGenerator` + `ToolsetRegistry` enabled and are not registered at all on UE 5.7.

- **Unreal Insights trace capture and analysis** (`UAIPRuntimeInsights` module, **new**): AI agents can now record an Unreal Insights trace, mark it up while it records, and analyse the capture offline. Adds 11 commands under `UAIP.Runtime.Insights.Trace` — read-only `ListTraceChannels`, `GetTraceStatus`, `ListTraceFiles` plus control `StartTrace`, `StopTrace`, `PauseTrace`, `ResumeTrace`, `SetTraceChannels`, `AddTraceBookmark`, `BeginTraceRegion`, `EndTraceRegion` — and 3 commands under `UAIP.Runtime.Insights.Analysis` (`AnalyzeTrace`, `GetTraceAnalysisStatus`, `GetTraceAnalysisResult`), which are only registered in builds where trace analysis is supported and report `CommandNotFound` elsewhere. Adds 4 capabilities: `RuntimeInsightsInspect` (DefaultAllow) and `RuntimeInsightsControl` / `RuntimeInsightsAttachTraceFile` / `RuntimeInsightsAnalyze` (all DefaultDenied). Adds a new artifact type `Trace`, and 3 SafetyPolicy keys: `AllowExternalTraceAnalysis` / `ExternalTraceDirectory`, which only take effect when **both** are set (they let `AnalyzeTrace` read a `.utrace` captured outside UAIP; symbolic links inside that directory are not resolved, so point it at a directory kept for UAIP alone), and `AllowDisclosingTraceAttachment`, which allows a captured `.utrace` to be handed over as an artifact when the channels it recorded with could have captured host paths, screen content or network addresses (log text stays governed by `AllowLogDump`, and a channel this build does not classify is refused whatever both are set to). A trace is always written to a file under `Saved/Profiling/UAIP/` — no command in the module can send one to a network destination — and the `trace.` prefix is added to the `UAIP.Runtime.World.ExecuteConsoleCommand` deny-list so that route is closed as well; note that `RunEditorPythonScript` still reaches the engine's trace system and is managed through its own capabilities. Channels are classified by what they can disclose, and `StartTrace` is rejected with `PolicyViolation` when the effective channel set (already enabled ∪ requested) records log text while `AllowLogDump` is false. Analysis is asynchronous with one run at a time; a request that arrives while another run is parsing or extracting is rejected with `TooManyRequests` naming the `AnalysisId` of the run holding the slot, so it can be watched through `GetTraceAnalysisStatus` instead of blind-polling, and each requested section is written out as its own JSON artifact. A run also belongs to the session that started it: `GetTraceAnalysisStatus` and `GetTraceAnalysisResult` find an `AnalysisId` only when they are called with the same `SessionId` as `AnalyzeTrace` was, and from any other session it reads as `NotFound` — deliberately the same answer as for an identifier that never existed, so neither command can be used to find out whether another caller's run exists. Omitting `SessionId` breaks this: the transport makes a fresh anonymous session per call, so a run started that way can never be looked up again. ⚠️ Attaching the raw `.utrace` (`StopTrace` with `AttachTraceFile: true`) always discloses the process command line whatever the channel set was, because it is written through an always-on internal channel — this is why it is gated by its own `RuntimeInsightsAttachTraceFile` capability, and why the analysis `Diagnostics` section (which reports a sanitised command line) is not equivalent to the file itself. The editor also enables the log, bookmark, region and screenshot channels by itself at startup, so in practice attaching a trace captured in the editor needs **both** `AllowLogDump=True` and `AllowDisclosingTraceAttachment=True`; `StartTrace` reports an `AttachDisabledByPolicy` warning up front when the channels it is about to record with would keep the file from being handed over. Stopping does not close the captured file on its own — the engine's trace writer finishes that on its own thread a moment later — so a `StopTrace` that asks for the file waits up to three seconds for it and reports `AttachSkippedReason: "TraceFileStillOpen"` if it is still being written by then, which is worth asking for again in a moment; a stop that did not ask for the file never waits. Demo build: only the 3 read-only trace commands are included; the analysis provider is compiled out entirely.

- **Asset audits run as a job instead of holding the game thread** (`UAIPEditorAssets` module): auditing a large content folder used to occupy the game thread until it finished, freezing the editor — and every other UAIP command — for tens of seconds to minutes, with no way for the caller to tell a working audit from a hung editor. Adds 3 read-only commands under `UAIP.Editor.Assets`: `StartAssetAudit` registers the job and returns an `AuditId` straight away, after which the scan advances a little at a time between editor frames while the editor stays usable and other commands keep being answered; `GetAssetAuditStatus` reports `State` (`Preparing` / `Running` / `Completed` / `Failed`), the report currently being scanned, processed and total counts, elapsed seconds, how many assets were skipped for disappearing mid-scan, and a failure reason; `GetAssetAuditResult` hands back one artifact per report once the job is `Completed`. All three require only `EditorInspect` — no new capability — and all three **require an explicit `SessionId`**, rejecting an anonymous one with `InvalidParams`, because a job started under a per-call anonymous session could never be polled or read afterwards. A job is visible only to the session that started it: an unknown, expired, or other-session `AuditId` all read as `NotFound`, deliberately indistinguishable. One audit runs at a time (`TooManyRequests` otherwise, naming the `AuditId` only when it is the caller's own), a start is refused with `NotAllowed` while the editor shows a modal dialog or a progress bar and paused rather than failed if one appears mid-scan, resubmission is rate limited per session and again across all sessions by how much editor time audits have consumed recently, and a job that has spent ten minutes of editor time is failed rather than left running (waiting time is not counted — consumed time is). A finished job is kept for 15 minutes, restarted on each successful read and capped at an hour from the start, and is dropped when the session ends, when Live Coding reloads, and when the editor stops — read the result before ending the session. How much of each frame a scan may take is configurable via `Config/DefaultUAIP.ini` → `[UAIP.Jobs] AuditStepBudgetMs` (default `10.0`, clamped to `[1.0, 100.0]`); see [Configuration → `[UAIP.Jobs]`](config.md#uaipjobs--per-frame-budget-for-job-style-commands). ⚠️ `FindUnreferencedAssets`, `FindCircularReferences`, `FindBrokenReferences` and `RunAssetAudit` are now `Deprecated`, with `StartAssetAudit` as their `MigrationTarget`. **They keep working exactly as they did** — same results, same progress firing — but they still hold the game thread for the whole audit, which is the reason to migrate. This also fixes a miscount in the circular-reference report: when the output limit was reached while examining the last root asset, the report said it had not been truncated. The cycles reported and their order are unchanged.

- **`AddAnimationToPoseSearchDatabase` gained an opt-in to bypass its own idempotency check** (`UAIPEditorMotionMatching` module). The command has always been idempotent for an animation already registered as a normal (non-BranchIn) entry — re-adding one reports the existing entry's `Index` instead of creating a second one — but that left no way to deliberately register the same animation more than once under different per-entry settings (a legitimate `UPoseSearchDatabase` use, and something the editor UI already allows). A new optional parameter, `bAllowDuplicate` (defaults to `false`), bypasses that check when set to `true`: the command always inserts a new entry, `InsertAt` and every settings field are applied the same way they are for a first-time add, and the response reports the new entry's `Index` with `bAlreadyPresent: false`. The default is unchanged — omitting the parameter keeps the existing idempotent behaviour exactly as before.

- **Typed ControlRig component editing — generic CRUD plus two typed domains** (`UAIPEditorControlRig` module): AI agents can now attach, inspect, and mutate ControlRig hierarchy components (`FRigBaseComponent` substructs) directly, instead of going through the generic hierarchy element commands. Adds 9 generic component commands under `UAIP.Editor.ControlRig` (`ListComponents`, `GetComponent`, `ListAddableComponentTypes`, `CanAddComponent`, `AddComponent`, `RemoveComponent`, `RenameComponent`, `ReparentComponent`, `SetComponentContent`) that work for every component type the module allowlist covers. Adds two typed domains on top of the same components: `UAIP.Editor.ControlRig.Dynamics` (16 commands — 6 typed reads, 6 typed writes, and 4 orchestration commands that build or rewire a whole solver / particle / constraint setup in one call; `Experimental`; requires the `ControlRigDynamics` plugin; UE 5.8+ only) and `UAIP.Editor.ControlRig.Physics` (8 commands — 4 typed reads and 4 typed writes for the solver / body / joint / control types; `Stable`; requires the `ControlRigPhysics` plugin, which the engine enables by default; available on UE 5.7 as well as UE 5.8). Adds 1 DefaultDenied capability, `ControlRigComponentEdit`, required by every write across the generic commands and both typed domains — deliberately one capability for all of them, since creating a component with initial content reaches the same import path as replacing an existing one's content. Every write is rejected while PIE is running; the Physics domain's writes are also rejected against a ModularRig asset. On an editor build UAIP compiles support for both plugins in automatically (see the optional-plugin loading change below); each domain still needs its plugin actually enabled for the project, and `ControlRigDynamics` additionally needs UE 5.8+.

- **Open and close arbitrary editor tabs by their Slate layout identifier** (`UAIPEditorWorkspace` module): AI agents previously had no way to open a tab that was not already open — `FocusEditorTab` only brings an already-open asset editor to the front, and driving the menu through `SelectMenuItem` reaches only tabs registered with the newer `UToolMenus` framework, so a tab reachable solely through a legacy `FMenuBarBuilder` menu was structurally unreachable. Adds 3 commands under `UAIP.Editor.Workspace`: `ListSpawnableTabs` (read-only; lists spawnable-tab candidates together with their owner identifier, display name, tooltip, and whether the tab is already open — read against the owner the row names, so a `Global` row answers for the whole editor rather than for one window, since a tab registered editor-wide docks wherever the layout puts it — the list is not exhaustive and says so), `OpenTabById` (opens a tab given its `TabId` and `OwnerTabId`, using an owner that is already open as it stands and opening the owner window first only when it is not, and reports whether it opened the owner itself so the caller knows what to clean up afterward — so every row the listing returns can be passed straight back in, including the panels inside an asset editor window), and `CloseTabById` (closes by the same pair of identifiers, taking an `InstanceId` when several instances of the same tab type are open). An `OwnerTabId` that matches several open windows at once is rejected with `InvalidParams` listing the candidates rather than guessed at; a tab the editor's own permission list rejects comes back as `NotAllowed` rather than the retried `ExecutionFailed`; and `OpenTabById` rolls back anything it opened (innermost first) when the target tab itself fails to come up. Adds 1 new DefaultDenied capability, `EditorTabSpawn`, required by all 3 commands — opening or closing an arbitrary tab can run third-party plugin code (`OnSpawnTab` / `OnCanCloseTab`, and the display-name / tooltip delegates the read-only list also has to call), so `ListSpawnableTabs` needs it too despite being read-only. ⚠️ Granting `EditorTabSpawn` lets an AI agent close **any** tab currently open, not only the ones it opened itself, and it can close a tab type the editor is configured to forbid **opening** — closing is deliberately exempt from that restriction so cleanup does not fail because of a display-permission change.

- **Writing references, structs and containers through the property commands — and editing one container element at a time** (`UAIPEditorShared` plus every module that writes properties): a property whose value is — or contains, at any depth — an object / class / soft / weak / lazy / interface reference, a delegate or a field path, and a property that is a struct outside the built-in value catalogue, an array, a set, a map, an optional or a fixed-size array, used to be refused **by type**, whatever the session held. Reading such a property always worked, so an AI agent could read a value and then had no way to write it back. Those writes are now governed by permission instead of being refused outright. Adds 2 DefaultDenied capabilities — `PropertyReferenceEdit` (the reference half; clearing a reference needs it too, since attaching and detaching a dependency are the same kind of change) and `PropertyStructuredEdit` (the struct / container half) — where a struct containing a reference needs **both**, so the structured one is never a way around the reference gate. Adds 4 optional parameters to 18 existing commands, with no new command and no signature change: `ValueJson` (the value as a JSON document instead of engine text, exclusive with the command's text value parameter), `Operation` (`Replace`, the default and exactly what these commands have always done, plus `Insert` / `Remove` / `Clear`), `ElementIndex` (the array position) and `ElementKeyJson` (the set element, or the map key). The commands are `SetActorProperty`, `SetAssetProperty`, `SetBlueprintDefault`, `SetWorldSetting`, `SetProjectSetting`, `SetDataTableRow` (`UAIP.Editor.Property`), `SetBlueprintComponentProperty`, `SetSectionProperty` (whose text parameter is `PropertyValue`), `SetSoundClassSettings`, `SetSoundAttenuationSettings`, `SetSoundMixSettings`, `SetSoundCueNodeProperty`, `SetDataflowNodeProperty`, `SetAnimNotifyProperty`, `SetPoseSearchSchemaChannelProperty`, and the three Subsonic property setters — `SetSubsonicEventActionProperty`, `SetSubsonicActionModifierProperty`, `SetSubsonicParameterValue` — whose `Value` is already a JSON value rather than engine text and which therefore take the three element-operation parameters but **no** `ValueJson`. A module that already governs reference writes through a capability of its own keeps that name for the reference half (`AnimNotifyReferenceEdit`, `DataflowReferenceEdit`, `SubsonicEventEdit`) while the struct / container half is always `PropertyStructuredEdit`, and a refusal always names the capability that command's own write path actually reads. `SetPCGNodeProperty`, `SetCustomCppPCGNodeProperty`, `SetCustomBlueprintPCGNodeProperty` and `SetConversationNodeProperty` already took their value as a JSON document and gain no element operations, but they no longer refuse references, structs and containers permanently either — those writes are gated by the same two capabilities. Element operations are idempotent where the container makes them so (adding an element a set already holds, removing one it never held), which is why the artifact reports `Changed` beside `Operation`; a container already holding more than 4096 elements is refused whatever the operation, `Remove` and `Clear` included, because the whole container is copied and validated before anything is committed. ⚠️ **A reference is never resolved by loading.** A hard reference names an object path that something already has loaded — "does not exist" and "exists but is not loaded" come back as the same refusal, because resolving without loading cannot tell them apart — while a soft reference is validated against the asset registry and needs nothing loaded. ⚠️ **References and composite values cannot be written as text**: passing an object path in `Value` is refused even with the capability granted, because the text import path would resolve and load the reference behind the capability gate. ⚠️ `SetPoseSearchSchemaChannelProperty` refuses a reference-bearing type outright with `PolicyViolation` and no capability lifts that, because writing a channel's sub-channel array directly would sidestep the class allowlist `AddPoseSearchSchemaChannel` enforces — add channels with that command instead. Because both capabilities are decided from the property's type while the write runs, neither appears in any command's declared `RequiredCapabilities`; see [Commands → Writing references, structs and containers](commands.md#writing-references-structs-and-containers).

- **Property reads now report what changing that property would take** (`UAIPEditorShared` plus the modules whose getters use it): the two capabilities above are decided from the property's type at write time, so they never appear in a command's declared `RequiredCapabilities` and `uaip_describe_command` cannot surface them — leaving an AI agent to discover them by being refused. Every property getter on the shared read path now attaches a `WriteRequirements` object beside the value it read: `RequiredCapabilities` (named as that command's own write path looks them up, so granting them actually unblocks the write), `HeldCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities` (the same names split by what the session issuing the read holds), `IsWritable` / `RefusalReason` (whether the property's type and flags permit a write at all, whatever the session holds), and `WriteInputForm` (`TextOrJson` or `JsonOnly` — which input field the value belongs in). `GetAnimNotifyClassSchema` and `GetPoseSearchChannelClassSchema` attach the identical `WriteRequirements` object nested per property, so a struct, set or map either command can in fact write in the structured form is reported as writable rather than being called unwritable the way their own `bIsWritable` used to. A handful of commands decide writability on their own terms and attach no report; for those the refusal names the missing capability instead, so the guidance is what is absent, not the way forward. `GetCustomPCGNodeSchema`, `GetCustomBlueprintPCGNodeSchema` and `GetPCGNativeNodeSchema` still show a session without those capabilities exactly the curated set of types they showed before, but no longer omit the rest silently: they now report `HiddenCount`, `HiddenCapabilities` (which permissions would reveal them) and a `HiddenReasons` object whose `MissingCapability` / `Unwritable` keys are always present and always sum to `HiddenCount` — the same "what was left out" shape `uaip_list_commands` and `SnapshotUI` already use. The names of the hidden properties are still not disclosed.

- **`QueryCapabilities` now says which capabilities exist, not only which ones the session holds** (`UAIPCore` module): `Capabilities` — the effective set — and `OperationalConstraints` are unchanged, and the response gains `RegisteredCapabilityCount` and `UngrantedCapabilityCount`, plus — when called with the new `IncludeUnavailable: true` — `RegisteredCapabilities`, one entry per capability every loaded module has declared, carrying its `Name`, its `DefaultPolicy` (`Allowed` or `Denied`) and an `IsGranted` flag. The catalog is opt-in because it runs to well over a hundred entries and this is the command an agent is told to call first; the two counts are returned on every call, so a caller that never opts in still learns the catalog exists and how much of it this session cannot use, the same promise `uaip_list_commands` makes with `HiddenCount`. "Which capabilities would an operator have to enable?" is answered from the catalog alone — they are the entries whose `DefaultPolicy` is `Denied` — so no second field repeats those names. Until now a DefaultDenied capability could only be found through some command's declared `RequiredCapabilities`. That covers all of them except the two the property write path decides from the type of the property being written: no command declares `PropertyReferenceEdit` or `PropertyStructuredEdit`, so an AI agent had no way to learn they existed short of attempting a write and reading the refusal, or reading a property's `WriteRequirements` — it could not plan around a permission it had no way to name. The catalog is deliberately **not** narrowed by the session's bound role: a role-restricted session sees the names with `IsGranted: false`, which is what lets it ask an operator to enable one or understand why an expected command is missing, while the role still denies the operation itself. Only capability names and their default policies are disclosed — no values, and nothing about the project's contents.

- **Four module-specific reference capabilities** (`UAIPEditorUMG`, `UAIPEditorEnhancedInput`, `UAIPEditorStateTree`, `UAIPEditorNiagara` modules): `WidgetSlotReferenceEdit`, `EnhancedInputReferenceEdit`, `StateTreeParameterReferenceEdit` and `NiagaraReferenceEdit` — all DefaultDenied — gate reference writes on `SetSlotProperties`, on the four Enhanced Input trigger / modifier setters, on `SetStateTreeParameter`, and on a reference-typed parameter default passed to `AddSetParameterEntry` / `AddSetParametersModule` respectively, following the same module-owns-its-reference-capability pattern as `AnimNotifyReferenceEdit` and `DataflowReferenceEdit`. Writing a struct or container on the same properties additionally requires `PropertyStructuredEdit`. Like the other two, they are decided from the property's type while the write runs, so they never appear in a command's declared `RequiredCapabilities`; all four are listed in `QueryCapabilities(IncludeUnavailable: true)`'s `RegisteredCapabilities` catalog, and the matching getters report them per property — except the Niagara one, since no read command returns a Set Parameters entry's default value. `NiagaraReferenceEdit` covers giving a Niagara parameter a default that points at an asset: the value is supplied as an object path and is stored in the dedicated data-interface / object slot of `FNiagaraVariant`, so the reference is retained properly rather than packed into the byte payload the other parameter types use. ⚠️ In practice only **data interface** types are reachable through it — an ordinary object type such as `UTexture2D` is refused at the parameter type-name stage by the type allowlist, before the capability is ever consulted. What this changes for existing callers is described under **Changed** below.

- **`MaterialCustomTypeEdit`, a new capability gating project- and plugin-defined Material Expression classes** (`UAIPEditorMaterial` module): `AddMaterialNode`'s `ExpressionClass` admitted only classes from four built-in engine modules (`Engine`, `RenderCore`, `MaterialEditor`, `Landscape`) and refused everything else outright — there was no way to add a project's own `UMaterialExpression` subclass at all, however ordinary. A class outside those four modules now requires the new DefaultDenied capability `MaterialCustomTypeEdit` instead of being refused unconditionally. Separately, `MaterialCustomNodeEdit` — already registered, but not required by any command — now actually gates `UMaterialExpressionCustom` / `UMaterialExpressionCustomOutput` and their subclasses (arbitrary HLSL), regardless of which module they come from; a class that is both project-defined and custom-HLSL needs both capabilities together. Neither is granted by default. What this changes for existing callers is described under **Changed** below.

- **`AnimBlueprintCustomTypeEdit`, a new capability gating project- and plugin-defined AnimGraph node classes** (`UAIPEditorAnimBlueprint` module): `AddAnimGraphNode`'s `NodeClass` admitted only classes from three built-in engine modules (`AnimGraph`, `AnimGraphRuntime`, `Engine`) and refused everything else outright — there was no way to add a project's own `UAnimGraphNode_Base` subclass at all, however ordinary. A class outside those three modules now requires the new DefaultDenied capability `AnimBlueprintCustomTypeEdit` instead of being refused unconditionally. Unlike Material, there is no companion "dangerous node" capability here: eight node kinds (`UAnimGraphNode_StateResult`, `TransitionResult`, `TransitionPoseEvaluator`, `Root`, `StateMachineBase`, `LinkedAnimGraph`, `LinkedAnimLayer`, `CustomProperty`) stay refused at the AnimGraph root regardless of any capability held — they are internal- or sub-graph-only node kinds the graph does not accept from that direction, not a danger a capability grant unlocks. Not granted by default. What this changes for existing callers is described under **Changed** below.
- **`ControlRigCustomTypeEdit`, a new capability gating project- and plugin-defined RigVM unit structs and rig hierarchy component structs** (`UAIPEditorControlRig` module): `AddGraphNode`'s `StructPath` and `AddComponent`'s `ComponentStructPath` admitted only structs from the seven modules this domain ships against and refused everything else outright — there was no way to place a project's own `FRigUnit` descendant, or attach its own `FRigBaseComponent` descendant, at all. A struct outside those modules now requires the new DefaultDenied capability `ControlRigCustomTypeEdit` instead of being refused unconditionally. As in AnimBlueprint and unlike Material, there is no companion "dangerous type" capability: a unit struct is a compiled function the VM calls with values from pins and a component struct is data hanging off a hierarchy element, so neither carries code the caller supplied. Control types are not gated at all — the set is fixed by the engine, so there is no project-defined control type for a capability to guard. Structs marked `Deprecated` or `Hidden` stay refused regardless of any capability held. Not granted by default. What this changes for existing callers is described under **Changed** below.
- **`EnhancedInputCustomTypeEdit`, a new capability gating project- and plugin-defined Trigger and Modifier classes** (`UAIPEditorEnhancedInput` module): the four trigger / modifier setters resolved `Class` only inside `/Script/EnhancedInput` and refused anything from elsewhere outright — there was no spelling that reached a project's own `UInputTrigger` / `UInputModifier` subclass at all, let alone a Blueprint one. `Class` now also accepts a full object path (`/Script/MyGame.MyGameInputTrigger`, `/Game/Input/BPT_Hold.BPT_Hold_C`), and a class from outside that module requires the new DefaultDenied capability `EnhancedInputCustomTypeEdit` instead of being unreachable. Short names keep resolving exactly as before. As in AnimBlueprint and ControlRig, and unlike Material, there is no companion "dangerous type" capability: a trigger evaluates key state and a modifier rescales an input value, so neither runs code the caller supplied. Two trigger kinds — `UInputTriggerChordAction` and `UInputTriggerChordBlocker`, plus anything derived from either — stay refused regardless of any capability held: Enhanced Input wires them up as part of a chord rather than offering them to an author, and the engine's own class picker hides them for the same reason. Not granted by default. What this changes for existing callers is described under **Changed** below.
- **`GetAvailableInputTriggerClasses` and `GetAvailableInputModifierClasses`, two new read-only commands listing every loaded `UInputTrigger` / `UInputModifier` subclass** (`UAIPEditorEnhancedInput` module): before these existed, finding out that a project's own trigger or modifier class needed `EnhancedInputCustomTypeEdit` meant naming it in one of the four setters and being refused — there was nowhere to learn it in advance. Each entry carries `Admission` (`Allowed` / `RequiresCapabilities` / `NotAddable` / `CompatibilityUnknownUntilAuthorized`) plus `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities`, judged by the same policy those setters validate against; `UInputTriggerChordAction` / `UInputTriggerChordBlocker` are listed as `NotAddable` since no capability opens them. Ordered by `ClassPath`; each reports `TotalCount` / `ReturnedCount` / `Truncated` (max 200). Both require only `EditorInspect`.

- **`MetaSoundCustomTypeEdit`, a new capability gating project- and plugin-defined MetaSound node classes** (`UAIPEditorMetaSound` module): `AddMetaSoundNode`'s `ClassName` admitted only classes from four namespaces (`UE`, `Metasound`, `MetasoundStandardNodes`, `MetasoundEditor`) and refused everything else outright — there was no way to add a project's own node class at all, however ordinary. A class from outside those namespaces now requires the new DefaultDenied capability `MetaSoundCustomTypeEdit` instead of being refused unconditionally. ⚠️ **This affects ordinary graphs more than in the other gated domains**: MetaSound registers every MetaSound asset's own graph class without a namespace, so a node standing for another MetaSound asset — as a subgraph or a preset — is always outside the four namespaces and always needs this capability, regardless of what that other asset otherwise contains. Not granted by default. What this changes for existing callers is described under **Changed** below.

- **Three new capabilities gating BehaviorTree node classes, node properties, and Blackboard key types** (`UAIPEditorBehaviorTree` module): the four `Add*` node commands and `AddBlackboardKey` admitted types only from `/Script/AIModule` (plus `/Script/AITestSuite` for node classes, `/Script/Engine` for node properties) and refused everything else outright — there was no way to add a project's own BT node, write a property such a node declares, or declare a Blackboard key of a project's own key type, at all. The new DefaultDenied capability `BehaviorTreeCustomTypeEdit` now gates all three surfaces for a type this domain does not ship. A second new capability, `BehaviorTreeExternalBehaviorNodeEdit`, separately gates five node kinds whose body is defined somewhere other than the class itself — the two sub-tree runners (`BTTask_RunBehavior`, `BTTask_RunBehaviorDynamic`) and the three Blueprint-backed bases (`BTTask_BlueprintBase`, `BTDecorator_BlueprintBase`, `BTService_BlueprintBase`) — asked for independently of where the class came from, since `/Script/AIModule` ships these kinds itself. ⚠️ A Blueprint-authored BT node needs both capabilities together.
