A third, `BlackboardReferenceKeyTypeEdit`, gates the two Blackboard key kinds that hold a reference the writer chooses (`UBlackboardKeyType_Object`, `UBlackboardKeyType_Class`), independently of the other two. None is granted by default. What this changes for existing callers is described under **Changed** below.

- **Two new capabilities gating EQS Generator classes, Test classes, and node properties** (`UAIPEditorEQS` module): `AddEQSGenerator`, `AddEQSTest`, `SetEQSGeneratorProperty` and `SetEQSTestProperty` admitted types only from `/Script/AIModule` and refused everything else outright — there was no way to add a project's own Generator or Test, or write a property such a node declares, at all. The new DefaultDenied capability `EQSCustomTypeEdit` now gates all three surfaces for a type this domain does not ship. A second new capability, `EQSDelegatedGeneratorEdit`, separately gates a Generator kind whose item production is not its own compiled code — `UEnvQueryGenerator_Composite`, which runs a set of nested Generator instances held inside it, and `UEnvQueryGenerator_BlueprintBase`, whose subclasses carry a graph authored in the editor — asked for independently of where the class came from, since `/Script/AIModule` ships these kinds itself, and for a property such a Generator declares. ⚠️ A project-defined Composite-derived Generator needs both capabilities together. The Test surface has no companion "dangerous type" capability — nothing this domain accepts as a Test runs code somewhere other than its own compiled class. Neither is granted by default. What this changes for existing callers is described under **Changed** below.

- **`StateTreeCustomTypeEdit`, a new capability gating project- and plugin-defined StateTree task, evaluator and enter condition classes, and the classes declaring their properties** (`UAIPEditorStateTree` module): `AddStateTask`, `AddGlobalTask`, `AddEvaluator`, `AddStateEnterCondition` and the four `Set*Property` commands admitted types only from `/Script/StateTreeModule`, `/Script/AIModule` and `/Script/GameplayStateTreeModule`, and refused everything else outright — there was no way to add a project's own task, evaluator or enter condition, or write a property such a node declares, at all. The new DefaultDenied capability `StateTreeCustomTypeEdit` now gates both surfaces (the node's own class, and the class declaring a property being written) for a type this domain does not ship. A property write checks the two independently, so a session missing either one is refused, naming both when both are missing. There is no companion "dangerous type" capability for this domain, as in MetaSound and EQS's Test surface: a task, evaluator or enter condition field is a compiled function the tree calls with values from its own instance data, and a node property is a plain data member — neither carries code the caller wrote. Not granted by default. What this changes for existing callers is described under **Changed** below.
- **`WorldConditionsCustomTypeEdit`, a new capability gating project- and plugin-defined WorldCondition structs and the structs declaring their properties** (`UAIPEditorWorldConditions` module): `AddWorldCondition` and `ReplaceWorldCondition` admitted condition structs only from `/Script/WorldConditions` and refused everything else outright — there was no way to add a project's own condition struct at all, however ordinary. The new DefaultDenied capability `WorldConditionsCustomTypeEdit` now gates both surfaces (the condition struct itself, and the struct declaring a property being written) for a type this domain does not ship. Writing a property already sitting in an asset is gated by it too — this is a new restriction rather than a migrated one: before this capability existed, a project-defined condition's own properties were writable without any capability check on the declaring struct at all. There is no companion "dangerous type" capability for this domain, as in MetaSound, EQS's Test surface and StateTree: a condition's `IsTrue()` is a compiled function its own author wrote, and a condition property is a plain data member — neither carries code the caller supplied. Not granted by default. What this changes for existing callers is described under **Changed** below.
- **`SequencerCustomTypeEdit`, a new capability gating project- and plugin-defined track classes and decoration classes** (`UAIPEditorSequencer` module). It covers two independent surfaces that share one name because neither has a companion "dangerous type" capability behind it. On the track surface, `AddTrack` admitted track classes only from four modules (`/Script/MovieScene`, `/Script/MovieSceneTracks`, `/Script/LevelSequence`, `/Script/MovieSceneAnimMixer`) and refused everything else outright — there was no way to add a project's own track class at all, however ordinary; `UMovieSceneSubTrack`, `UMovieSceneCinematicShotTrack` and `UMovieSceneEventTrack` keep being refused on `AddTrack` regardless of any capability held, since this domain only ever creates the first two through their own dedicated commands and has no command for the third at all. The new DefaultDenied capability `SequencerCustomTypeEdit` now gates the track class named in `AddTrack`, and the owning track's class in `RemoveTrack` / `AddSection` / `RemoveSection` / `AddKeyframe` / `RemoveKeyframe` / `SetKeyframeValue` — except that removing one of the three internal-only track classes already in a sequence needs no capability at all, as long as it comes from a trusted module, so an asset that already holds one stays repairable. On the decoration surface, `AddDecoration` / `RemoveDecoration` (native and the `Toolset.Editor.SequencerAnimMixer.*` bridge versions of each) used to resolve `DecorationClass` with `StaticLoadClass` against no base type constraint at all — any class that resolved, project-defined or not, was force-loaded and accepted outright. The same capability now gates a class from outside five modules (`/Script/MovieScene`, `/Script/MovieSceneTracks`, `/Script/MovieSceneAnimMixer`, `/Script/MovieScenePoseSearchTracks`, `/Script/MovieSceneMixedControlRig`), and `StaticLoadClass` is gone — `DecorationClass` is resolved only against what this editor already has loaded. ⚠️ **The decoration surface is a new restriction, not a migrated one**: before this capability existed, a project's own decoration class was already usable outright. There is no companion "dangerous type" capability for either surface: a track class runs only the engine's own compiled logic against data the request supplies, and a decoration class is a plain data object whose class name is the only thing a request ever contributes. Not granted by default. What this changes for existing callers is described under **Changed** below.
- **`SoundCueCustomTypeEdit`, a new capability gating project- and plugin-defined SoundCue node classes** (`UAIPEditorSoundCue` module): `AddSoundCueNode` admitted node classes only from `/Script/Engine` and refused everything else outright — there was no way to add a project's own `USoundNode` subclass at all, however ordinary. The new DefaultDenied capability `SoundCueCustomTypeEdit` now gates the class named in `AddSoundCueNode`, and the class of the node or endpoint acted on in `RemoveSoundCueNode` / `ConnectSoundCuePins` / `DisconnectSoundCuePins` / `SetSoundCueNodeProperty`. `CompileSoundCue` never asks for it — it names no node class of its own, and the classes a cue already holds are judged by a separate, permission-free question this domain always answers the same way, so a cue containing a project's own node stays compilable without it. The graph's root output node owns no `USoundNode` at all and is never judged by either question. There is no companion "dangerous type" capability for this domain, as in ControlRig, MetaSound and StateTree: a `USoundNode` subclass processes audio according to its own class implementation and never runs code the request itself supplies. Not granted by default. What this changes for existing callers is described under **Changed** below.
- **`GetAvailableSoundCueNodeClasses`, a new read-only command listing every loaded `USoundNode` subclass** (`UAIPEditorSoundCue` module): before it existed, finding out that a project's own node class needed `SoundCueCustomTypeEdit` meant naming it in `AddSoundCueNode` and being refused — there was nowhere to learn it in advance. Each entry carries `Admission` (`Allowed` / `RequiresCapabilities` / `NotAddable` / `CompatibilityUnknownUntilAuthorized`) plus `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities`, judged by the same policy `AddSoundCueNode` validates against. Ordered by `NodeClass`; reports `TotalCount` / `ReturnedCount` / `Truncated` (max 200). `SchemaVersion` is `1`. Requires only `EditorInspect`.
- **`MotionMatchingCustomTypeEdit`, a new capability gating project- and plugin-defined PoseSearch channel classes and the classes declaring their properties** (`UAIPEditorMotionMatching` module): `AddPoseSearchSchemaChannel` admitted channel classes only from `/Script/PoseSearch` and refused everything else outright — there was no way to add a project's own `UPoseSearchFeatureChannel` subclass at all, however ordinary. The new DefaultDenied capability `MotionMatchingCustomTypeEdit` now gates both surfaces (the channel class itself, and the class declaring a property being written) for a type this domain does not ship. Writing a property already sitting in a schema is gated by it too — this is a new restriction rather than a migrated one: before this capability existed, a project-defined channel's own properties were writable without any capability check on the declaring class at all. A Blueprint generated channel class stays refused outright regardless of any capability held — it is not the kind of custom type this capability opens. There is no companion "dangerous type" capability for this domain, as in MetaSound, EQS's Test surface, StateTree and WorldConditions: a channel class's `Finalize` / `BuildQuery` / `IndexAsset` is a compiled function its own author wrote, and a channel property is a plain data member — neither carries code the caller supplied. Not granted by default. What this changes for existing callers is described under **Changed** below.
- **`ConversationCustomTypeEdit`, a new capability gating project- and plugin-defined Conversation node classes, including Blueprint generated ones** (`UAIPEditorConversation` module): `AddConversationNode` / `AddConversationSubNode` admitted node classes only from `/Script/CommonConversationRuntime` and refused everything else outright — including a Blueprint generated subclass of `UConversationTaskNode` / `UConversationRequirementNode` / `UConversationSideEffectNode` / `UConversationChoiceNode`, all four of which the stock graph editor's own "Add Node" menu offers as Blueprintable bases. There was no way to add a project's own node class, or a Blueprint one built from an offered base, however ordinary. The new DefaultDenied capability `ConversationCustomTypeEdit` now gates the class named in `AddConversationNode` / `AddConversationSubNode`, and the class of the node or endpoint acted on in `RemoveConversationNode` / `ConnectConversationNodes` / `DisconnectConversationNodes` / `SetConversationNodeProperty`. The implicit bank rebuild every mutation performs never asks for it on the classes a database already holds — rebuilding runs no code the request itself supplies, so a database containing a project's own node stays compilable and repairable without a grant. There is no companion "dangerous type" capability for this domain, as in ControlRig, MetaSound and StateTree: a `UConversationNode` subclass runs the behaviour its own class implements and never runs code the request itself supplies. Not granted by default. What this changes for existing callers is described under **Changed** below.
- **AnimMixer layer, section, transition and decoration editing** (`UAIPEditorSequencer` module, requires the `MovieSceneAnimMixer` plugin). ⚠️ **Not a new feature — these commands shipped in UAIP 1.0.0 and were never written up here; this entry backfills that gap.** AI agents can author a LevelSequence's animation mixer directly: create, name, reorder and clear layers, place and retime the animation sections inside them, set a layer's blend weight / mute / enabled state, wire transitions between sections, and attach decorations to a layer. Adds 36 commands under `UAIP.Editor.Sequencer`, all addressed by the stable `(AssetPath, BindingGuid, LayerIndex)` triplet — track and layer inventory plus naming (`GetAnimMixerTrackInfo`, `GetMixerLayers`, `GetMixerLayerCount`, `GetLayerName`, `SetLayerName`, `GetLayerIndex`, `GetLayerSections`, `IsLayerEmpty`), layer lifecycle (`AddMixerLayer`, `InsertMixerLayer` (inserts an empty layer at an index, shifts the rest down, and returns the new index), `RemoveMixerLayer`, `MoveMixerLayer`, `ClearMixerLayer`), layer compositing state (`GetLayerBlendWeight`, `SetLayerBlendWeight`, `IsLayerMuted`, `SetLayerMuted`, `IsLayerEnabled`, `SetLayerEnabled`; the three getters answer with the engine defaults — weight `1.0`, not muted, enabled — while the layer still carries no decoration), sections (`AddMixerSection`, `RemoveMixerSection`, `SetMixerSectionRange`, `SetMixerSectionAnimation`, `GetMixerSectionInfo`), transitions (`AddMixerTransition`, `RemoveMixerTransition`, `GetTransitionsForSection`, `GetTransitionBetween`, `GetTransitionInfo`, `GetTransitionName`, and `ChangeTransitionType`, which replaces a transition with one of `NewTransitionClass` as a create-then-delete inside a single transaction), and decorations (`GetCompatibleDecorations`, `GetDecorations`, `FindDecoration`, `AddDecoration`, `RemoveDecoration`, with `DecorationClass` given as a fully-qualified class path). ⚠️ `SetMixerSectionRange` and `GetMixerSectionInfo` speak raw `FFrameNumber` ticks rather than the DisplayRate frames the rest of the module uses. No new capability was added — the read-only commands require `EditorInspect` and the writing ones the module's existing `SequencerStructureEdit`. On UE 5.8+ with the `SequencerAnimMixerToolset` plugin enabled, 20 bridge commands mirroring the layer, transition and decoration operations are also available under `Toolset.Editor.SequencerAnimMixer.*` (Layers 10, Transitions 5, Decorations 5); one of them, `AddAnimationToMixer`, has no native counterpart — natively the same result comes from `AddMixerSection` followed by `SetMixerSectionAnimation`.

- **AnimBlueprint Anim Layers, dynamic pose pins, and a UAF graph embed** (`UAIPEditorAnimBlueprint` module): AI agents can now write to an AnimGraph node's own properties, add and remove dynamic pose input pins on nodes that support them, author self-contained and interface-implemented Anim Layer graphs, place `LinkedAnimLayer` nodes, and embed a Unified Animation Framework graph directly into an AnimGraph. Adds 8 commands under `UAIP.Editor.AnimBlueprint` — `SetAnimGraphNodeProperty` (dot-notation `PropertyPath`; references go through `ValueJson` and the new `AnimBlueprintReferenceEdit`, structs / containers through `PropertyStructuredEdit`, see [Writing references, structs and containers](commands.md#writing-references-structs-and-containers)), `GetAnimGraphNodeDetails` (read-only pin / property inspection, secret values withheld), `AddAnimGraphNodePosePin` / `RemoveAnimGraphNodePosePin` (currently only `UAnimGraphNode_LayeredBoneBlend` supports this), `AddAnimLayerGraph` / `RemoveAnimLayerGraph` (self-contained layers, root AnimBlueprint only), `ImplementAnimLayerInterface` (generates one layer graph per anim-layer function an interface declares), and `AddLinkedAnimLayerNode` (places a `LinkedAnimLayer` node at either kind of layer). Adds 1 more command, `AddUAFGraphNodeToAnimBlueprint`, under the new `UAIP.Editor.AnimBlueprint.UAF` provider segment (requires the Engine's `UAFAnimGraph` plugin; kept apart from the rest of the module because it cannot construct before that optional plugin is present). Adds 1 new DefaultDenied capability, `AnimBlueprintReferenceEdit` — required, statically or dynamically depending on the command, whenever a call writes an object or class reference on an Anim Blueprint (see [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#blueprint--anim-blueprint-editing)). `GetAnimBlueprintInfo` gains an optional `IncludePins` (default `false`) that adds a `Pins[]` array to each node entry; omitting it leaves the output exactly as before. Every write command in this entry is refused during Play-in-Editor.
- **Unified Animation Framework (UAF) graph editing, plus Skeleton BlendProfile editing** (`UAIPEditorUAF` module, **new**, requires the Engine's own `UAF` plugin; BlendProfile commands ship in the existing `UAIPEditorSkeleton` module): AI agents can now inspect and edit UAF assets (`UUAFAnimGraph` / `UUAFSystem`) — RigVM graph nodes, pins, variables and event graphs — and create and populate Skeleton BlendProfiles. Adds 19 commands under `UAIP.Editor.UAF` — read-only `GetUAFAssetInfo`, `ListUAFEntries`, `ListUAFGraphs`, `ListUAFNodes`, `GetUAFNodeInfo`, `ListUAFPins`, `GetUAFPinValue`, `ListUAFVariables`, `GetUAFVariable`, `GetAvailableUAFUnitStructs`; and mutating `AddUAFGraphNode`, `RemoveUAFGraphNode`, `ConnectUAFPins`, `DisconnectUAFPins`, `SetUAFPinValue`, `AddUAFVariable`, `RemoveUAFVariable`, `AddUAFEventGraph`, `CompileUAFAsset` — plus 1 more command, `GetAvailableUAFTraits`, under a separate `UAIP.Editor.UAF.AnimGraph` provider segment that additionally requires the `UAFAnimGraph` plugin. **Every command in both provider segments is `Stability: Experimental`**, since the underlying UAF plugin is itself an Experimental engine feature and its API may change without notice in a future engine release. Adds 3 DefaultDenied capabilities: `UAFGraphEdit` (required by all 9 mutating commands), `UAFCustomTypeEdit` (a RigVM unit struct or animation trait from outside the framework's own packages, asked for dynamically the same way `AnimBlueprintCustomTypeEdit` is), and `UAFReferenceEdit` (`SetUAFPinValue` writing an object / class reference into a pin). Separately, adds 3 commands under `UAIP.Editor.Skeleton` — `CreateBlendProfile` (`Mode` is `TimeFactor` / `WeightFactor` / `BlendMask`), `SetBlendProfileBoneScale`, `ListBlendProfiles` (read-only) — governed by the existing `SkeletonAssetEdit` capability; no new capability added for these three.
- **Attach a Control Rig as a child track inside an AnimMixer layer** (`UAIPEditorSequencer` module, requires `MovieSceneAnimMixer` and `ControlRig`): AI agents can now add, remove, list, and move a ControlRig child track parented to an AnimMixer layer, and set or read the animation section's blend type, instead of only editing the compositing layer itself. Adds 6 commands under `UAIP.Editor.Sequencer` — `AddMixerChildTrack` (creates a `UControlRig`-derived instance of `ControlRigPath`, attaches its parameter section to the layer at `LayerIndex` — which may equal the current layer count to append a new row — and takes an optional `IsLayered` (default `false`) to configure the new rig as additive; fails with `Conflict` and no side effects when the target layer already holds a child track or animation sections), `RemoveMixerChildTrack` (tears down the layer reference, the child-track bookkeeping, its decorations, and the binding together; `NotFound` when the layer has no child track), `GetMixerChildTracks` (read-only; lists every child track across all layers of a binding's AnimMixer track, each entry carrying `LayerIndex` / `TrackName` / `TrackClass`), `MoveMixerChildTrack` (moves the child track parented to one layer to another, succeeding as a no-op when the two indices are equal and failing with `Conflict` when the destination already holds a child track or animation sections), `SetMixerSectionBlendType` (matches `BlendType` case-insensitively against Absolute / Additive / Relative / Override, and rejects a value the target section does not support), and `GetMixerSectionBlendType` (read-only; reports the current blend type together with the set of blend types the section supports). The four write commands require the domain's existing `SequencerStructureEdit` capability — no new capability was added — and the two read commands require only `EditorInspect`.
- **`GraphGuid`, an alternative to naming a graph by `GraphName` when the name might not resolve to exactly one graph** (`UAIPEditorBlueprint`, `UAIPEditorAnimBlueprint` modules): `AddGraphNode` (Blueprint), `AddLinkedAnimLayerNode`, and `AddUAFGraphNodeToAnimBlueprint` now accept an optional `GraphGuid` — the target's `UEdGraph::GraphGuid` in 32-hex-digit form — mutually exclusive with the corresponding graph-name parameter (`InvalidParams` when both are set). It exists to resolve a name that matched more than one graph; see the corresponding entries under **Changed** and **Fixed** for when that can happen and what these commands do about it.
- **`GetBehaviorTreeNodeProperties`, a new read-only command for inspecting a BehaviorTree node's own properties** (`UAIPEditorBehaviorTree` module, **new command**): before this, the domain had no way to read a node's properties at all — `SetBehaviorTreeNodeProperty` could write one, but there was no way to confirm what it wrote, or to see a property's current value before touching it. Addressed the same way as the setter (`AssetPath` + `NodeId`), it returns every property the node's `NodeInstance` declares: `PropertyName`, `PropertyType` (the C++ type name), `PropertyValue` in the exact form the setter accepts back — UE's text-import notation, a JSON document for a struct outside the shared writable value catalog, or the bare key name for a `FBlackboardKeySelector` — and a nested `WriteRequirements` object (`RequiredCapabilities` / `HeldCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities` / `IsWritable` / `RefusalReason` / `WriteInputForm`). Requires only `EditorInspect`; permitted during PIE (degraded mode). See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.BehaviorTree](commands.md#uaipeditorbehaviortree).
- **`GET /uaip/instance-proof`, a new unauthenticated HTTP route that lets a client verify it is talking to this project's editor before it sends any credential** (`UAIPTransportHTTP` module): a client sends a fresh 64-character lowercase-hex `challenge` as a query parameter; the editor signs it with a per-launch secret only it can read (`Saved/UAIP/InstanceSecrets/<port>.txt`, regenerated on every start and removed on a clean stop) and answers with `{Algorithm: "HMAC-SHA1", Version, Port, Mode, AuthRequired, Proof}`. A caller who computes the same HMAC over the same secret confirms the program on that port is genuinely this project's UAIP editor — not a different project's editor sharing the port after a restart, an unrelated program, or a port-forwarding relay — before it ever attaches a Bearer token or role token to a request. `AuthRequired` reports whether a credential is actually needed: for FullHTTP it reflects `-uaip-http-no-auth`; for MCPOnly it reflects whether any `[UAIP.Roles]` are defined, since that mode's own auth flag never applies to it. HMAC-SHA1 was chosen over a newer hash because HMAC's security does not depend on the underlying hash resisting collision attacks, and SHA1 was already the only hash the engine's own `FSHA1` exposes for this. Both the MCP Bridge and the new [Claude Code Plugin](claude-code-plugin.md) use this before sending a credential. See [Security → Instance proof](security.md#instance-proof) for the full protocol and [Connections → HTTP API](connections.md#http-api-pro) for the endpoint.
- **`POST /uaip/commands` can now be given a longer wait per request, with an optional top-level `TimeoutSeconds`** (`UAIPTransportHTTP` module): the HTTP transport's async command timeout was fixed at 120 seconds for every command, so a command that legitimately runs longer than that — a large Automation Test run, for instance — always came back as a timeout, even though the editor kept working on it. A JSON number from 1 to 1800, at the top level of the request body (not inside `Params`), now overrides that wait for this one request; omitting the field keeps the 120-second default unchanged. A value outside that range, of the wrong JSON type, or a numeric string such as `"300"` is refused with 400 `InvalidParams` before the command starts, and the field is never copied into `Params`, so no command handler ever sees it. See [Connections → HTTP API → Executing a command](connections.md#executing-a-command) and [API Reference → Request format](api.md#2-request-format) (§2.1). The bundled [Claude Code Plugin](claude-code-plugin.md)'s `exec.py` script gained a matching `--timeout N` flag (1–1800) that sets this field and waits `N + 10` seconds for the response; it cannot be combined with scenario mode, which has its own 1800-second wall-clock limit instead. The MCP Bridge's `uaip_execute` tool call accepts the same field — see the **MCP Bridge** section below.
- **`UAIP.Core.ListIntegrations`, a new read-only command that reports the state of every optional-plugin integration UAIP knows about** (`UAIPCore` module; see the optional-plugin loading change below for what an "integration" is and why this exists): for each one, returns its load state (`Pending` / `Loading` / `Loaded` / `CompiledOut` / `PluginDisabled` / `PluginNotInstalled` / `DependencyNotLoaded` / `NotApplicable` / `LoadFailed`, plus `NotInThisEdition` in the free demo build for an integration only the Pro edition includes), the plugins it requires and which of them are missing, the other integrations it depends on, the command name(s) or prefix it answers for, and a plain-language `Remedy` — the same wording a `CommandNotFound` hint gives for one of its commands, so the two surfaces never disagree. An optional `State` parameter narrows the returned `Integrations` array to that one state; `IntegrationCount` / `LoadedCount` / `NotLoadedCount` always cover every integration regardless of the filter, and an unrecognised `State` is refused with `InvalidParams` rather than returning an empty list. Requires no capability. See [Commands Reference — UAIP.Core](commands.md#uaipcore).
- **Pending interactions — commands that hand one step to a human in the editor — and `DrawPCGSpline`, the first of them** (`UAIPCore` and `UAIPEditorPCG` modules; `DrawPCGSpline` requires the `PCG` plugin): some editor operations only make sense with a human's hand on the mouse, and drawing a spline in the level viewport is one. A command can now start such an operation and return at once with an `InteractionId` instead of blocking until the human finishes; `ListCommands` and `DescribeCommand` mark it with the new `IsInteractive: true` field. Adds 3 commands under `UAIP.Core` to follow one up — `GetPendingInteractionStatus` (where it stands, without waiting), `WaitForPendingInteraction` (blocks for up to `TimeoutSeconds`, default 30, range [1, 600]; a timeout leaves the interaction waiting for the human, and up to 4 calls may watch the same interaction) and `CancelPendingInteraction` — gated by the new `PendingInteractionInspect` capability (DefaultAllow); and `UAIP.Editor.PCG.DrawPCGSpline` with its bridge counterpart `Toolset.Editor.PCG.DrawSpline`, gated by the new `PCGSplineDraw` capability (DefaultDenied). The native command waits up to 1800 seconds for the human by default, the bridge up to 600; one interaction may hold the level viewport at a time, and the editor shows a notification with a cancel button while it is pending. Starting any interaction also requires the new SafetyPolicy flag `AllowUserInteractionPrompt` (default `False`; CLI `-uaip-policy-allow-user-interaction-prompt`), refused with `PolicyViolation` before anything in the editor changes — the capability says *what* may be touched, the flag says that taking over the human's viewport and input focus is acceptable at all. All four commands require an explicitly given `SessionId`, and only the session that started an interaction can see or cancel it. See [Commands Reference — UAIP.Core](commands.md#uaipcore), [UAIP.Editor.PCG](commands.md#uaipeditorpcg-) and [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md).
- **`ListCommands` now accounts for every command it leaves out, and says why each one is unavailable** (`UAIPCore` module): the default response has always omitted the commands a session cannot call, but gave no sign they existed. It now always carries `HiddenCount`, `HiddenCapabilities` (the capabilities whose absence hid them) and `HiddenReasons`, which attributes each hidden command to exactly one of five reasons — `DeniedCommand`, `MissingCapability`, `RoleRestricted`, `ReadOnlyPolicy`, `HandlerUnavailable` — so its values sum to `HiddenCount`. `HiddenAdditionalReasons`, with the same five keys, counts reasons that applied *on top of* that one, so a caller can tell "granting these capabilities would reveal the commands" from "they would still be unavailable afterwards". With `IncludeUnavailable: true`, each hidden row carries `UnavailableReason`, `AdditionalUnavailableReasons` and `RequiredCapabilities`, and `DescribeCommand` reports the same two reason fields for a single command. The MCP `uaip_list_commands` tool schema gains the `ProviderPrefix`, `KeywordFilter`, `IncludeUnavailable` and `Stability` arguments, matching the command's own parameters. See the behavior change for `DeniedCommands` under **Changed** below, and [API Reference → Command availability fields](api.md#65-command-availability-fields).
- **Roles — a narrower capability set per MCP client** (`UAIPCore` and `UAIPTransportHTTP` modules): a new `[UAIP.Roles]` section defines named roles with `+Role=(Name="…", DeniedCapabilities=(…))`. A role can only take capabilities away from the process-wide set, never add any, and its name must match `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}`. With no role defined — the default — nothing changes. Once at least one is defined, the editor writes one token per role to `Saved/UAIP/Roles/<Role>.token` at startup, MCP requests must carry one as `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (401 otherwise), and the first request for a `SessionId` binds that session to its role for good; a later request presenting a different role for it is refused. `ListCommands` counts role-denied commands under `HiddenReasons.RoleRestricted`, `DescribeCommand` reports `UnavailableReason: "RoleRestricted"`, `QueryCapabilities` reports the role-narrowed set, and calling a role-denied command fails with `CapabilityNotAvailable` naming both the role and the capability. ⚠️ **WebSocket, CLI and the FullHTTP mode cannot carry a role, so once a role is defined they refuse to start**, unless `AllowRoleBlindTransports=True` is set — in which case each logs a warning and runs with the unrestricted process-wide set. The bridge side is in the **MCP Bridge** section below. See [Safety & Capabilities → Roles](safety.md#roles-layer-15) and [Configuration → `[UAIP.Roles]`](config.md#uaiproles--role-based-capability-downgrade).
- **Passive waits can run alongside each other and alongside ordinary commands, if you opt in** (`UAIPTransportHTTP` module — the HTTP and MCP transports): a command that only waits — for a condition, a widget, shader compilation, a human — used to hold the editor's single command slot for as long as it waited, so nothing else could run meanwhile. Setting the new `[UAIP.Transport] AllowConcurrentPassiveWaits=True` (default `False`) moves the 6 commands that declare themselves passive waits (`WaitForPendingInteraction`, `WaitForShaderCompilation`, `WaitForCondition`, `WaitSeconds`, `WaitForWidget`, `ObserveWidget`) into a separate pool capped by `MaxConcurrentPassiveWaits` (default 16, range [1, 64]; each session gets `max(1, N / 4)`). Exceeding either cap is refused with `TooManyRequests`. CLI equivalents are `-uaip-allow-concurrent-passive-waits` / `-uaip-max-concurrent-passive-waits=N`; there is deliberately no console variable, so a session cannot widen its own limit. WebSocket is not covered. See [Configuration → Passive-wait concurrency](config.md#uaiptransport--passive-wait-concurrency-off-by-default).
- **`UAIP.Core.Artifacts.GetArtifact`, a new read-only command that returns an artifact's stored content over any transport** (`UAIPArtifacts` module): until now an AI client saw an artifact's body only when the MCP Bridge fetched it over HTTP and inlined it into the tool response. Given an `ArtifactId` the calling session owns, it returns text-shaped artifacts (`Json` / `Log` / `Report` / `Bundle`) in `Content`, paged with `Offset` / `MaxBytes` (default and ceiling 65536) and `NextOffset`, each window ending on a character boundary; binary artifacts (`Image` / `Trace`) come back as metadata and `TotalBytes` only. An artifact of another session reports `NotFound`, indistinguishable from one that never existed. Requires the new `ArtifactContentRead` capability (DefaultAllow). MCP Bridge 1.2.0 relies on this command rather than inlining artifact bodies by default. See [Commands Reference — UAIP.Core.Artifacts](commands.md#uaipcoreartifacts).
- **`ListDirtyPackages`, a new read-only command that lists the packages holding unsaved changes** (`UAIPEditorAssets` module): a pre-flight check before saving. It reads the same source the editor-wide save consults, so its list and the set `SaveAllPackages` would write are the same. Requires `EditorInspect`. See [Commands Reference — UAIP.Editor.Assets](commands.md#uaipeditorassets).
- **`GetUndoHistory`, a new read-only command that reads the undo history without executing anything** (`UAIPEditorWorkspace` module): returns how many steps can be undone or redone, and their names. It requires only `EditorInspect`, so seeing the queue is not tied to being allowed to change it. See [Commands Reference — UAIP.Editor.Workspace](commands.md#uaipeditorworkspace).

**Changed**

- **Optional-plugin integrations (Niagara, StateTree, the Toolset bridge, GroomAsset, ControlRig Dynamics/Physics, DataValidation, the WebSocket transport, Python execution, and dozens more) now load at run time instead of needing to be compiled in by hand — this is what makes them work in the Fab-distributed Pro build for the first time** (`UAIPCore` module, plus every optional-plugin module): before this release, a prebuilt UAIP binary — the Fab Pro build, or any build produced by the plugin's own packaging pipeline — had every optional-plugin integration compiled out entirely, because the packaging pipeline's synthetic host project never declares any plugin in a `.uproject`. Building from source worked, but only after adding an explicit `{ "Name": "<Plugin>", "Enabled": true }` entry to the project's `.uproject` and rebuilding — including for plugins the engine already enables by default, such as `ControlRigPhysics` and `DataValidation`. **On an editor target, none of that is required anymore**: UAIP now compiles in support for an optional plugin whenever the plugin exists in the engine being built against, whatever the project's `.uproject` says, and decides at run time — after the plugin manager reports the plugin enabled or disabled, once its original loading phase completes — whether to actually load the integration. To use one: enable the plugin (**Edit → Plugins**, or add it to `.uproject` yourself) and restart the editor; no rebuild. A disabled plugin's commands are simply not registered and return `CommandNotFound`, now with a hint (see below); a plugin genuinely absent from this engine version reports `UnavailableDetail: "OptionalPluginDisabled"` instead, which restarting does not fix. `UAIP.Core.ListIntegrations` (new, see above) reports every integration's state and remedy in one call. Packaged games are unchanged: which integrations end up in the package is still decided by what the project's `.uproject` has enabled at packaging time. See [Safety & Capabilities → Capability reference](safety.md#capability-reference) and the updated `OptionalPluginDisabled` guidance throughout this reference and the [API Reference](api.md#65-command-availability-fields).
- **`CommandNotFound` now explains itself when the missing command belongs to an optional integration that has not loaded** (`UAIPCore` module): instead of the bare `Command not found: <name>`, the response now names the integration, its current state, and what to do about it — enable a disabled plugin and restart the editor, wait for one still loading, or, when the plugin genuinely is not compiled into this engine version, that no toggle fixes it — and points to `UAIP.Core.ListIntegrations` for the full picture. A name that matches no known integration (a typo) still gets the original bare message, unchanged. Applies wherever `CommandNotFound` can be returned: `uaip_execute`, `uaip_describe_command`, and the deferred-execution submit path.
- ⚠️ **Breaking — the Python extension no longer scans and runs a project's `Scripts/UAIPCommands/**/*.py` files at editor startup unless the `PythonExtensionReload` capability (DefaultDenied) is already granted** (`UAIPEditorPythonExtension` module): the startup scan used to execute those scripts directly, bypassing the Dispatcher entirely, so `PythonCommandExecution` / `PythonExtensionReload` never actually gated it — a project that could place a file under that folder could run code at every editor launch regardless of capability configuration. The startup scan is now gated exactly like `ReloadPythonCommands`: if `PythonExtensionReload` is off (the default), no Python commands are registered at startup, and a line is logged saying the startup scan was held back; granting the capability and calling `UAIP.Editor.PythonExtension.ReloadPythonCommands` (or restarting with the capability already granted) loads them. **If a source-built project has been relying on `Scripts/UAIPCommands` being scanned automatically on launch, grant `PythonExtensionReload` in `Config/DefaultUAIP.ini`** to keep that behavior. See [Safety & Capabilities → Script execution](safety.md#script-execution).
- ⚠️ **Breaking — every one of the 413 Toolset bridge commands has had its delegation target checked: 16 now work, and 8 now say they cannot run**: what a bridge delegates to is two independent string literals on our side — (toolset name, tool name) — and the engine's ToolsetRegistry resolves them with **one exact lookup** and no fallback (no prefix match, no suffix match). A misspelling therefore compiled, linked, registered and **listed itself as available**, failing only when someone called it. Checking all 413 statically established the full set: **398 reach a target, 15 do not**. **The 16 that now work**: all 7 of `Toolset.Editor.DataflowAgent.*` (the toolset name was unqualified, and `ConnectDataflowPins` / `DisconnectDataflowPins` also had the wrong tool name), 8 of `Toolset.Editor.SlateInspector.*` (`SnapshotUI`, `ListObservers`, `ClickWidget`, `HoverWidget`, `InputText`, `PressKey`, `SetComboSelection`, `FillForm` — unqualified in the same way), and `Toolset.Editor.Niagara.GetAssetDiscoveryInfo` (it delegated to `NiagaraToolset_Info` while the tool lives on `NiagaraToolset_Assets`). **The 8 that now report `Available: false` with `UnavailableDetail: "DelegationTargetMissing"`**: `Toolset.Editor.GameFeatures.CreateGameFeaturePlugin`, `FindGameFeatureData`, `GetActions`; `Toolset.Editor.UMG.ReparentWidgetBlueprint`, `SetWidgetAsVariable`; `Toolset.Editor.Niagara.GetSystemInfo`; `Toolset.Editor.SlateInspector.ObserveWidget`, `UnobserveWidget`. **Enabling a plugin does not resolve these eight** — the function they delegate to exists on no engine version, so the `OptionalPluginDisabled` they used to report was misleading advice. Five of the eight have a native command that performs the same operation, named in the response's `UnavailableDetailMessage`; the other three (`FindGameFeatureData`, `GetActions`, `UnobserveWidget`) have no native counterpart. **This check is now a permanent static audit, so a misspelled target cannot be committed again.**
- ⚠️ **Breaking — `AddBlueprintComponent` and `DuplicateBlueprintComponent` now require `ComponentCustomTypeEdit` for a project- or plugin-defined component class** (`UAIPEditorBlueprint` module): until now, adding or duplicating a component on a Blueprint went through on `BlueprintComponentEdit` alone, whatever class it was. Every route that **increases how many instances of a component class exist** now asks the same question under the same capability name — the new instance-side `AddActorComponent` and these two Blueprint / SCS commands. A class declared by `/Script/Engine` or `/Script/LiveLinkComponents` is still admitted on the command's own capability, exactly as before. **Any other class — your project's own C++ components, another plugin's (engine plugins such as Niagara included), and Blueprint-generated component classes — is now refused with `CapabilityNotAvailable` naming `ComponentCustomTypeEdit`** unless the session holds it. **How to restore the previous behaviour**: add `+AllowedCapabilities=ComponentCustomTypeEdit` to `[UAIP.SafetyPolicy]` in `Config/DefaultUAIP.ini`. Both routes were gated at once because gating only the new one would leave the Blueprint route as a way to reach the same class. **Delete, rename, reparent and property writes are unaffected** — none of them increases the number of instances of a class, so none of them is gated by this. Because the requirement is decided from the class while the command runs, it appears in none of the three commands' declared `RequiredCapabilities`; `ListActorComponents` reports per class what an add would take, `QueryCapabilities` with `IncludeUnavailable: true` lists the name in its catalog, and the refusal itself always names what is missing. See [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md#level--actor--property-editing) and [Commands — Components — SCS](commands.md#components--scs-8).
- **Commands whose artifact is the response now fail when that artifact cannot be saved** (**371 commands**, all modules): a failed save of a screenshot, state dump or report used to still return `Success`, and the response even **carried one artifact entry with nothing behind it**. An AI agent reads that as "succeeded, one artifact available" and only discovers otherwise when it tries to fetch it. Fewer than 1% of the call sites checked the save result; the rest did not. This is not limited to captures and dumps — it covers **query commands at large**, which assign nothing to `Result` and carry their answer only in the artifact, so a failed save left them returning "success with an empty body". ⚠️ **Behavior change — 371 commands that used to return `Success` can now return `ExecutionFailed`.** This only happens when a save genuinely fails (disk conditions, a rejected path, a source file that disappeared), so it is not observable in normal operation. The error message **distinguishes whether retrying is worth anything**: a failed write, a failed directory creation and a failed copy say "retrying may succeed", while a rejected path or a source outside the project directory say "retrying with the same input will not help". Commands whose artifact is only a supporting record still return success, but they **no longer advertise an artifact that does not exist** — a failed one simply does not appear in `Artifacts[]`. When a diagnostic artifact fails to save on a response that already failed for another reason, the original `ErrorCode` and `ErrorMessage` are left untouched.
- ⚠️ **Behavior change — a command blocked by `DeniedCommands` is now hidden from `ListCommands` and reported as unavailable** (`UAIPCore` module): such a command used to stay in the default `ListCommands` response and — incorrectly — report `Available: true`, although calling it was always refused. It is now omitted like any other unavailable command, counted under `HiddenReasons.DeniedCommand`, and reported with `Available: false` and `UnavailableReason: "DeniedCommand"` when listed with `IncludeUnavailable: true` or described with `DescribeCommand`. Calling it is refused exactly as before. A caller that looked for a denied command in the default list, or read `Available: true` as permission, must now pass `IncludeUnavailable: true`.
- ⚠️ **Behavior change — a running scenario now excludes single commands, and the other way round** (`UAIPTransportHTTP` and `UAIPScenario` modules, every transport): scenarios used to exclude only other scenarios, so a single command from any session could interleave in the middle of one. Now, while a scenario runs, a single command from any session — including the one that submitted the scenario — is refused with `TooManyRequests` (passive waits excepted when `AllowConcurrentPassiveWaits` is on), and submitting a scenario is refused with `TooManyRequests` while a non-passive command is still executing, even one whose own response has already timed out. If the same command stays in flight far longer than a scenario's wall-clock cap would allow, the refusal becomes `InternalError` with a message saying the editor needs a restart, so that a wedged handler is told apart from ordinary congestion. A workflow that slipped a single command in while its scenario ran must issue it before or after the scenario instead. See [Scenario Execution → Exclusivity with single commands](scenario.md#exclusivity-with-single-commands).

> **Why none of this is MAJOR.** This release changes behavior in a few places, but none of them crosses the line into a SemVer MAJOR change. Each case is either a fix to a contract that was documented but never actually reachable, a specification change to a command marked `Experimental`, or a fix to behavior that contradicted its own declared contract. A `Stable` command whose declared, reachable behavior actually changes is always MAJOR, regardless of the reason for the change.

- **Command parameters are now type-checked against the schema the handler declares** (`UAIPCore` module). Until now only unknown properties and missing required fields were enforced, and the value itself went straight to `FJsonObject::TryGet*Field`, whose implicit conversions accept almost anything — `"yes"` reached a handler as the boolean `true`, and `"abc"` reached it as the integer `0`. Every supplied value is now checked against the declared `Type` before dispatch, and a mismatch is rejected with `InvalidParams` naming the expected and actual type. Array elements are checked against `Items.Type` one level deep, `Integer` additionally requires no fractional part, JSON `null` is treated as "not supplied" so handler-side defaulting is unaffected, and an unrecognised type keyword imposes no constraint. ⚠️ **Behavior change — check your calls if you relied on the old coercion** — most visibly in scenarios, where the type-preservation rule means a numeric `Variables` entry spliced into a String parameter arrives as a number: `"Variables": { "ExpectedHp": 100 }` feeding `AssertActorProperty`'s `ExpectedValue` must now be written `"ExpectedHp": "100"`.
- **`RunAutomationSpec` now declares and validates its parameter schema, and rejects unknown parameters** (`UAIPEditorExecution` module): the command's `GetParameterSchema()` returned nothing, so `SpecName` and any other misspelled or invented key passed straight through unchecked and was silently ignored. It now declares the same four keys `RunAutomationTest` reads through the shared parser — `TestName` (required), `TimeoutSec`, `RunAllMatching`, `MaxMatchingTests` — with `AdditionalProperties: false`. ⚠️ **Behavior change**: a call naming `SpecName` (or any other key outside this set) now fails with `InvalidParams` instead of being silently ignored. `RunAllMatching` and `MaxMatchingTests` are accepted for schema symmetry with `RunAutomationTest`, but have no effect on a Spec run — `Execute()` always forces a single exact-name match, regardless of what either parameter is set to. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Execution](commands.md#uaipeditorexecution).
- **`BeginSandboxSession`, `EndSandboxSession` and `CommitSandboxChanges` now declare and validate their parameter schemas, and reject unknown parameters** (`UAIPEditorSandbox` module): all three read parameters but published no schema, so `DescribeCommand` answered `null` and a misspelled key was silently ignored. They now declare `SandboxName` / `Description`, `DiscardPending`, and `AssetPaths` (array of strings) respectively, with `AdditionalProperties: false`. ⚠️ **Behavior change**: a call naming any other key now fails with `InvalidParams` instead of being silently ignored. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Sandbox](commands.md#uaipeditorsandbox).
- **`FocusEditorTab` and `CloseEditorTab` renamed their `TabId` parameter to `AssetPath`** (`UAIPEditorWorkspace` module). ⚠️ **Behavior change — check your calls to these two commands** — `TabId` is now rejected with `InvalidParams`. Both commands have always resolved their target as an asset path, so the old name invited callers to pass the `ActiveTabId` that `DumpEditorState` reports, which is a Slate layout identifier and could never resolve. The old name is rejected rather than accepted as an alias, because a silently ignored `TabId` would fail later and further from the cause. `CaptureEditorTabImage` keeps its `TabId`, which really is a layout identifier.
- **`CaptureActiveWindowImage` no longer requires the editor to be the foreground application** (`UAIPEditorObservation` module): the command previously failed whenever another application was in front, because Slate reports no active top-level window until Windows sends a real activation message — an editor launched by the MCP bridge while the user works elsewhere never gets one. Screenshots do not read the screen, so the background editor was always capturable; the command now falls back to the editor's main window and reports which one the image is of in `Result.CapturedWindow` (`"ActiveWindow"` / `"MainWindow"`). The fallback only reaches the main window, so a floating asset editor or modal dialog still needs `CaptureEditorTabImage` while another application is in front.
- **`CaptureEditorTabImage` now resolves tabs owned by a nested tab manager** (`UAIPEditorObservation` module): identifiers such as `LevelEditorViewport` name minor tabs belonging to a major tab's own manager, so the previous global-only lookup rejected most of the values `DumpEditorState` reports as `ActiveTabId`. Its documentation already told callers to pass that value straight through; the lookup now matches. Note that capturing brings the named tab to the front of its stack — a tab behind a sibling is not drawn and would come back empty — so capturing changes which tab the user sees.
- **Scenario template pointers now have a settled grammar, and a failed resolution says why** (`UAIPScenario` module). A step could not splice a bridge command's response into a later step: the tokenizer rewrote every `.` in the pointer to `/`, so a body written with a leading slash became `//refPath` and missed, while the guides disagreed about which spelling was right. The pointer body now has two readings, told apart by the first character — a body opening with `/` is taken as a literal JSON pointer, which is the only way to reach a key containing `.`; any other body keeps treating `.` and `/` alike, so **every pointer that resolved before still resolves**. `${StepName.Data}` with no pointer now addresses the whole object. ⚠️ **Behavior change for pointers that used to hit by accident** — object keys are now matched case-sensitively (`Data.refPath` and `Data.RefPath` are no longer the same key), array indices accept only `0` or a non-zero digit run (`01`, `+1`, `1abc` are rejected), `~1` and `~0` now decode as RFC 6901 escapes (a key containing a literal `~` must be written `~0`), and an object or array referenced from inside a larger string now fails instead of quietly becoming an empty string. Resolution failures now report which kind of failure occurred — a misspelled step name, a missing variable, a pointer that did not match, or a malformed escape each get their own sentence — and the error code moved from `ExecutionFailed` to **`InvalidParams`**, which also means these failures are **no longer retried** by `RetryCount`. Values spliced from step data are size-checked, objects and arrays are copied rather than shared with the recorded step result, and the whole params tree now resolves against a byte budget charged on expansion growth.
- **Command parameters across five modules renamed to their PascalCase spelling — and they now actually reach the handler** (`UAIPEditorSequencer`, `UAIPEditorUIAutomation`, `UAIPEditorPhysics`, `UAIPEditorGameFeatures`, `UAIPEditorEnhancedInput` modules). Each of these parameters was advertised in the command schema under one spelling while the parser read a different one, so **neither spelling ever worked**: the advertised name passed schema validation and was then silently dropped, leaving the parameter at its default, and the spelling the parser wanted was rejected up front with `InvalidParams` because these schemas declare `AdditionalProperties: false`. The schema now advertises exactly the name the parser reads, so these parameters are usable for the first time. Renamed:
  - `UAIP.Editor.Sequencer` — `bDeferNotification` → `DeferNotification` on `AddKeyframe`, `AddSection`, `AddTrack`, `BindActor`, `RemoveKeyframe`, `RemoveSection`, `RemoveTrack`, `SetKeyframeValue`, `SetPlaybackRange`, `UnbindActor`; `bRemoveAll` → `RemoveAll` on `RemoveKeyframe`.
  - `UAIP.Editor.UIAutomation` — `bStopOnFirstError` → `StopOnFirstError` on `FillForm`.
  - `UAIP.Editor.Physics` — `offset_x` / `offset_y` / `offset_z` → `OffsetX` / `OffsetY` / `OffsetZ` on `SetBox`, `SetCapsule`, `SetSphere`; `constraint_mode` → `ConstraintMode` on `AddConstraint`.
  - `UAIP.Editor.GameFeatures` — `plugin_name` → `PluginName` and `output_path` → `OutputPath` on `CreateGameFeaturePlugin`; `plugin_name` → `PluginName` and `plugin_url` → `PluginUrl` on `GetGameFeatureInfo`; `filter_state` → `FilterState` on `ListGameFeatures`.
  - `UAIP.Editor.EnhancedInput` — `path_filter` → `PathFilter` on `ListInputActions` and `ListMappingContexts`.

  ⚠️ **Two things change for existing callers, and neither is a loss of working behaviour.** First, a call that sent one of the old names is now rejected with `InvalidParams` instead of being accepted and ignored — the value it sent never had any effect, so replacing the spelling is the whole fix. Second, a call that sends the new name now gets the documented behaviour rather than the default the command silently used before: `DeferNotification` really defers the Sequencer notification, `RemoveAll` really selects the removal scope, `StopOnFirstError` really stops `FillForm` at the first failing field, `OffsetX` / `OffsetY` / `OffsetZ` are really applied to the shape, and the GameFeatures / EnhancedInput filters really filter. `UAIP.Editor.Niagara` is not part of this entry; its parameters are covered by the Niagara entry immediately below.

- **Every `UAIP.Editor.Niagara` parameter and artifact field is now spelled in PascalCase** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module). The module carried two parallel sets of key constants — one PascalCase, used by the observation and topology commands and by every field the Niagara inspector writes into an artifact, and one snake_case, used by the editing commands added later — so the same logical field reached callers under two different names depending on which command produced it. The clearest symptom: `CompileNiagaraSystem` wrote its result as `compile_state` / `compile_errors` while `GetSystemCompileState` wrote the very same two fields as `CompileState` / `CompileErrors`, so no single key read both. The two sets are now one, and every key in the module is PascalCase. Each name below applies wherever it appears in the module — as a command parameter, as an artifact field, or as a field of an object inside the `Parameters` / `Variables` arrays:
  - Target identity — `asset_path` → `AssetPath`, `asset_type` → `AssetType`, `asset_name` → `AssetName`, `emitter_id` → `EmitterId`, `emitter_name` → `EmitterName`, `emitter_asset_path` → `EmitterAssetPath`, `module_id` → `ModuleId`, `module_name` → `ModuleName`, `script_path` → `ScriptPath`, `script_name` → `ScriptName`, `script_usage` → `ScriptUsage`, `stack_usage` → `StackUsage`, `input_name` → `InputName`, `parameter_name` → `ParameterName`, `enum_path` → `EnumPath`.
  - Values and options — `insert_index` → `InsertIndex`, `new_index` → `NewIndex`, `new_name` → `NewName`, `timeout_seconds` → `TimeoutSeconds`, `type_name` → `TypeName`, `type_path` → `TypePath`, `default_value` → `DefaultValue`, `variable_name` → `VariableName`, `variable_names` → `VariableNames`, `renderer_class_path` → `RendererClassPath`, `renderer_index` → `RendererIndex`, `local_space` → `LocalSpace`, `warmup_time` → `WarmupTime`.
  - Blueprint wrapper — `actor_identifier` → `ActorIdentifier`, `component_name` → `ComponentName`, `target_blueprint_path` → `TargetBlueprintPath`, `parent_class_path` → `ParentClassPath`, `blueprint_path` → `BlueprintPath`.
  - Artifact-only fields — `compile_state` → `CompileState`, `compile_errors` → `CompileErrors`, `requires_followup_compile` → `RequiresFollowupCompile`, `parsed_as_default` → `ParsedAsDefault`, `parsed_as_default_array` → `ParsedAsDefaultArray`, `variable_count` → `VariableCount`.
  - Differing only in case, and therefore still accepted under the old spelling — `kind` → `Kind`, `value` → `Value`, `force` → `Force`, `enabled` → `Enabled`, `name` → `Name`, `properties` → `Properties`, `parameters` → `Parameters`, `variables` → `Variables`, `determinism` → `Determinism`, `success` → `Success`.

  ⚠️ **Behavior change for every name in the first four groups.** JSON key matching in the engine is case-insensitive, which is why the last group keeps working under its old spelling — but the other names differ by more than case, so a request that sends the old one is now rejected with `InvalidParams` (these schemas declare `AdditionalProperties: false`), and code that reads an artifact by an old field name finds nothing. Replacing the spelling is the whole fix: no command, capability, value, or behaviour changed beyond the names. The `Toolset.Editor.Niagara.*` bridge commands are untouched — they forward the engine's own Toolset schema and keep its spelling. This also fixes `AddSetParametersModule` advertising the item schema for its `Parameters` array under `items` rather than `Items`, which kept `uaip_describe_command` from showing callers the shape of an entry.

- **`ReadOnly` no longer refuses `ShutdownEditor` and `RestartEditor`** (`UAIPCore` and `UAIPEditorWorkspace` modules). `ReadOnly=True` rejected every mutating command, and the two editor lifecycle commands are mutating — so an editor launched under that policy could not be stopped or restarted through UAIP at all, and because the policy is read at launch, editing the ini did not reach the running process either. What was left was closing the editor by hand or killing the process, which is what the documentation tells operators not to do and what leaves the plugin DLL locked. Both commands are now exempt from the read-only refusal and are reported as `Available: true` under it; nothing else about `ReadOnly` changes, their optional `SaveAll` is still governed by `DisableSave`, and no other command declares the exemption. ⚠️ **`EditorLifecycle` is DefaultAllow**, so on an otherwise untouched configuration `ReadOnly=True` now leaves the AI able to stop and restart the editor. Closing that again is explicit: `+DeniedCapabilities=EditorLifecycle`, or `+DeniedCommands=` naming the two commands. See [Safety & Capabilities → ReadOnly and the editor lifecycle commands](safety.md#readonly-and-the-editor-lifecycle-commands).
- **The CLI transport's auto-generated `SessionId` is a GUID rather than the process id** (`UAIPTransportCLI` module). A one-shot CLI call that passes no `SessionId` used to get one built from the process id; it now goes through the same anonymous-session path as the HTTP / WebSocket / MCP transports and gets `CLI-Anonymous-<guid>`. That path is what marks the session anonymous internally, which in turn lets commands requiring an explicit session (the asset audit job commands, the trace analysis ones) refuse it rather than accept a session no one can name again. ⚠️ The artifact folder for such a run is named after the generated id, so a script that located a one-shot run's artifacts by process id no longer finds them. Passing an explicit `SessionId` is unaffected — and is the better fix.
- **Corrected guidance: a scenario cannot guarantee that a cleanup step runs** (documentation and the MCP Bridge guides shipped with the plugin; no behavioural change). `AbortOnFailure` has always been evaluated on the step that **failed**, never on the steps after it — but the guides described the opposite, telling callers that marking a trailing cleanup step `AbortOnFailure: false` would make it run across an earlier failure. It does not: when an earlier step fails with the default `true`, the scenario finalizes there, and the cleanup step is never dispatched and never appears in `StepResults`. ⚠️ **Scenarios written against the earlier guidance have not been running their cleanup step** — a trailing `StopPIE`, `StopTrace`, close-tab or delete-temp-asset step was silently skipped whenever an earlier step failed. The guides now describe what actually works (mark **every** step before the cleanup block `false`, or clean up from the client's own loop after the scenario returns) and name the cases no in-scenario pattern can cover at all: a submit-time rejection, the whole-scenario watchdog (which responds with an empty `StepResults` while the runner keeps going in the background), and an editor crash. See [Scenario Execution → Failure handling and cleanup](scenario.md#failure-handling-and-cleanup).
- **`SetAssetProperty` / `SetActorProperty` / `SetWorldSetting` / `SetDataTableRow` / `SetBlueprintDefault` / `SetProjectSetting` now return `InvalidParams` instead of `ExecutionFailed` when the write itself is rejected** (`UAIPEditorProperty` module). The rejection is always a function of the caller-supplied `PropertyPath` / `Value` against the resolved target — an unresolved path, a property type `SetPropertyValue` refuses to write, or a value that does not parse in UE text-import format for that property — never an environmental or runtime failure, so `InvalidParams` is the accurate code. This also brings the six commands in line with `SetBlueprintComponentProperty` (`UAIPEditorBlueprint` module), which already used `InvalidParams` for the identical failure. ⚠️ **Behavior change** — callers that pattern-matched `ExecutionFailed` for this specific failure on these six commands must switch to `InvalidParams`; inside a scenario, `RetryCount` no longer retries it either, since `InvalidParams` is never retried. Every other `ExecutionFailed` case on these commands (editor unavailable, target resolution failure, `PreEdit` rejection) is unchanged. **Superseded in part by the reference / struct / container entry above**: a write refused for want of `PropertyReferenceEdit` or `PropertyStructuredEdit` now reports `CapabilityNotAvailable` and names the missing capability, since asking for a permission and fixing a malformed request are different things to be told.
- **`AddGraphNode`'s module allowlist is now an exact match, and `GetAvailableRigVMUnitStructs` reports `SchemaVersion: 3`** (`UAIPEditorControlRig` module). A `StructPath`'s owning package used to be accepted whenever it merely **began with** `/Script/ControlRig`, `/Script/AnimationCore`, or `/Script/Engine` — which meant unrelated packages that only shared the prefix, such as `/Script/ControlRigDeveloper`, `/Script/ControlRigEditor`, or `/Script/EngineMessages`, were also accepted, contrary to what the allowlist was ever meant to cover. It is now compared for equality against seven modules — `/Script/ControlRig`, `/Script/ControlRigDynamics`, `/Script/ControlRigPhysics`, `/Script/ControlRigSpline`, `/Script/ControlRigModules`, `/Script/AnimationCore`, `/Script/Engine` — naming the ControlRig sibling modules explicitly rather than reaching them only as a side effect of the old prefix match, so the physics and dynamics rig units stay addable under the stricter rule. `GetAvailableRigVMUnitStructs` now reports `SchemaVersion: 3`: each entry carries `Admission` (`Allowed` / `RequiresCapabilities` / `NotAddable` / `CompatibilityUnknownUntilAuthorized`) sourced from the very same policy `AddGraphNode` validates against, plus `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities`, plus `TotalCount` / `ReturnedCount` / `Truncated` on the response; `ClassPath` and `ClassDisplayName` are unchanged. See the next entry below for what replaced the `Addable` / `NotAddableReason` fields this response used to carry. ⚠️ **Breaking — a call that relied on the old prefix match stops working**: any `StructPath` whose package only shared a prefix with an allowed module — not the module itself — is now rejected with `ModuleNotAllowed`. There is no opt-out; the allowlist was never meant to reach those packages.
- **`ListAddableComponentTypes`, `CanAddComponent` and `GetAvailableRigVMUnitStructs` report `Admission` instead of a plain `Addable` flag** (`UAIPEditorControlRig` module). Each entry — a component struct from the first two commands, a unit struct from the third — carries `Admission`, one of `Allowed` (usable by this session right now), `RequiresCapabilities` (usable once an operator grants the capabilities named in `MissingCapabilities`, itself a subset of `RequiredCapabilities`), `NotAddable` (refused for a structural reason no capability grant fixes — wrong base type, `Deprecated`, or `Hidden`), or `CompatibilityUnknownUntilAuthorized` (the session already holds `ControlRigCustomTypeEdit` for this struct, but the engine's own compatibility check has not run for it — a listing must not run a struct's own code for a session it has not authorized). `CanAddComponent` additionally asks the engine — `URigHierarchy::CanAddComponent` — but only for a type whose `Admission` is `Allowed` or `CompatibilityUnknownUntilAuthorized`; its answer lands in `CanAdd` / `FailureReason` rather than overwriting `Admission`, so a type reported `NotAddable` or `RequiresCapabilities` is answered without the engine ever running that type's own code. ⚠️ **Breaking — the boolean `Addable` and the string `NotAddableReason` are gone from all three commands.** A caller that branched on either must switch to `Admission`; `CanAddComponent`'s `ComponentStructPath` is now `InvalidParams` when malformed instead of a `CanAdd: false` response, matching `AddComponent`. See [Commands — `UAIP.Editor.ControlRig`](commands.md#uaipeditorcontrolrig).
- **`DumpOpenTabs` no longer outputs a `TabId` field** (`UAIPEditorObservation` module). ⚠️ **Behavior change — check code that reads `DumpOpenTabs` output** — the same value is still available under the `AssetPath` field of the same object; switch to reading that instead. Despite its name, this field held the asset's path rather than a tab identifier, so passing it to a command that expects a Slate layout identifier (such as `CaptureEditorTabImage`'s `TabId`) never resolved. The value was always duplicated under `AssetPath` on the same object, so removing the misleading field loses no information.
- **`FocusEditorTab` and `CloseEditorTab` now return a JSON artifact on success** (`UAIPEditorWorkspace` module), bringing them in line with the project-wide rule that every command returns artifacts. The artifact carries the resolved asset path (and, when a subgraph tab was targeted, its name). This is additive only: the existing response fields are unchanged, and a failed call still returns no artifact, exactly as before.
- **The text value parameter of the 18 property-writing commands moved from required to optional** (`UAIPEditorProperty`, `UAIPEditorBlueprint`, `UAIPEditorSequencer`, `UAIPEditorSound`, `UAIPEditorSoundCue`, `UAIPEditorDataflow`, `UAIPEditorAnimSequence`, `UAIPEditorMotionMatching`, `UAIPEditorSubsonic` modules). `Value` — `PropertyValue` on `SetSectionProperty` — was declared `Required` in each command's schema, and a JSON schema has no way to express "exactly one of `Value` or `ValueJson`"; leaving it required would have made `Remove`, `Clear` and every `ValueJson` write unreachable, because schema validation would have rejected the request before the command ever saw it. ⚠️ **The set of requests that get refused is unchanged, but the refusal moved and its wording changed.** A `Replace` supplying no value at all used to be rejected by schema validation with a message naming `Value` as a required parameter; it is now rejected by the command itself with `Invalid parameters: operation 'Replace' requires either 'Value' or 'ValueJson'.`, or with `Invalid parameters: operation 'Replace' requires 'Value'.` on the three Subsonic commands, which have no `ValueJson`. `ErrorCode` is `InvalidParams` either way, so the only callers that need a change are those matching on the message text.
- **The Subsonic property setters no longer load the asset a reference names** (`UAIPEditorSubsonic` module). `SetSubsonicEventActionProperty`, `SetSubsonicActionModifierProperty` and `SetSubsonicParameterValue` resolved an object path by loading the asset when it was not already in memory, which made a property write pull content in as a side effect — the one thing every other property write path in UAIP promises not to do. They now resolve without loading. ⚠️ **Behavior change — a write that used to succeed can now fail**: the target asset must already be loaded, so open it in the editor first and re-issue. "Does not exist" and "exists but is not loaded" are deliberately reported identically, because resolving without loading cannot tell them apart; the two separate refusal messages these commands used to return have been merged into one that says so and tells the caller to open the asset first.
- **A property path's intermediate segments are now checked, and `VisibleAnywhere` is refused where it used to be accepted** (`UAIPEditorShared` plus the modules whose write paths use it). Writing `Struct.Inner` checked only the last segment, so naming an inner member walked straight past a `Struct` that was read-only, deprecated, disabled for this context, or absent from the Details panel — and the same bypass worked through a container index (`Array[0].Inner`). All four conditions are now checked on every intermediate segment, with the container itself inspected when an index is in the way. Separately, several write paths gated only on the **presence** of `CPF_Edit`, which UHT sets on a `VisibleAnywhere` property **together with** `CPF_EditConst` — so a read-only property passed the gate. Those paths now call the same shared check as everything else, in `UAIP.Editor.BehaviorTree`, `UAIP.Editor.EQS`, `UAIP.Editor.StateTree`, `UAIP.Editor.Conversation`, `UAIP.Editor.PCG`, `UAIP.Editor.MotionMatching` and `UAIP.Editor.UMG`; `UAIP.Editor.WorldConditions`, whose write path had no flag check at all, gained one. Values that hold an asset path (`FSoftObjectPath`, `FSoftClassPath`, `FTopLevelAssetPath`) are no longer decomposable either — their internal members cannot be addressed, because writing them piecemeal would be a way past the reference gate. ⚠️ **Behavior change — a write that used to succeed through one of these routes is now refused.** Reads are deliberately unaffected: these are write-path checks, and where one had leaked into a read path it was taken back out — `GetSlotProperties` (`UAIP.Editor.UMG`) now enumerates every slot property unconditionally, and an `EditInstanceOnly` slot property is writable on an instance as a result.
- **Reference, struct and container writes are now gated on the paths that were missing the check** (`UAIPEditorUMG`, `UAIPEditorEnhancedInput`, `UAIPEditorStateTree`, `UAIPEditorBlueprint`, `UAIPEditorControlRig`, `UAIPEditorNiagara`, `UAIPEditorPCG`, `UAIPEditorBehaviorTree`, `UAIPEditorWorldPartition`, `UAIPEditorEngineManagement` modules, over the shared write path in `UAIPEditorShared`). [Safety & Capabilities](safety.md) has always said `PropertyReferenceEdit` and `PropertyStructuredEdit` gate reference / struct / container writes in **every** domain that writes properties; a set of write paths never consulted them, so those writes went through unchecked — including values that only parsed part-way, which were committed as fragments. Those paths now run the same check as everywhere else. ⚠️ **Behavior change — a write that used to succeed can now be refused with `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming what to grant.** The names are `WidgetSlotReferenceEdit` / `EnhancedInputReferenceEdit` / `StateTreeParameterReferenceEdit` on the three commands that own one, `NiagaraReferenceEdit` on a reference-typed parameter default passed to `AddSetParameterEntry` / `AddSetParametersModule` (see the entry below), `PropertyReferenceEdit` elsewhere, and `PropertyStructuredEdit` for the struct / container half in all cases — add them under `[UAIP.SafetyPolicy]` with `+AllowedCapabilities=` and the write goes through again. Two consequences worth planning for: **composite values often have to be sent as JSON now**, because a reference or a struct outside the built-in value catalogue cannot be written through the engine text path — read the property first and use the `WriteInputForm` it reports; and **a few commands take a value only as text**, so on those a reference or container is refused no matter what the session holds. That last group is `SetSettingsValues`, `AddBlueprintVariable` / `SetBlueprintVariableDefault` and ControlRig's `AddVariable`; a general property command reaches the same object and accepts the structured form — `SetProjectSetting` for the first, `SetBlueprintDefault` for the rest.
- **Four more read paths report what a write would take** (`UAIPEditorUMG`, `UAIPEditorEnhancedInput`, `UAIPEditorStateTree`, `UAIPEditorWorldConditions` modules). `GetSlotProperties`, `GetInputActionInfo`, `GetMappingContextInfo`, `GetStateTreeParameters` and `GetWorldConditionInfo` now carry the same `WriteRequirements` report the shared read path already attaches elsewhere — `RequiredCapabilities`, `HeldCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities`, `IsWritable` / `RefusalReason` and `WriteInputForm` — so a caller can find out that a value has to go as JSON, or that a permission is missing, before attempting the write rather than after being refused. The first three expose it as a `PropertyWriteRequirements` map keyed by property name, beside the flat value map they already return; the last two nest a `WriteRequirements` object in each entry. The value maps themselves are unchanged. `WriteInputForm` gains a third value, `None`, for a property no input form can write. `GetWorldConditionInfo` reports an empty `RequiredCapabilities` throughout, which is the honest answer for that path: it accepts no reference or container at all, so there is no permission to ask for.
- **AnimNotify and MotionMatching's schema commands now report write requirements through the same shared vocabulary as the five paths above** (`UAIPEditorAnimSequence`, `UAIPEditorMotionMatching` modules). `GetAnimNotifyClassSchema` and `GetPoseSearchChannelClassSchema` used to report per-property writability under their own field names — `bIsWritable`, `NotWritableReason`, `WriteInputForm` and `RequiredCapabilities` — sitting directly on the property entry. Both now nest a `WriteRequirements` object in each property entry instead, reusing the exact `IsWritable` / `RefusalReason` / `WriteInputForm` / `RequiredCapabilities` fields the paths above use, plus `HeldCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities`, which neither command reported before. Judging whether a given property is writable is still each module's own call, not the shared resolver's — `GetPoseSearchChannelClassSchema` in particular keeps refusing a reference-bearing type outright, capability or not, exactly as before — only how the answer is rendered is shared now. ⚠️ **Behavior change — check any code that reads these two commands by the old field names**, which are gone from the property entry. No information is lost: the write path itself did not change, and neither did which properties are writable — reading `WriteRequirements.IsWritable` / `.RefusalReason` / `.WriteInputForm` / `.RequiredCapabilities` gets the same judgement as before, and `.HeldCapabilities` / `.MissingCapabilities` are additions, not renamed fields. With this, every UAIP read command that reports what a write would take uses one shape.
- **`GetAnimNotifyProperty` reports what a write would take as well** (`UAIPEditorAnimSequence` module). The read-only counterpart of `SetAnimNotifyProperty` carried no permission guidance at all, so the only way to learn what a write needed was to call `GetAnimNotifyClassSchema` separately or to attempt the write and read the refusal. It now attaches the same nested `WriteRequirements` object `GetAnimNotifyClassSchema` does, with the same field names (`RequiredCapabilities`, `HeldCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities`, `IsWritable` / `RefusalReason`, `WriteInputForm`) and the same nesting — inside each entry of `Properties` for an all-properties read, and directly in `Data` beside `PropertyName` / `Value` for a single-property read. The values it reports are resolved against the actual notify instance addressed by `NotifyGuid`, which is the object `SetAnimNotifyProperty` writes to, rather than against the class default. Nothing about the property values themselves changed, so a caller that only reads values is unaffected.

- **A reference-typed Niagara parameter default is now written instead of silently dropped** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module). `AddSetParameterEntry` and `AddSetParametersModule` accept a `DefaultValue`, but when the parameter's type was a data interface or an object reference the value was **quietly ignored** — the request succeeded and the parameter was created with no default at all. Such a value is now supplied as an object path and stored in the dedicated data-interface / object slot of `FNiagaraVariant`, where the reference is retained properly rather than packed into the byte payload the other parameter types use. ⚠️ **Behavior change — a request that used to succeed while dropping the value can now be refused**: `CapabilityNotAvailable` naming `NiagaraReferenceEdit` when the session does not hold it, `NotFound` when the object path names an asset nothing has loaded (a write never loads an asset as a side effect), and `InvalidParams` when the object is not of the parameter's class. With the capability granted and the target loaded, the write succeeds and the reference is kept. **Leaving `DefaultValue` unset behaves exactly as before** and needs no extra permission. ⚠️ Only **data interface** types are reachable in practice: an ordinary object type such as `UTexture2D` is refused at the parameter type-name stage by the type allowlist, before the capability is consulted. Note also that no read command returns a Set Parameters entry's default value, so the write cannot yet be confirmed by reading it back.

- **`GetInputActionInfo` and `GetMappingContextInfo` return the values they used to skip** (`UAIPEditorEnhancedInput` module). Their `Params` map dropped object / weak / soft / interface references, arrays, maps and sets outright and flattened every struct into one text string.
