It now serialises them, mirroring the dispatch order the write path uses to read a value back: references export as the `Class'/Path'` form the setters accept, arrays and sets become JSON arrays, maps become arrays of `{"Key": …, "Value": …}`, and a struct outside the value catalogue becomes a JSON object keyed by member name — which also makes a reference buried inside a struct visible. Scalars and catalogue structs keep the exact shape and spelling they had. Without this the round trip did not close: these properties could be written but never read back.
- **`GetStateTreeParameters` names the parameter types it used to call `Unknown`** (`UAIPEditorStateTree` module): `ParameterType` now reports enum, struct, object, soft-object, class and soft-class parameters, plus the remaining integer widths, instead of reporting every non-scalar as `Unknown`. `Unknown` is now reserved for a bag type that genuinely has no name.
- **`SetMultipleWorldConditionProperties` is all-or-nothing, and its success payload changed shape** (`UAIPEditorWorldConditions` module). Every edit is staged and checked — domain gate, text import, part-way-parse detection, validation — and only when all of them pass is a transaction opened and the batch committed; one failure fails the request with a top-level `ErrorCode` / `ErrorMessage` and writes nothing. ⚠️ **Behavior change — the per-edit `Results[]` and `AllSucceeded` are gone**, because with no partial application there is no per-edit outcome to report. The success payload is `AppliedEdits` (the `{ConditionIndex, SubPropertyName}` pairs committed) and `SkippedEdits` (the same shape, for names matching no property — an unmatched name still does not fail the batch). Update any caller reading the old fields.
- **Three toolset bridge commands ask for the same capability as the native command they mirror** (`UAIPEditorNiagara`, `UAIPEditorPCG` modules). A bridge asking for a different name meant an operator who had closed the native capability could still reach the same write through the bridge, which defeats the point of closing it. ⚠️ **Behavior change — check which capabilities you grant**: `Toolset.Editor.Niagara.SetRendererData` now requires `NiagaraStackEdit` (was `NiagaraEmitterEdit`), and `Toolset.Editor.PCG.SetGraphInstanceParams` and `Toolset.Editor.PCG.ResetGraphInstanceParams` now require `PCGGraphEdit` (was `PCGGraphExecute`). **A session granted only `PCGGraphExecute`, or only `NiagaraEmitterEdit`, loses access to the corresponding command.** Both replacements are DefaultDenied too, so a default configuration is unaffected. Separately, and stated here because the capability parity invites the opposite assumption: **a bridge write does not run UAIP's value checks.** `SetRendererData`, `SetGraphParams`, `SetGraphInstanceParams` and `ResetGraphInstanceParams` write inside the engine's toolset, where the type gate, the range / NaN checks, the part-way-parse check and the all-or-nothing batching cannot be inserted — use the native command when you want them. This is now stated in each of those commands' `DescribeCommand` description as well.
- **A refused write no longer leaves a mark on the asset** (`UAIPEditorUMG`, `UAIPEditorStateTree`, `UAIPEditorControlRig`, `UAIPEditorBehaviorTree`, `UAIPEditorPCG` modules). Several paths called `Modify()` before deciding whether the write was allowed, and cancelling the transaction afterwards does not undo that — `FScopedTransaction::Cancel()` discards the undo record but leaves the package dirty. The check now runs before `Modify()`, and where the failure could only happen during application (the PCG parameter bags) the edits are applied to a copy and written back only once all of them succeed — which also removes the partial application those two commands used to leave behind.
- **Other refusals that used to be silent successes.** `SetRendererData` (`UAIPEditorNiagara`) answers `NotFound` when the emitter has no renderer of the named class instead of writing to whichever renderer came first, fails the whole request with `PolicyViolation` on a property its write path cannot handle instead of ignoring it, and raises `PostEditChangeProperty` on success so the editor shows the change immediately. `SetBehaviorTreeNodeProperty` (`UAIPEditorBehaviorTree`) refuses a Blackboard key selector write when the tree has no Blackboard asset assigned, rather than storing a name and a type that do not match. `AddBlueprintVariable` (`UAIPEditorBlueprint`) and ControlRig's `AddVariable` (`UAIPEditorControlRig`) validate the initial value once the variable's type has resolved and **remove the variable again** if it is refused, instead of leaving one behind with an empty value. Setting a variable default with compilation skipped no longer skips validation along with it.
- **A value that cannot be interpreted is refused with `InvalidParams`, which scenarios do not retry** (`UAIPEditorShared` and the modules on the shared write path). The shared text import now checks that the whole value was consumed, so a partly-parsed value is refused instead of being committed as a fragment; the refusal uses `InvalidParams` rather than `ExecutionFailed`, because `RetryCount` retries `ExecutionFailed` and re-sending a value the property cannot interpret produces the same answer every time. Out-of-range and NaN values on the PCG parameter commands moved to `InvalidParams` for the same reason. Fix the value and send it again rather than retrying.
- **`AddAnimGraphNode` refuses project- and plugin-defined node classes with `CapabilityNotAvailable` instead of `PolicyViolation`, and no longer loads an unresolved `NodeClass`** (`UAIPEditorAnimBlueprint` module). A class outside the domain's three built-in modules used to be refused outright with `PolicyViolation`, which named nothing an operator could grant, because nothing could be granted. It is now `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming `AnimBlueprintCustomTypeEdit` (see **Added** above). ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** A caller that branched on `PolicyViolation` for a project- or plugin-defined `NodeClass` must switch to `CapabilityNotAvailable`; the old code meant "granting a permission would not have helped", so keeping it would describe a permission system that did not yet exist. The eight internal- and sub-graph-only node kinds AnimGraph refuses at the root regardless of capability keep returning `PolicyViolation` — this change does not touch them. Separately, `NodeClass` must already be loaded — `AddAnimGraphNode` no longer loads it as a side effect on demand, and an unresolvable class path is refused with `NotFound`, whatever class it names. `AnimBlueprintCustomTypeEdit` is also re-checked on every later operation against an existing node of a gated class, not only on `AddAnimGraphNode` — editing, connecting, disconnecting, compiling, and deleting it all now consult the same capability. ⚠️ **Deleting and disconnecting such a node used to be entirely ungated** — before this change, only `AddAnimGraphNode` checked the type, so removing or disconnecting a node went through regardless of what it was. A class that fails to load, or that a later engine version dropped support for, cannot be re-added, but that alone does not block deleting or disconnecting an existing node of it, as long as the session holds whatever capability that type requires. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **`AddMaterialNode` refuses project-defined and custom-HLSL expression classes with `CapabilityNotAvailable` instead of `PolicyViolation` / `InvalidParams`, and no longer loads an unresolved `ExpressionClass`** (`UAIPEditorMaterial` module). A class outside the engine's built-in modules used to be refused outright with `PolicyViolation`, and `UMaterialExpressionCustom` / `UMaterialExpressionCustomOutput` (and their subclasses) were refused the same way regardless of module — neither refusal named anything an operator could grant, because nothing could be granted. Both are now `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming `MaterialCustomTypeEdit` and/or `MaterialCustomNodeEdit` (see **Added** above). ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** A caller that branched on `PolicyViolation` / `InvalidParams` for these classes must switch to `CapabilityNotAvailable`; the old codes meant "granting a permission would not have helped", so keeping them would describe a permission system that did not yet exist. Separately, `ExpressionClass` must already be loaded — `AddMaterialNode` no longer loads it as a side effect on demand, and an unresolvable class path is refused with `NotFound`, whatever class it names. These capabilities are also re-checked on every later operation against an existing node of a gated class, not only on `AddMaterialNode` — editing, connecting, disconnecting, reparenting, and deleting it all consult the same capabilities; compiling only re-checks `MaterialCustomNodeEdit` for whichever dangerous kinds an asset actually contains, not `MaterialCustomTypeEdit` for merely being project-defined, so an asset containing an ordinary custom expression is not blocked from compiling. ⚠️ **Deleting and disconnecting such a node used to be entirely ungated** — before this change, only `AddMaterialNode` checked the expression class, so removing or disconnecting a node went through regardless of what it was. A class that fails to load, or that a later engine version dropped support for, cannot be re-added, but that alone does not block deleting or disconnecting an existing node of it, as long as the session holds whatever capability that type requires. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **The generic property-write commands now additionally judge the class being written into a hard reference or an instanced-subobject property against the target domain's own type-admission policy, when that domain has registered one** (`UAIPEditorShared` module, new `FReferencedTypeAdmissionRegistry`; `UAIPEditorMaterial` module, first registrant). Before this change, holding `PropertyEdit` + `PropertyReferenceEdit` was enough to write any already-loaded class into a hard reference or instanced-subobject property through `SetAssetProperty` and the other same-shaped commands, whatever that class was — a project's own gated `UMaterialExpression` subclass reached a Material asset this way without ever being asked for `MaterialCustomTypeEdit` / `MaterialCustomNodeEdit`, sidestepping the exact check `AddMaterialNode` enforces. Constructing an instanced subobject from a caller-named class is judged the same way. ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** A session that already holds `PropertyReferenceEdit` and writes a Material expression class through a generic property command is now refused `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming the missing Material capability, unless it holds that too. A session holding neither `PropertyEdit` nor `PropertyReferenceEdit` sees no change — it could never reach a reference write on this path in the first place. Only Material has registered a judge so far; the remaining domains' own gated classes are not yet caught by this path. See [Commands — Writing references, structs and containers](commands.md#writing-references-structs-and-containers).
- **`AddGraphNode` and `AddComponent` refuse project- and plugin-defined structs with `CapabilityNotAvailable` instead of `ModuleNotAllowed`, no longer load an unresolved `StructPath`, and every later operation on an existing node or component of such a struct is now gated too** (`UAIPEditorControlRig` module). A struct outside the domain's seven modules used to be refused outright, which named nothing an operator could grant, because nothing could be granted. It is now `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming `ControlRigCustomTypeEdit` (see **Added** above). ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** A caller that branched on the old refusal for a project- or plugin-defined struct must switch to `CapabilityNotAvailable`; the old outcome meant "granting a permission would not have helped", so keeping it would describe a permission system that did not yet exist. Structs marked `Deprecated` or `Hidden`, and structs that do not derive from the expected base, are unaffected and stay refused whatever is held. ⚠️ **Deleting, connecting, disconnecting, duplicating, moving and editing pin values used to be entirely ungated** — before this change, only the two `Add*` commands consulted the type policy, so every other route into the same rig went through regardless of what the node or component was. `RemoveGraphNode`, `ConnectControlRigPins`, `DisconnectControlRigPins`, `SetPinValue`, `ResetPinValue`, `SetNodePosition`, `DuplicateNode`, `RemoveComponent`, `RenameComponent`, `ReparentComponent`, `SetComponentContent` and the typed `Set*` commands of the Dynamics and Physics domains now all require `ControlRigCustomTypeEdit` when the struct involved is project- or plugin-defined. `CompileControlRig` is not affected: this domain has no dangerous kind of type, so compiling a rig that merely contains such a struct still requires nothing extra. Separately, `StructPath` must already be loaded — neither command loads it as a side effect any more, and a struct that is not in memory is refused with `NotFound` rather than `InvalidParams`; a malformed path is still `InvalidParams`. The order of two refusals also changed: a request that gets both the struct and the asset path wrong is now answered about the asset, because the struct is judged with the asset in hand. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **The four trigger / modifier setters refuse project- and plugin-defined classes with `CapabilityNotAvailable` instead of `PolicyViolation` / `InvalidParams`, accept a full object path, never load a class, and every route that discards such an instance is now gated too** (`UAIPEditorEnhancedInput` module). A `Class` outside `/Script/EnhancedInput` used to be refused outright, which named nothing an operator could grant, because nothing could be granted — and no spelling reached one in the first place. `SetInputMappingTrigger`, `SetInputMappingModifier`, `SetInputActionTrigger` and `SetInputActionModifier` now answer `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming `EnhancedInputCustomTypeEdit` (see **Added** above). ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** A caller that branched on `PolicyViolation` / `InvalidParams` for such a class must switch to `CapabilityNotAvailable`; the old codes meant "granting a permission would not have helped", so keeping them would describe a permission system that did not yet exist. The two chord trigger kinds are unaffected and keep returning `PolicyViolation`. ⚠️ **A class of the wrong family is now refused.** The previous condition never asked what a class derived from, so naming an Enhanced Input class that is neither a trigger nor a modifier — an `InputAction`, say — passed it and reached the call that instanced it into the list; that combination is now `InvalidParams`. Separately, `Class` must already be loaded — nothing is loaded to decide whether it was allowed to be, and a name that resolves to nothing is refused with `NotFound` rather than `InvalidParams`. ⚠️ **Removing a mapping entry and deleting an asset used to be entirely ungated** — before this change, only the four setters consulted the type policy, so `RemoveInputMapping`, `DeleteInputAction` and `DeleteMappingContext` went through regardless of what the target held. All three now require `EnhancedInputCustomTypeEdit` when the mapping entry or asset holds an instance of a class from outside the module. A target holding only Enhanced Input classes — including a chord trigger set up from the editor — is removed and deleted exactly as before, with nothing extra required. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **`AddStateTask` / `AddGlobalTask` / `AddEvaluator` / `AddStateEnterCondition` and the four `Set*Property` commands refuse project- and plugin-defined classes with `CapabilityNotAvailable` instead of `PolicyViolation`, and no longer load an unresolved class path** (`UAIPEditorStateTree` module). A class or struct outside the domain's three built-in modules used to be refused outright with `PolicyViolation`, which named nothing an operator could grant, because nothing could be granted. It is now `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming `StateTreeCustomTypeEdit` (see **Added** above). ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** A caller that branched on `PolicyViolation` for such a class must switch to `CapabilityNotAvailable`. A class path nothing currently loaded answers to is now `NotFound`; a class that is abstract, deprecated, superseded, or not a StateTree node at all is `InvalidParams`. Class resolution has always used `FindObject` rather than a loading fallback; that has not changed. `StateTreeCustomTypeEdit` is also re-checked on every later operation against an existing node of a gated class, not only on the `Add*` commands — editing and deleting it now consult the same capability. ⚠️ **Deleting such a node used to be entirely ungated** — `RemoveStateTask`, `RemoveGlobalTask`, `RemoveEvaluator` and `RemoveStateEnterCondition` previously consulted no policy at all, so removing a node went through regardless of its class. A class that fails to load, or that a later engine version dropped support for, cannot be re-added, but that alone does not block deleting an existing node of it, as long as the session holds whatever capability that type requires. Compiling is never gated by it — `CompileStateTree` asks nothing about the classes an asset merely contains. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **`GetAvailableTaskClasses`, `GetAvailableConditionClasses` and `GetAvailableEvaluatorClasses` now run their native struct hierarchy through the same policy as the add commands, and no longer cap each of the two hierarchies independently** (`UAIPEditorStateTree` module). All three used to run the `FStateTree*Base` native struct hierarchy through no policy at all, and ran the Blueprint class hierarchy through it only for `GetAvailableTaskClasses` — so a project's own struct-based task was listed exactly as freely as an engine-shipped one, and a Blueprint-backed condition or evaluator was listed without regard to where it came from either. All three now answer both hierarchies from the same policy the add commands validate against, report `Admission` (`Allowed` / `RequiresCapabilities` / `NotAddable` / `CompatibilityUnknownUntilAuthorized`) plus `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities` per entry, and include classes that cannot be placed at all as `NotAddable` rather than dropping them. ⚠️ **The 200-entry cap now applies once to the two hierarchies combined** rather than once per hierarchy — previously a response could in principle carry up to 400 entries (200 native struct + 200 Blueprint class); it can now carry at most 200. `SchemaVersion` moved to `2` for all three commands.
- **`GetStateTasks`, `GetStateEnterConditions`, `GetStateTreeGlobalTasks` and `GetStateTreeEvaluators` no longer mask a class this domain does not ship** (`UAIPEditorStateTree` module). All four used to replace `TaskClass` / `ConditionClass` / `EvaluatorClass` with `"<redacted>"` and set `MaskedDueToPolicy: true` for a node whose class the add path would refuse. All four now name the real class and attach `Admission` / `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities` instead. `MaskedDueToPolicy` stays in the response for compatibility but is now always `false`. This is unrelated to the redaction that still applies during a degraded (PIE) read, which is unaffected. ⚠️ Two unrelated bugs were fixed in the same change: a global task, evaluator or enter condition node backed by a native struct previously bypassed the node class policy entirely; and an evaluator or enter condition node was previously judged as though it were a Task node, which could report a `WrongBaseType`-style refusal for a class the add path accepts. `SchemaVersion` moved to `2` for all four commands.
- **`AddWorldCondition` and `ReplaceWorldCondition` refuse project- and plugin-defined condition structs with `CapabilityNotAvailable` instead of `PolicyViolation`, and no longer load an unresolved `ConditionClass` / `NewConditionClass`** (`UAIPEditorWorldConditions` module). A struct outside `/Script/WorldConditions` used to be refused outright with `PolicyViolation`, which named nothing an operator could grant, because nothing could be granted. It is now `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming `WorldConditionsCustomTypeEdit` (see **Added** above). ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** A caller that branched on `PolicyViolation` for such a struct must switch to `CapabilityNotAvailable`; the old code meant "granting a permission would not have helped", so keeping it would describe a permission system that did not yet exist. A class path nothing currently loaded answers to is now `NotFound`; this domain never fell back to loading an unresolved class as a side effect. `WorldConditionsCustomTypeEdit` is also re-checked on every later operation against an existing condition of a gated struct, not only on `AddWorldCondition` / `ReplaceWorldCondition` — `SetWorldConditionOperator`, `SetWorldConditionExpressionDepth`, `SetWorldConditionProperty` and `SetMultipleWorldConditionProperties` now consult it too. ⚠️ **Deleting, moving and duplicating a condition, and writing a property already sitting in one, used to be entirely ungated** — before this change, only `AddWorldCondition` / `ReplaceWorldCondition` consulted the struct policy, and a property write checked only the property's own kind, never the struct declaring it. `RemoveWorldCondition`, `ClearWorldConditionQuery`, `MoveWorldCondition`, `DuplicateWorldCondition`, `SetWorldConditionOperator`, `SetWorldConditionExpressionDepth`, `SetWorldConditionProperty` and `SetMultipleWorldConditionProperties` now all require `WorldConditionsCustomTypeEdit` when the condition acted on, or the property's declaring struct, is project- or plugin-defined. A struct that cannot be instantiated at all stays refused regardless of any capability held, but only for the commands that would place a fresh one of it — removing an existing one is unaffected once the capability is held. `ListWorldConditionClasses` no longer filters by module alone; it answers from the same policy `AddWorldCondition` validates against, reports `Admission` plus `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities` per entry, and includes structs that cannot be placed at all as `NotAddable` instead of silently dropping them. `GetWorldConditionInfo` gains the same three fields per condition — it never withheld the struct name itself, unlike the domains above. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **`AddTrack` refuses project- and plugin-defined track classes with `CapabilityNotAvailable`, no longer loads an unresolved `TrackClass`, and every later route into an existing track's structure is now gated too** (`UAIPEditorSequencer` module). A class outside the four modules this domain has always drawn tracks from is now `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming `SequencerCustomTypeEdit` (see **Added** above). `TrackClass` must already be loaded — `AddTrack` no longer loads it as a side effect, and an unresolvable class path is `InvalidParams`, naming the path, rather than reaching the capability check at all. ⚠️ **`RemoveTrack`, `AddSection`, `RemoveSection`, `AddKeyframe`, `RemoveKeyframe` and `SetKeyframeValue` used to be entirely ungated with respect to the owning track's class** — before this change, only `AddTrack` consulted the type policy, so every other route that names or acts on an existing track's class went through regardless of what that class was. All six now require `SequencerCustomTypeEdit` when the owning track's class is untrusted, **except** `RemoveTrack` for the three internal-only track classes (`UMovieSceneSubTrack`, `UMovieSceneCinematicShotTrack`, `UMovieSceneEventTrack`): those still cannot be added through `AddTrack` under any capability, but removing one already in the sequence needs no capability at all as long as it comes from a trusted module. `AddTrack`'s failure order also changed: it now resolves `TrackClass`, then loads the asset, then judges the class against the loaded sequence's own engine compatibility, where it previously judged the class before loading the asset — a request that gets both wrong now answers `NotFound` (the asset) rather than the type refusal. `GetAvailableSequencerTrackClasses`'s `SchemaVersion` is now `2`: each entry carries `Admission` plus `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities`, and the response carries `TotalCount` / `ReturnedCount` / `Truncated`. ⚠️ **The listing used to silently exclude every `Deprecated`, `NewerVersionExists` or `HideDropDown` class; it now includes them, describing what each would take rather than dropping them** — this matches what `AddTrack` itself has always done with the same class, rather than restricting the listing further than the add path does. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **`AddDecoration` and `RemoveDecoration` no longer resolve `DecorationClass` with `StaticLoadClass`, require `SequencerCustomTypeEdit` for a project- or plugin-defined class, and `GetCompatibleDecorations` / `GetDecorations` report `Admission` instead of a bare class-path array** (`UAIPEditorSequencer` module, `MovieSceneAnimMixer` decoration surface; applies to both the native commands and the `Toolset.Editor.SequencerAnimMixer.*` bridge versions of `AddDecoration` / `RemoveDecoration`). ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** Before this change, `DecorationClass` was resolved with `StaticLoadClass` against `UObject`, which force-loaded any class path that resolved and accepted it outright, with no base-type constraint and no origin check at all — a project's own decoration class was already reachable without any capability. `DecorationClass` is now resolved only against what this editor already has loaded — nothing is force-loaded any more, and an unresolvable path is `NotFound` where it used to be loaded on demand — and a class from outside the five modules that ship decorations (`/Script/MovieScene`, `/Script/MovieSceneTracks`, `/Script/MovieSceneAnimMixer`, `/Script/MovieScenePoseSearchTracks`, `/Script/MovieSceneMixedControlRig`) now requires `SequencerCustomTypeEdit`, naming it in the refusal on both `AddDecoration` and `RemoveDecoration`. An interface class, or one declaring an incompatible `ClassWithin`, is refused as `InvalidParams` regardless of any capability held — no such class could ever have been held by a decoration container in the first place. ⚠️ **Breaking — `CompatibleDecorations` and `Decorations` array elements are now objects, not plain class-path strings**: each carries `DecorationClass` plus `Admission` / `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities`, so a caller that read the array as `string[]` must switch to reading `DecorationClass` off each entry. This applies to the native `GetCompatibleDecorations` / `GetDecorations` only — the `Toolset.Editor.SequencerAnimMixer.*` bridge versions pass the external Toolset registry's response through unchanged and do not carry the new fields. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **`AddSoundCueNode` refuses project- and plugin-defined node classes with `CapabilityNotAvailable` instead of `PolicyViolation`, no longer loads an unresolved `SoundNodeClass`, and every later route into an existing node's class is now gated too** (`UAIPEditorSoundCue` module). A class outside `/Script/Engine` used to be refused outright with `PolicyViolation`, which named nothing an operator could grant, because nothing could be granted. It is now `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming `SoundCueCustomTypeEdit` (see **Added** above). ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** A caller that branched on `PolicyViolation` for such a class must switch to `CapabilityNotAvailable`. `SoundNodeClass` must already be loaded — `AddSoundCueNode` no longer loads it as a side effect, and an unresolvable class path is now `NotFound` rather than `InvalidParams`. ⚠️ **`RemoveSoundCueNode`, `ConnectSoundCuePins`, `DisconnectSoundCuePins` and `SetSoundCueNodeProperty` used to be entirely ungated with respect to the node's class** — before this change, only `AddSoundCueNode` consulted the class policy, so every other route that names or acts on an existing node's class went through regardless of what that class was. All four now require `SoundCueCustomTypeEdit` when the node's class, or an endpoint's class for `ConnectSoundCuePins` / `DisconnectSoundCuePins`, is untrusted; the graph's root output node owns no node class of its own and is never itself a reason to refuse. `CompileSoundCue` is unaffected — it was never gated by the node class policy and still is not, for the reason given under **Added** above. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **`AddPoseSearchSchemaChannel` refuses project- and plugin-defined channel classes with `CapabilityNotAvailable` instead of `PolicyViolation`, and `RemovePoseSearchSchemaChannel` / `MovePoseSearchSchemaChannel` / `SetPoseSearchSchemaChannelProperty` / `StartPoseSearchDatabaseIndexBuild` are now gated too** (`UAIPEditorMotionMatching` module). A channel class outside `/Script/PoseSearch` used to be refused outright with `PolicyViolation`, which named nothing an operator could grant, because nothing could be granted. It is now `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming `MotionMatchingCustomTypeEdit` (see **Added** above), and actually succeeds once the capability is granted, which the previous implementation did not. A class path nothing currently loaded answers to is still `NotFound`; this domain never fell back to loading an unresolved class as a side effect. ⚠️ **Removing a channel, moving one, writing a property already sitting in a schema, and starting an index build used to be entirely ungated with respect to the channel's class** — before this change, only `AddPoseSearchSchemaChannel` consulted the class policy. `RemovePoseSearchSchemaChannel` now requires `MotionMatchingCustomTypeEdit` when the removed channel's class, or any nested descendant's class, is untrusted — judged over the whole nested subtree a removal takes with it. `MovePoseSearchSchemaChannel` requires it only for the moved channel's own class, since nothing nested is disturbed by a reorder, and the check runs even when the requested position is a no-op, so a session cannot use one to probe a gated channel's position for free. `SetPoseSearchSchemaChannelProperty` requires it for the channel's own class and, independently, for the class declaring the property being written. `StartPoseSearchDatabaseIndexBuild` requires it for every distinct channel class the target schema holds, since the engine's own build pipeline runs all of them, not only the ones a caller named when they were added. `AddDefaultPoseSearchSchemaChannels` is unaffected — the two channels it adds are always the engine's own, with no caller-supplied class. ⚠️ **Breaking — `GetAvailablePoseSearchChannelClasses`'s response shape changed.** `NumClasses` (on both the artifact and `CommandResponse.Result`) is gone; `CommandResponse.Result` is no longer set at all. Read `TotalCount` / `ReturnedCount` / `Truncated` on the artifact instead. The listing no longer silently drops a class gated behind `MotionMatchingCustomTypeEdit` — each entry now carries `Admission` plus `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities`, and a new 500-entry cap applies after sorting by full class path, where none applied before. `GetPoseSearchSchemaInfo`'s `RawChannels[]` entries (nested ones included) gain the same three fields, additively. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **`AddConversationNode` and `AddConversationSubNode` refuse project- and plugin-defined node classes — including Blueprint generated ones — with `CapabilityNotAvailable` instead of an outright refusal, and `RemoveConversationNode` / `ConnectConversationNodes` / `DisconnectConversationNodes` / `SetConversationNodeProperty` are now gated too** (`UAIPEditorConversation` module). A node class outside `/Script/CommonConversationRuntime` used to be refused outright with no way to grant access, whether it was a project- or plugin-defined native class or a Blueprint generated subclass of one of the four Blueprintable bases the stock graph editor offers. It is now `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming `ConversationCustomTypeEdit` (see **Added** above), and actually succeeds once the capability is granted. A class path nothing currently loaded answers to is `NotFound`; this domain never fell back to loading an unresolved class as a side effect. ⚠️ **Removing a node, connecting or disconnecting one, and writing a property already sitting on one used to be entirely ungated with respect to the node's class** — before this change, only `AddConversationNode` / `AddConversationSubNode` consulted the class policy. `RemoveConversationNode` now requires `ConversationCustomTypeEdit` when the removed node's class, or any SubNode removed along with a top-level node, is untrusted. `ConnectConversationNodes` / `DisconnectConversationNodes` require it when either endpoint's class is untrusted. `SetConversationNodeProperty` requires it for the target node's own class, independently of the `PropertyReferenceEdit` / `PropertyStructuredEdit` check on the value being written. ⚠️ **`ListConversationNodeTypes` no longer filters by module alone, and no longer reports `NodeTypesTruncated`.** It answers from the same policy `AddConversationNode` / `AddConversationSubNode` validate against, reports `Admission` plus `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities` per entry ordered by `ClassPath`, and includes classes that cannot be placed at all as `NotAddable` instead of silently dropping them. The response's own `NodeTypesTruncated` field is gone; read the shared `TotalCount` / `ReturnedCount` / `Truncated` fields instead. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **`AddMetaSoundNode` refuses project- and plugin-defined node classes with `CapabilityNotAvailable` instead of `PolicyViolation`, and no longer loads an unresolved `ClassName`** (`UAIPEditorMetaSound` module). A class outside the four namespaces this domain draws nodes from used to be refused outright with `PolicyViolation`, which named nothing an operator could grant, because nothing could be granted. It is now `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming `MetaSoundCustomTypeEdit` (see **Added** above). ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** A caller that branched on `PolicyViolation` for such a class must switch to `CapabilityNotAvailable`. Two other codes moved with it: a `ClassName` nothing in the frontend registry answers to is now `NotFound` rather than `PolicyViolation`, and a deprecated class is now `InvalidParams` rather than `PolicyViolation`; a template class (`Reroute` and the like) keeps returning `PolicyViolation`. `MetaSoundCustomTypeEdit` is also re-checked on every later operation against an existing node of a gated class — connecting, disconnecting, editing its default value, and removing it all now consult the same capability. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **`GetAvailableMetaSoundNodeClasses` and `GetMetaSoundInfo` report `Admission` and no longer hide or mask anything** (`UAIPEditorMetaSound` module). `GetAvailableMetaSoundNodeClasses` used to drop every class outside the four namespaces from its response; it now returns the registry's external classes in full, ordered by `ClassName`, each with `Admission` / `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities` plus `TotalCount` / `ReturnedCount` / `Truncated` for a new 1000-entry cap. ⚠️ **`GetMetaSoundInfo` changed more sharply**: it used to report any node outside those namespaces as `ClassName: "<Unknown>"` and drop every edge touching one, so the returned topology did not match the asset. It now reports real class names and all edges, with `Admission` / `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities` attached to each node. A caller that treated `"<Unknown>"` as a sentinel, or relied on the edge list being pre-filtered, must stop. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **The four `Add*` BehaviorTree node commands and `AddBlackboardKey` refuse project- and plugin-defined types with `CapabilityNotAvailable` instead of `PolicyViolation`, and no longer load an unresolved class** (`UAIPEditorBehaviorTree` module). A type outside the modules this domain ships used to be refused outright with `PolicyViolation`, which named nothing an operator could grant, because nothing could be granted. It is now `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming every missing capability at once (see **Added** above). ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** A caller that branched on `PolicyViolation` for such a type must switch to `CapabilityNotAvailable`. Two other codes moved with it: a class path nothing currently loaded answers to is now `NotFound` rather than `InvalidParams`, and a class that is abstract, deprecated, superseded, or not a Behavior Tree node at all is now `InvalidParams` rather than `PolicyViolation`. Separately, nothing is loaded to resolve a class path any more — the four `Add*` commands and `AddBlackboardKey` used to fall back to loading the named class when it was not already in memory. ⚠️ **Deleting, editing, and removing such a type used to be entirely ungated** — before this change, only the `Add*` commands and `AddBlackboardKey` checked the type, so `RemoveBehaviorTreeNode`, `SetBehaviorTreeNodeProperty` and `RemoveBlackboardKey` went through regardless of what the target held. All three now require whatever capability the target's type asks for. Rebuilding a tree by adding or removing a main node is never gated by the classes the asset already holds. The four `GetAvailableBT*Classes` listings no longer filter by class flags alone; they answer from the same policy, report `Admission` plus `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities` per entry, and include classes that cannot be placed at all as `NotAddable` instead of silently dropping them. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **`AddEQSGenerator`, `AddEQSTest`, `SetEQSGeneratorProperty` and `SetEQSTestProperty` refuse project- and plugin-defined types with `CapabilityNotAvailable` instead of `PolicyViolation`, and no longer load an unresolved class** (`UAIPEditorEQS` module). A type outside `/Script/AIModule` used to be refused outright with `PolicyViolation`, which named nothing an operator could grant, because nothing could be granted. It is now `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming every missing capability at once (see **Added** above). ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** A caller that branched on `PolicyViolation` for such a type must switch to `CapabilityNotAvailable`. A class path nothing currently loaded answers to is now `NotFound` rather than `InvalidParams`; this domain never fell back to loading an unresolved class as a side effect, so nothing else changes there. ⚠️ **Deleting a Generator or Test, and toggling a Test's `bTestEnabled` flag, used to be entirely ungated** — before this change, only the `Add*` and `Set*` (for the property it writes to the node instance) commands checked the type, so `RemoveEQSGenerator`, `RemoveEQSTest`, and toggling `bTestEnabled` through `SetEQSTestProperty` all went through regardless of what the target held. All three now require whatever capability the target's type asks for. Rebuilding a query by adding or removing a Generator or Test is never gated by the classes the asset already holds. The two `GetAvailableEQS*Classes` listings no longer filter by module and class flags alone; they answer from the same policy, report `Admission` plus `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities` per entry, and include classes that cannot be placed at all as `NotAddable` instead of silently dropping them. `GetEQSQueryInfo` no longer withholds a class name during a degraded (PIE) read, reporting `Admission` / `RequiredCapabilities` / `MissingCapabilities` for every Option and Test instead. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **Three existing AnimMixer / ControlRig-track commands now expose the new child-track behavior** (`UAIPEditorSequencer` module). `GetCompatibleDecorations`, `GetDecorations`, `FindDecoration`, `AddDecoration` and `RemoveDecoration` gain an optional `Target` parameter (`"Layer"`, the default, or `"ChildTrack"`) that selects whether the decoration is resolved against the layer itself or against its child track — `FindDecoration` and `RemoveDecoration` report `NotFound` when `Target` is `"ChildTrack"` but the layer has no child track, and `AddDecoration`'s compatibility check runs against whichever container `Target` resolves to. `FindOrCreateControlRigTrack` gains an optional `IsLayered` (default `false`) that configures a newly created ControlRig as additive; it is ignored when an existing track is found. `GetControlRigTracks` adds `IsChildTrack` to every entry, with `LayerIndex` present only when it is `true`. All three parameters are optional and default to the prior behavior, so a caller that omits them is unaffected, and no new capability is required by any of the five commands.
- **Every write command in `UAIP.Editor.Skeleton` now answers `NotAllowed` instead of `NotFound` for a path that satisfies the `/Game/` prefix check but fails deeper validation** (`UAIPEditorSkeleton` module) — `..` path traversal, over 512 characters, an invisible control character, or a malformed package name. This is not a breaking change in the SemVer sense: such paths never succeeded before, and the previous error code for this specific edge case was never documented. Note it if your tooling matches `ErrorCode` for path rejections on `AddSocket`, `RemoveSocket`, `SetSocketTransform`, `AddVirtualBone`, `RemoveVirtualBone`, `SetSkeletalMeshMaterial`, `CreateBlendProfile` and `SetBlendProfileBoneScale`.
- **`CreateAsset` now admits the StateTree schema class it is given, and five modules of engine-shipped schemas need `StateTreeCustomTypeEdit`** (`UAIPEditorStateTree` module). `FactoryParams.SchemaClass` used to be accepted on two conditions alone — the path resolved, and the class derived from `UStateTreeSchema` — with nothing asked about where the class came from. It now goes through the same policy as this domain's node classes, so a schema class from outside `/Script/StateTreeModule`, `/Script/AIModule` and `/Script/GameplayStateTreeModule` requires `StateTreeCustomTypeEdit`. ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** This is not limited to a project's own classes: `/Script/MassAIBehavior`, `/Script/GameplayCameras`, `/Script/GameplayInteractionsModule`, `/Script/AvalancheTransition` and `/Script/UAFStateTree` all ship with the engine and all sit outside those three modules, so creating a StateTree against one of their schemas now needs the capability granted; `/Script/GameplayStateTreeModule` schemas are unaffected. Two smaller changes on the same path: the class is no longer loaded on demand — one that is not already in memory is refused instead of being loaded in order to decide whether the caller was allowed to name it — and the class-flag check now matches the editor's own schema picker, refusing an abstract, deprecated, superseded or dropdown-hidden class, where it previously refused only an abstract one and merely logged a warning for a deprecated one. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.StateTree](commands.md#uaipeditorstatetree).
- **`CreateAsset` now admits the Control Rig parent class it is given** (`UAIPEditorControlRig` module). `FactoryParams.ParentClass` used to be accepted on the factory's own conditions alone: the path resolved, the class derived from `UControlRig`, and — for the blueprint-based factory — it could serve as a Blueprint base. It now goes through the same origin rule as this domain's unit and component structs, so a class from outside the seven modules listed under `ControlRigCustomTypeEdit` requires that capability. ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** Every parent class the engine ships stays free, since they all live in those seven modules; what now needs the grant is a class of the project's own — a native `UControlRig` subclass declared by the project or a third-party plugin, and the generated class of a Control Rig Blueprint in the project (`/Game/….CR_Foo_C`). Omitting `ParentClass` still produces a plain `UControlRig` and asks for nothing. The class is no longer loaded on demand either: one that is not already in memory is refused rather than loaded to answer the question. Separately, the `ControlRigBlueprintCreate` check that guards the command as a whole is now asked of the calling session rather than of the process, so a session bound to a role that denies that capability is refused with `PolicyViolation` where it used to be allowed. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.ControlRig](commands.md#uaipeditorcontrolrig).
- **A missing capability on the asset-creation path is reported as `CapabilityNotAvailable`, naming the capability, the same as everywhere else** (`UAIPEditorAssets` module, and the two domains above). `FCreateAssetInterceptorContext` now carries an error classification alongside the success flag and message its three decision functions already return (`ReportErrorCode()` / `ConsumeReportedErrorCode()`), without changing any of their signatures — none of the interceptor's other 11 implementers had to be touched. `StateTreeCreateAssetInterceptor` and `ControlRigBlueprintCreateInterceptor` report the classification only when a capability check fails, so a missing `StateTreeCustomTypeEdit` / `ControlRigCustomTypeEdit` now arrives as `CapabilityNotAvailable`. A structural refusal on the same field (unresolved class, wrong base type, abstract, deprecated) is unaffected and stays `InvalidParams` — that is a statement about the parameter, not about a missing permission. `ShouldBlockCreation`'s own mapping is unchanged: ControlRig's `ControlRigBlueprintCreate` and MetaHuman's `MetaHumanAssetCreate` veto checks still answer `PolicyViolation` on a capability shortfall. See [Commands — Capability-gated custom types](commands.md#capability-gated-custom-types).
- **`GetAvailableStateTreeSchemaClasses` reports `SchemaVersion: 2`, per-entry admission, and the classes it used to hide** (`UAIPEditorStateTree` module). Each entry now carries `Admission` — `Allowed`, `RequiresCapabilities`, `NotAddable` or `CompatibilityUnknownUntilAuthorized` — plus `RequiredCapabilities` and `MissingCapabilities`, sourced from the same policy `CreateAsset` admits a named schema class through, and the response gains `TotalCount`, `ReturnedCount` and `Truncated`. ⚠️ **Behavior change — there is no compatibility window.** The listing is also wider than it was: classes the editor's own schema picker hides used to be dropped from it entirely, which left a caller unable to tell a class that does not exist from one that was withheld. They are listed now, saying what they would take, so a reader that treated a class's absence as proof it does not exist has to read `Admission` instead. Entries are ordered by `ClassPath`, so a response cut short by the entry cap always cuts the same tail. `ClassPath` and `ClassDisplayName` are unchanged.
- **`AddGraphNode` (Blueprint) now looks at every graph in the asset, matches `GraphName` case-insensitively, and refuses a name matching more than one graph instead of silently picking one** (`UAIPEditorBlueprint` module). It used to search only the event graph and function graphs, require an exact-case match, and — even within that narrow search — resolve an ambiguous name to whichever graph its internal traversal happened to visit first, a selection the caller had no way to predict or control. It now also reaches macros, interface-implementation graphs, and graphs nested inside a collapsed graph. ⚠️ **Behavior change — case-insensitive matching can turn a previously-successful call into a rejection.** A name differing only in case now matches; an asset holding two graphs whose names differ only in case, where a call used to succeed against the one graph matching the exact case given, now hits both and is rejected. A `GraphName` matching more than one graph answers `InvalidParams`, adds nothing, and reports every candidate's `GraphGuid` under `Result.MatchedGraphGuids` — pass one of those back as the new `GraphGuid` parameter (see **Added**) to target exactly one. Empty `GraphName` and the literal `"EventGraph"` are unaffected and never enter this check. Two acceptance checks run only for this command, since none of the other commands in this table decide where a *new* node goes: a graph that refuses edits at all (an event dispatcher's own definition graph, an interface asset's own graph, a math-expression graph, an editor-internal graph) answers `NotAllowed`; a graph that accepts edits but not this kind of node (a state machine's top-level graph, a Blend Space graph) answers `InvalidParams`. Naming a graph that does not exist now answers `NotFound` instead of `ExecutionFailed`. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Blueprint](commands.md#uaipeditorblueprint).
- **`AddLinkedAnimLayerNode` and `AddUAFGraphNodeToAnimBlueprint` now refuse a graph name matching more than one graph instead of resolving to one of them** (`UAIPEditorAnimBlueprint` module). Both answer `InvalidParams` and place nothing when `TargetGraph` matches more than one graph, reporting every candidate's `GraphGuid` under `Result.MatchedGraphGuids` — pass one of those back as the new `GraphGuid` parameter (see **Added**) to target exactly one. `AddLinkedAnimLayerNode`'s separate `LayerName` / `InterfacePath` resolution (which layer the placed node points at, distinct from which graph it is placed in) gets the same `InvalidParams` + `Result.MatchedGraphGuids` treatment for a name collision found within one set of layers — either the AnimBlueprint's own self-contained layers, or one already-implemented interface's own layer functions, never both at once. The pre-existing priority when resolving `TargetGraph` by name is unchanged: a self-contained layer is checked first and is never treated as ambiguous against a same-named interface layer, since the interface fallback only runs once the self search comes up empty. Naming a graph or layer that resolves to nothing anywhere still answers `NotFound`. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.AnimBlueprint](commands.md#uaipeditoranimblueprint).
- **Seven ControlRig commands that write to the asset now refuse a `GraphName` matching more than one RigVM model instead of picking one** (`UAIPEditorControlRig` module): `AddEventNode`, `AddVariableNode`, `DeleteGraph`, `DuplicateNode`, `ResetPinValue`, `SetNodePosition`, `SetPinValue`. `GraphName` accepts a prefix — matching more than one model this way is an everyday occurrence of that intentional shorthand, not an edge case — and until now every one of these commands silently used the first match its internal search order produced. They now answer `InvalidParams` without changing anything and report every candidate's full RigVM model name under `Result.MatchedFullGraphNames`; re-issue the call with one of those full names as `GraphName` to resolve to exactly one graph. No new parameter was added for this — ControlRig's own short-form lookup already yields an unambiguous full name to fall back to, unlike the `GraphGuid` contract Blueprint and AnimBlueprint use above. The eight read-only commands that resolve `GraphName` the same way — `FindNodes`, `GetConnectedPins`, `GetGraph`, `GetNodeInfo`, `GetNodePosition`, `GetPinValue`, `ListNodes`, `ListPins` — are unaffected and keep picking one of the matches, since inspecting the wrong graph has no side effect to correct. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.ControlRig](commands.md#uaipeditorcontrolrig).
- **`GetStackInputData` now reports each input's actual value, not just its value mode** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module): before this, a caller could learn that a module input was `Local`, `Linked`, `Dynamic`, `DataInterface` or `Expression`, but never what value it actually held — there was no way to read back a default `AddSetParameterEntry` had written, references included. Each input entry now carries a `Value` field alongside `ValueMode`: the literal for `Local`, the linked parameter's handle name for `Linked`, the dynamic input script's path for `Dynamic`, the custom expression text for `Expression`, and the data interface object's path for `DataInterface`. Each entry also gains a nested `WriteRequirements` object describing what changing it through `AddSetParameterEntry` would take. ⚠️ **Known limitation** — an input already holding a plain object reference (as opposed to a data interface) still reports `ValueMode: "Unknown"` and an empty `Value`, because `AddSetParameterEntry`'s own type allow-list has no route to create that kind of entry in the first place; the gap is in what can be written, not in what this command reads. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetSystemTopology`, `GetEmitterTopology`, `GetScriptStackTopology` and `GetModuleTopology` now report each module's `ModuleId`** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module): all four used to report a module by name only, and the id `GetStackInputData` requires to read a specific module's inputs was otherwise obtainable only as a side effect of `AddModule`'s own response — there was no way to read the modules of an asset nobody had used UAIP to build in the first place. Every module entry across the four reads now also carries `ModuleId`, in the same lowercase-hyphenated form `AddModule` returns and `GetStackInputData` accepts, so it can be passed straight from any of these reads into `GetStackInputData` without a write ever having happened. No existing field was removed. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetStackInputTopology`'s pre-5.8 fallback now returns the same `Name`/`Type`/`IsVisible`/`IsEditable`/`IsDynamic`/`DynamicInputChildren` shape as UE 5.8+, and now reads ordinary modules too** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module): the fallback branch used to return only `InputName` and `InputType`, so a caller reading this command's response needed a separate parsing path depending on the running engine version. Both branches now build through one shared helper and return the identical field set. The fallback also used to succeed only against a "Set Parameters" (assignment) module, failing the whole call with `ExecutionFailed` against anything else; it now also reads an ordinary function-call module directly from the graph, with no view model involved. How this fallback walks a multi-element `InputNameStack` changed too — see the entry below. ⚠️ **How much of `IsVisible`/`IsEditable`/`IsDynamic` is measured now depends on which kind of module matched.** Against a Set Parameters module, nothing changed: `IsVisible` and `IsEditable` are always reported as `true` — not from inspection (an assignment target carries no visibility/editability metadata of its own), but true because reaching this outcome already means the target's whole purpose is exposing its assignment targets for editing — and `IsDynamic` is always reported as `false` as a conservative default, since this path still cannot detect an actual dynamic input chain on an assignment target. Against an ordinary module, `IsDynamic` is now genuinely measured (`true` only when the input's override pin connects to a Dynamic Input script's function-call node, the same condition UE 5.8+ calls value mode `Dynamic`), and `IsVisible` is measured from the module's own current hidden-input set — but a `VisibleCondition` expression is not evaluated, so an input hidden only by a false condition is still reported visible. `IsEditable` reuses that same measured visibility and is only half-measured for the same reason: an `EditCondition` expression, which needs a sibling input's current value and cannot be resolved from the graph alone, is not evaluated either. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetStackInputSchema`, `GetModuleSchema` and `GetDynamicInputSchema` now also read ordinary modules on their pre-5.8 fallback, and that fallback's key names now match UE 5.8+** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module): each of these three commands used to succeed on UE 5.7 only against a "Set Parameters" (assignment) module — `GetStackInputSchema` and `GetDynamicInputSchema` failed the whole call with `ExecutionFailed` against anything else, and `GetModuleSchema` answered success with `Inputs` as an empty array instead of failing. All three now also read an ordinary function-call module directly from the graph, with no view model involved. `GetModuleSchema` now fails, rather than answering an empty array, when the target module has no valid script or graph to read. `GetDynamicInputSchema` still fails when the addressed input is not actually driven by a dynamic input — that refusal is intentional, since there is nothing to describe, not a bug. Separately, this fallback's JSON keys for an input entry changed from `InputName`/`InputType` to `Name`/`Type` — for `GetStackInputSchema`'s own entries, and for each element of the `Inputs` array `GetModuleSchema` and `GetDynamicInputSchema` return — so all three now use the same key names UE 5.8+ always did. `GetDynamicInputSchema`'s own Set Parameters branch also changed its envelope to match its own ordinary-module branch: it now returns `{ModuleAssetPath, Inputs: [{Name, Type}], Outputs: []}` instead of the previous bespoke `{InputName, InputType, Inputs: [], Outputs: []}`. **None of this touches `GetStackInputData`**, which keeps returning `InputName`/`InputType` on every engine version. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetStackInputTopology`, `GetStackInputSchema` and `GetDynamicInputSchema`'s pre-5.8 fallback now walks every element of `InputNameStack`, not only the first one** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module): all three used to consult only `InputNameStack[0]` and silently ignore the rest — a caller targeting a nested dynamic input's own input (e.g. `[TopLevelInput, NestedInput]`) got back `TopLevelInput`'s own data, reported as a normal success, with nothing indicating the nested element was never read. ⚠️ **Breaking change — this means a prior nested-stack answer was not merely incomplete, it described the wrong input while reporting success.** The fallback now walks the whole stack the same way UE 5.8+ already does: `InputNameStack[0]` resolves against the module's own input, and each further element resolves against the input on the Dynamic Input script instance currently driving the previous element. When an element along the way is not actually driven by a dynamic input, or its name does not resolve, the call now fails instead of silently answering a different input than the one addressed. `GetDynamicInputSchema` now describes the dynamic input instance driving the *resolved leaf*, not the one driving `InputNameStack[0]`. Against a **Set Parameters** (assignment) module specifically, a stack of more than one element is now refused outright — an assignment target carries no dynamic input of its own to walk into, so previously it silently answered the assignment target's own data regardless of how many elements were addressed. **UE 5.8+ is unaffected** — it already walked the full stack. A single-element `InputNameStack`, the common case, resolves exactly as before on every engine version. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetModuleTopology`'s pre-5.8 fallback returns a different shape, now honors `ScriptName`, and can target a system-level script** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module): the fallback used to walk a system view model across the whole emitter stack and answer `{ModuleName, ModuleAssetPath, Inputs: [{InputName, InputType}], Outputs: []}`, ignoring `ScriptName` entirely — the module was matched anywhere in the emitter's stacks, and an empty `EmitterName` never matched anything, so a system-level module could not be read through this branch at all. The fallback now reads the addressed script's graph directly, the same crash-safe way UE 5.8+ already does; `ScriptName` is genuinely consulted, and an empty `EmitterName` now resolves the system's own spawn/update script, matching what `GetEmitterTopology` and `GetScriptStackTopology` already accepted. ⚠️ **Breaking change — the response shape changed to match.** `ModuleAssetPath` is renamed to `ModuleScript`; `Outputs` is gone (a module's topology carries no output concept on either engine version, and the fallback's own `Outputs` was always an empty array, so nothing is actually lost); `Enabled` and `IsSetParametersModule` are new; each `Inputs[]` entry now carries `Name`/`Type`/`IsVisible`/`IsEditable`/`IsDynamic`/`DynamicInputChildren` instead of `InputName`/`InputType` alone. Module-name matching is also now case-sensitive, where it used to ignore case. `Enabled` is read off the module's own function-call node; a module whose sub-nodes were disabled independently of the node itself can report a different `Enabled` than UE 5.8+'s own stack view would for the same abnormal state. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetEmitterTopology`'s pre-5.8 fallback drops `IsEnabled`, wraps each script stack in the same `{ScriptName, Modules}` shape UE 5.8+ has always used, and each `Modules[]` entry gains five fields** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module): the fallback used to answer `{EmitterName, IsEnabled, EmitterSpawn: [{ModuleName}], EmitterUpdate: [...], ParticleSpawn: [...], ParticleUpdate: [...], Renderers: [{RendererClass}]}`, with `EmitterSpawn`/`EmitterUpdate`/`ParticleSpawn`/`ParticleUpdate` as bare arrays of modules each carrying only `ModuleName`. ⚠️ **Breaking change.** `IsEnabled` is gone — UE 5.8+'s own emitter topology never carried it either, so a field that only ever existed on one engine version was worse than no field at all; the emitter's enabled state stays readable on every engine version from `GetSystemTopology`'s per-emitter entries or from `GetEmitterData`. Each of the four script stacks is now wrapped as `{ScriptName, Modules}`, the same object shape UE 5.8+ has always returned — a caller that iterated one of them as a bare array of modules must switch to reading its `Modules` field. Each `Modules[]` entry grows from a single `ModuleName` field to the same six fields `GetModuleTopology` and `GetScriptStackTopology`'s module entries carry: `ModuleName`, `ModuleId`, `Enabled`, `IsSetParametersModule`, `ModuleScript`, and `Inputs`. Each `Renderers[]` entry also gains `RendererIndex` alongside the existing `RendererClass`, matching UE 5.8+ — pass the reported index back into `RemoveRenderer`. `RendererClass`'s own value also changes on this fallback, from a full class path to the bare class name UE 5.8+ has always reported (see the `GetRendererSchema` entry below). See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetScriptStackTopology`'s pre-5.8 fallback adds fields to each `Modules[]` entry; nothing is removed** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module): each entry used to carry only `ModuleName`. It now carries the same six fields `GetModuleTopology` and `GetEmitterTopology`'s module entries carry (see above): `ModuleName`, `ModuleId`, `Enabled`, `IsSetParametersModule`, `ModuleScript`, and `Inputs`. `ModuleName` keeps meaning what it always meant, and the surrounding `{ScriptName, Modules}` envelope is unchanged, so a caller that only ever read `ModuleName` off each entry keeps working unmodified. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetScriptStackTopology`'s pre-5.8 fallback now reports the canonical `ScriptName`, not the spelling the caller passed in** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module). ⚠️ **Breaking change.** The response's `ScriptName` field used to echo back the exact string passed as the `ScriptName` argument, whatever its spelling or case — a caller that requested `"particlespawn"` or even `"whatever"` got that same string reflected back, indistinguishable from a genuine answer. It now reports the same canonical name (`EmitterSpawnScript`, `ParticleUpdateScript`, `SystemSpawnScript`, and so on) that `GetEmitterTopology`'s own script-stack entries already reported on this fallback — the two commands used to answer differently for the same stack within the same engine session; they no longer do. **UE 5.8+ is unaffected** — it already reported the canonical name. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- ⚠️ **Breaking (UE 5.8+ only) — an enum-typed input's `Type` field now reports the enum's own name instead of `NiagaraInt32`** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module). The one function every `Type` field goes through used to test `FNiagaraTypeDefinition::GetStruct()` before `GetEnum()`; since Niagara stores an enum's value in an `int32` struct, `GetStruct()` never returned null for an enum type, so every enum-typed input was reported as `"NiagaraInt32"` with its enum identity lost. This is a fix, not a new capability: an enum-typed input now reports its own enum name (e.g. `"ENiagaraCoordinateSpace"`), while a struct-typed or class-typed input is unaffected. It touches `Type` wherever it appears on UE 5.8+: `GetModuleTopology`, `GetEmitterTopology` and `GetScriptStackTopology`'s per-input entries, `GetStackInputTopology`, `GetStackInputSchema`, `GetModuleSchema`, `GetDynamicInputSchema`, `GetDynamicInputSchemaFromAsset`, and `GetModuleSchemaFromAsset`. **UE 5.7 and earlier are unaffected** — their fallback already reported the enum's own name, so this change brings UE 5.8+ in line with what UE 5.7 and earlier always did. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetRendererSchema` now also accepts a bare class name for `RendererClassPath`, on every engine version** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module). It used to resolve only a full class path; it now also resolves the bare class name that `GetEmitterTopology`'s `Renderers[].RendererClass` and `GetRendererData` already report, closing a round trip that did not exist before. `RendererClassPath` keeps its name, and the previous path form still works. A bare name matching more than one renderer class is refused rather than guessed at. `AddRenderer` and `SetRendererData` accept the same two forms for their own `RendererClassPath` argument. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetDataInterfaceSchema` now also accepts a bare class name for `DataInterfaceClassPath`, on every engine version** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module). It used to resolve only a full class path; it now also resolves the bare class name this command's own `TypeName` field and a data-interface-typed `Type` field already report, so a type name read from either can be handed straight back in. `DataInterfaceClassPath` keeps its name, and the previous path form still works. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetModuleSchema` and `GetDynamicInputSchema`'s pre-5.8 fallback now reports the same four `Inputs[]` keys UE 5.8+ always has** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module). Each `Inputs[]` entry used to carry only `Name`/`Type`; it now also carries `Category` and `SupportsExpressions`. ⚠️ **Neither new field is actually measured on this fallback** — `Category` is always an empty string and `SupportsExpressions` is always `false`, since both are normally read off the stack view model and neither has an equivalent on the graph-only path this fallback uses. `Name` and `Type` are unaffected, and `Outputs[]` stays empty on this fallback as before. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetEmitterSchema`, `GetSystemSchema`, `GetRendererSchema` and `GetDataInterfaceSchema`'s pre-5.8 fallback now fails instead of always answering an empty `PropertySchema`** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module). ⚠️ **Breaking change.** All four used to report success on every call, with `PropertySchema` fixed at an empty string — reading as "this class has no editable properties", indistinguishable from the JSON Schema having genuinely never been built. `PropertySchema` is built by an engine API introduced in UE 5.8, and earlier engine versions have no equivalent to build it from. All four now answer `ExecutionFailed`, with `ErrorMessage` explaining that the command needs an engine API introduced in Unreal Engine 5.8 and is reported as a failure rather than as an empty result for exactly that reason — the same wording `GetStackIssues` and `ApplyStackIssueFix` use below, for the same reason. `GetRendererSchema` and `GetDataInterfaceSchema` are refused before their `RendererClassPath` / `DataInterfaceClassPath` argument is even resolved, so the `RendererClass` / `TypeName` field that used to accompany the empty `PropertySchema` is gone from the failure response too — an unresolved class is still reported separately as `NotFound`, before reaching this refusal. **UE 5.8+ is unaffected** — its own branch already could fail (an unresolved system or class) and keeps doing so unchanged. Read `GetRendererData` / `GetEmitterData` / `GetSystemData` for the actual property values on every engine version, as before. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`AddSetParameterEntry` and `AddSetParametersModule` now also accept the bare type name a read reports for `TypePath`, on every engine version** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module). `TypePath` used to resolve only a short form (`float`, `Vector3f`) or a full object path (`/Script/Niagara.NiagaraFloat`); it now also resolves the bare name that a read's own `Type` field already reports — `NiagaraFloat`, `ENiagaraCoordinateSpace`, `NiagaraDataInterfaceCurve`, `Quat4f`, and so on — so a type name read from `GetModuleTopology`, `GetEmitterTopology`, `GetScriptStackTopology`, `GetStackInputTopology`, `GetStackInputSchema`, `GetModuleSchema`, `GetDynamicInputSchema`, `GetDynamicInputSchemaFromAsset` or `GetModuleSchemaFromAsset` can now be handed straight back into `TypePath`, closing a round trip that did not exist before. **The set of types `TypePath` accepts is unchanged** — a bare name resolves through the registered Niagara type list and then the same allowlist the other two spellings already went through, so nothing that was unreachable before becomes writable; only the spelling widened. Both previous spellings keep resolving exactly as before, and a name that still does not resolve is still refused with `InvalidParams` naming it. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetDynamicInputSchemaFromAsset`'s pre-5.8 fallback now returns the asset's actual declared inputs instead of always answering an empty array** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module). The fallback used to answer `{ModuleAssetPath, Inputs: [], Outputs: []}` for every asset regardless of what it declared, and always reported success — a caller could only read that as "this asset declares no inputs", when the truth was that nothing had been read at all. ⚠️ **Breaking change.** It now reads the same `Module.`-namespace declarations UE 5.8+ already reads off the asset's graph (`Name`/`Type`/`Category`/`SupportsExpressions` per entry, static switches excluded), so a successful call's `Inputs` is populated whenever the asset declares any — and **a call that used to always succeed can now fail**: when the asset carries no readable graph, the command now answers `ExecutionFailed` instead of the previous empty-but-successful response. `Outputs` stays an empty array on this fallback, as it always was. Because the asset is read directly rather than through a call site inside a stack, the reported set is a superset of what the same script would report when read from inside a stack: an input hidden behind a static-switch branch a particular call site never takes is still listed, and stack-side hidden-input narrowing does not apply here. `GetModuleSchemaFromAsset` delegates to this same read and changes identically. **UE 5.8+ is unaffected** — its own branch already could fail on an asset with no graph to read, and keeps doing so unchanged. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetAvailableDynamicInputs`'s pre-5.8 fallback now fails instead of always answering an empty list** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module). ⚠️ **Breaking change.** Which dynamic input scripts a stack input accepts is decided by the same engine surface that offers them inside the stack UI, and earlier engine versions have no graph-level equivalent to read it from. The fallback used to answer `{DynamicInputs: []}` and report success for every call, which reads as "no dynamic input fits this input" — indistinguishable from the question never having been asked in the first place. It now answers `ExecutionFailed`, with `ErrorMessage` explaining that this needs an engine API introduced in Unreal Engine 5.8 and is reported as a failure rather than as an empty result for exactly that reason, so it cannot be mistaken for a genuine "no matches" answer. **UE 5.8+ is unaffected** — its own branch already could fail (an unresolved system, emitter, script or module) and keeps doing so unchanged. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **`GetStackIssues`'s pre-5.8 fallback now fails instead of always answering no issues, and `ApplyStackIssueFix`'s pre-5.8 fallback now fails instead of always answering `Applied: false`** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module). ⚠️ **Breaking change.** Both used to report success on every call — `GetStackIssues` with an empty `Issues` array, `ApplyStackIssueFix` with `{Applied: false}` — which reads as "no issues exist" / "this fix could not be applied", indistinguishable from the check never having run in the first place. Both now answer `ExecutionFailed`, with `ErrorMessage` explaining that the command needs an engine API introduced in Unreal Engine 5.8 and is reported as a failure rather than as an empty result for exactly that reason — the same wording for both, since they are meant to be read as a pair. **`ApplyStackIssueFix` keeps its ordinary `{Success: true, Applied: false}` answer for a genuine "this Fix could not be applied" outcome**, exactly as before — only the engine-version refusal is new, and a fix that legitimately does not apply is still reported success with `Applied: false`, never `ExecutionFailed`. **UE 5.8+ is unaffected for both commands**, including `ApplyStackIssueFix`'s ordinary "could not be applied" outcome, which was already reported this way and stays that way. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara).
- **161 Toolset bridge commands now declare and strictly validate their parameters, up from zero** (`UAIPEditorNiagara`, `UAIPEditorPCG`, `UAIPEditorSequencer`, `UAIPEditorPhysics`, `UAIPEditorUMG`, `UAIPEditorMVVM` and a handful of commands in other modules): every `Toolset.*` bridge command used to declare an empty parameter schema regardless of what it actually accepted, and any JSON sent to it — misspelled keys, missing required values, wrong types — passed straight through unchecked to the underlying Toolset call. 161 of these commands now declare a real schema (`AdditionalProperties: false`, accurate `Required` flags and types) and reject a non-conforming call up front with `InvalidParams`, naming the offending key, instead of letting it fail deep inside the engine (or silently succeed while ignoring the extra key). ⚠️ **Breaking for calls that previously "worked by accident"**: a call that sent an extra or misspelled key used to be silently accepted; on a migrated command it is now refused. The migration proceeds command by command — an unmigrated command keeps passing parameters through exactly as before, and which state a given command is in is always visible from its own schema (`uaip_describe_command`), never from this changelog alone. Five `Toolset.Editor.Niagara.*` commands (`DuplicateEmitter`, `GetScriptAssets`, `MoveModule`, `SetEmitterEnabled`, `SetEmitterName`) still do not declare parameters — that is no longer an open question, see the `DelegationTargetMissing` entry below. See [Commands — Toolset bridge commands are gaining declared, validated parameter schemas](commands.md#toolset-bridge-commands-are-gaining-declared-validated-parameter-schemas).
- **Seven Toolset bridge commands whose target function does not exist in any supported Niagara or Game Features toolset now report themselves unavailable instead of failing at call time** (`UAIPEditorNiagara`, `UAIPEditorGameFeatures` modules): `Toolset.Editor.Niagara.DuplicateEmitter`, `GetScriptAssets`, `MoveModule`, `SetEmitterEnabled`, `SetEmitterName`, `GetNiagaraParameterCollections`, and `Toolset.Editor.GameFeatures.ListGameFeatures` used to declare no parameters, report `Available: true` regardless, and fail every call at the toolset boundary with a generic `ExecutionFailed` — none of the seven could ever have succeeded, on any engine version this plugin supports, because the toolset each one forwards to declares no matching function under that name. All seven now report `Available: false` from `UAIP.Core.DescribeCommand` and `uaip_list_commands`, with `UnavailableReason: "HandlerUnavailable"` and a **new `UnavailableDetail` value, `DelegationTargetMissing`**. Calling any of the seven by name still fails, now with `PolicyViolation` and the same explanation in `ErrorMessage`. Five of the seven have a working native alternative that performs the same operation without delegating (`UAIP.Editor.Niagara.GetScriptAssets` / `GetNiagaraParameterCollections` / `SetEmitterEnabled` / `SetEmitterName`, and `UAIP.Editor.GameFeatures.ListGameFeatures`). **`DuplicateEmitter` and `MoveModule` do not** — their native counterparts (`UAIP.Editor.Niagara.DuplicateEmitter` / `MoveModule`) are themselves unavailable, for a separate reason (the engine APIs they need are never exported to a plugin, on any engine version), so **as of this update neither operation has a working path anywhere in this plugin**. This is not necessarily the full set of Toolset bridge commands in this state — check `uaip_describe_command` for any specific command's current status. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Niagara](commands.md#uaipeditorniagara-) and [API Reference — Command availability fields](api.md#65-command-availability-fields).
- **Fetching an artifact over HTTP without a `SessionId` query parameter now resolves against this editor process's own artifacts instead of always answering `NotFound`** (`UAIPTransportHTTP` module): `GET /uaip/artifacts/<ArtifactId>` used to require `SessionId` in every case it could actually resolve anything; recent changes made the artifact store capable of rediscovering artifacts left behind by a previous editor session (see the artifact-store entries above), and those rediscovered artifacts can only be looked up by naming a `SessionId` explicitly.
