Omitting `SessionId` now resolves through the same path this route always used for the current process's own artifacts, and logs a warning (with the resolved `SessionId` and `ArtifactId`) so the omission is visible in the log even though the call still succeeds. Artifacts are scoped per session, so **the fix is to name a session at the point you call the command that produces the artifact** — appending `?SessionId=...` to this GET by itself is not enough, since it can only resolve to whichever session the artifact was actually attributed to when it was created. **Command and scenario responses now echo back the resolved `SessionId`** (see [`CommandResponse`](api.md#3-response-format) / [`ScenarioResponse`](api.md#7-scenario-api) in the API Reference), so even a call that omitted `SessionId` tells you, in its own response, the value to reuse on every later call — including this route. You can also mint a `SessionId` explicitly ahead of time with `POST /uaip/sessions` (see [API Reference — Session API](api.md#6-session-api)). Artifacts already produced without naming a session remain reachable through the identifier that producing call's own response returned. Naming a `SessionId` explicitly already worked, and its behaviour is unchanged; it is now also the only way to reach a rediscovered artifact from a prior session. See [API Reference — Client examples](api.md#10-client-examples).
- **Four groups of commands now answer with a different `ErrorCode`, and several codes now map to a different HTTP status** (all modules). ⚠️ **Read this if you branch on `ErrorCode` or on the HTTP status.** (1) A precondition the handler needs but that is not yet met — the editor not fully available, no game world, an unregistered subsystem — now answers the **new `PreconditionFailed`** (HTTP 503) instead of `ExecutionFailed`; it is transient, and scenarios retry it via `RetryCount`. (2) A command refused because a required optional module or plugin is not loaded now answers `AbilityUnavailable` (501) rather than `ExecutionFailed`. (3) `NotAllowed` is no longer used to report an engine-version difference; that is `AbilityUnavailable` with an `UnavailableDetail` of `EngineVersion`. (4) The HTTP status mapping now matches the table this documentation has always published: `NotFound` returns 404 (was 500), `Timeout` 408 (was 500), `NotAllowed` 409 (was 500) and `TooManyRequests` 429 (was 500). **None of this is a breaking change under this project's versioning rules** — in each case the previous behaviour contradicted what the documentation declared, so the fix is a correction rather than a change of contract — but a caller that pattern-matched the old values will see different ones. Branch on `ErrorCode`, not on the HTTP status. See [API Reference — Error codes](api.md#4-error-codes).
- **78 commands that could never have succeeded in a given environment now say so before they are called** (`UAIPEditorSequencer` 74, `UAIPEditorAssets` 4). Each of them checked, inside `Execute()`, whether an engine module it needs (Sequencer, LevelSequenceEditor, ContentBrowser) was loaded, and failed the call when it was not — while `uaip_list_commands` and `UAIP.Core.DescribeCommand` both reported `Available: true`. A caller reading discovery had no way to learn, short of calling, that the command would fail every time. All 78 now report `Available: false` with `UnavailableReason: "HandlerUnavailable"` and an `UnavailableDetail` naming the environment reason, and the call itself answers `AbilityUnavailable` instead of a generic failure. **Where the module is loaded — which is the ordinary editor configuration — nothing changes.** See [API Reference — Command availability fields](api.md#65-command-availability-fields).
- **`UAIP.Editor.ControlRig.AddVariable` refuses an object or class `TypePath` on Unreal Engine 5.7 instead of taking the editor down** (`UAIPEditorControlRig` module). ⚠️ **Pre-5.8 only.** RigVM cannot hold an object-typed member variable before UE 5.8: the engine's own type resolution asserts on such a path rather than returning an error, and `TypePath` is a command parameter, so the assert was reachable from outside the plugin. The command now checks the type path against what that engine version's RigVM can actually represent and answers `UnsupportedOperation`, with a message listing what is accepted there (`bool`, `float`, `double`, `int32`, `FString`, `FName`, `FText`, plus any struct or enum named by its object path, optionally wrapped in a single `TArray<>`). A map type is refused for a different reason: RigVM accepts it silently and then creates nothing. **UE 5.8 is unaffected** — object types work there and always did. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.ControlRig](commands.md#uaipeditorcontrolrig).
- **`DumpSelectionState`'s `TabId` now actually resolves a graph editor** (`UAIPEditorObservation` module): `TabId` used to be accepted and silently ignored — `SelectedNodes` was always an empty array no matter what was passed, so a working `TabId` and a typo looked identical. It now resolves the tab the same way `CaptureGraphViewportImage` and `ListGraphNodes` do (an exact Slate tab match, then a case-insensitive substring match against an open asset's name; a `/`-bearing content-browser path is rejected with `InvalidParams`), and reports each selected node's `NodeId`, `NodeClass`, `NodeTitle` and `Position` in `SelectedNodes`. ⚠️ **Behavior change**: a `TabId` that names no open tab now answers `NotFound` instead of returning `Success` with an empty `SelectedNodes`. Omitting `TabId` is unchanged — `SelectedActors` / `SelectedObjects` are reported as before and `SelectedNodes` stays empty. See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Observation](commands.md#uaipeditorobservation).

**Deprecated**

- ⚠️ **`ActiveTabId` in the editor-state artifact of `OpenAsset` and `NormalizeEditorLayout` is deprecated, and its value changes in the next major version** (`UAIPEditorShared` module, used by `UAIPEditorAssets` and `UAIPEditorWorkspace`). **Nothing changes in 1.2.0 for code that reads it** — it still holds what it always held. The problem is that the same field name means two different things: in `DumpEditorState` it is the Slate layout identifier of the focused tab (`"LevelEditorViewport"`, `"OutputLog"`, …), which `CaptureEditorTabImage` accepts as `TabId`; in the artifact these two commands save, it is the **full object path of the asset** whose editor has focus (`"/Game/Maps/MyMap.MyMap"`), which every command taking a `TabId` rejects with `InvalidParams`, because it contains `/`.
  - **What is added now**: the same artifact gains `ActiveAssetPath`, holding exactly the value `ActiveTabId` holds today, and `DeprecatedFields` — an array with one entry, `{ "Field": "ActiveTabId", "Message": "...", "MigrationTarget": "ActiveAssetPath" }`, attached to every artifact that carries the deprecated value, so a caller can tell from the payload alone that it is reading the old field. `Message` and `MigrationTarget` are spelled the same as in a deprecated command's `DeprecationWarning`.
  - **What changes, and when**: in the **next major version** — not before 1.2.0 has been publicly released and two further minor releases have followed it — `ActiveTabId` in this artifact will report the Slate layout identifier of the focused tab, the same meaning `DumpEditorState` gives it, and `DeprecatedFields` will be empty. `ActiveAssetPath` stays as it is. No date is set yet.
  - **How to migrate**: read `ActiveAssetPath` wherever you read `ActiveTabId` from these two commands' artifact. For example, where you had `state["ActiveTabId"]` → `"/Game/Maps/MyMap.MyMap"`, read `state["ActiveAssetPath"]` → `"/Game/Maps/MyMap.MyMap"`. If what you actually wanted was a tab identifier to pass to `CaptureEditorTabImage`, read `ActiveTabId` from `DumpEditorState`, which already reports that.
  - **How to try the change now**: start the editor with **`-uaip-active-tab-id-as-layout-id`** (a command-line switch that takes no value; there is deliberately no ini setting for it, so it cannot be left switched on in a committed config file). With it, `ActiveTabId` in this artifact already reports the layout identifier and `DeprecatedFields` is empty — anything still reading the old value will see a different one. **This switch is provided for the 1.x releases only** and is removed when the new meaning becomes the default.
  - See [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Assets](commands.md#uaipeditorassets).

- ⚠️ **A new DefaultDenied capability, `GameplayTagExternalSourceEdit`, is now required to add, remove, or rename a gameplay tag whose write destination falls outside the project's own `Config/` directory** (`UAIPEditorGameplayTags` module): `AddGameplayTag`, `AddRestrictedGameplayTag`, `RemoveGameplayTag`, `RenameGameplayTag`, and the Toolset `AddTag` / `RemoveTag` / `RenameTag` bridges used to write to whatever `.ini` tag source they were pointed at, including one contributed by an engine plugin, a Fab / third-party plugin, or — just as easily — **the project's own game plugin or Game Feature plugin**. None of those destinations sit under the project's own `Config/` directory, so none of them were ever covered by `GameplayTagEdit` / `GameplayTagRestrictedEdit` alone. A write whose destination resolves outside `Config/` is now refused with `CapabilityNotAvailable` naming the capability and the reason, unless the session holds it. ⚠️ **Behavior change**: an add or rename that used to reach a plugin-owned source — including the project's own — now additionally requires this capability; being DefaultDenied, a configuration that never granted it starts refusing those specific writes. A Restricted source reached through a plugin's search path whose write destination does not match where it is actually read from is refused outright with `NotAllowed` instead — no capability lifts that refusal. The boundary is checked by path text alone; a symbolic link or junction placed inside `Config/` that points outside it is still treated as inside. See [Safety & Capabilities — Gameplay systems](safety.md#gameplay-systems).
- **Removing or renaming a Restricted gameplay tag now also requires `GameplayTagRestrictedEdit`** (`UAIPEditorGameplayTags` module): adding a Restricted tag has always required this capability, but removal and rename only ever asked for `GameplayTagEdit` — an asymmetry that let a session without restricted-tag privileges delete or rename one anyway. `RemoveGameplayTag` / `RenameGameplayTag` (native and Toolset bridge) now check for `GameplayTagRestrictedEdit` whenever the tag acted on — or, for a forced removal or a rename that carries children, any of its descendants — is Restricted. ⚠️ **Behavior change**: a session holding only `GameplayTagEdit` can no longer remove or rename a Restricted tag.
- **A Restricted tag source whose write destination does not match where it is actually read from is now refused outright, independent of any capability** (`UAIPEditorGameplayTags` module): a Restricted source reached through a plugin's search path is read from that plugin's own file, but the engine always writes a Restricted source's changes to `Config/Tags/<SourceName>` in the project — a mismatch no capability grant can paper over safely, since the write would land somewhere other than where the next read comes from. `AddRestrictedGameplayTag`, `RemoveGameplayTag`, `RenameGameplayTag`, and the Toolset bridge equivalents now detect this drift (and the case where a source's read path cannot be resolved to exactly one file) and refuse with `NotAllowed`, naming the reason.
- **Removing a gameplay tag now checks out its source file(s) from source control before writing**, matching what the tag editor UI has always done (`UAIPEditorGameplayTags` module). ⚠️ If a checked-out-but-unwritable file is found before any file is touched, the command refuses without checking out anything (`ExecutionFailed`, naming the file); if a later file in a multi-source removal fails to write after an earlier one already checked out and wrote successfully, the earlier checkout is left in place rather than rolled back — retry once the cause is fixed and the remaining sources are removed too.

**Fixed**

- ⚠️ **`SetAnimGraphNodeProperty` had its write discarded by the next compile whenever the property was exposed as a pin** (`UAIPEditorAnimBlueprint` module): a member of an AnimGraph node can be exposed as a pin, and for an exposed pin nothing is connected to, the Anim Blueprint compiler **writes the pin's default value into the node**. This command wrote only the property and left the corresponding pin alone, so the write returned success, `GetAnimGraphNodeDetails` read the requested value back, the asset saved — and **the compiled node still carried the value the pin held**. Reconstructing the node did not close the gap either: the engine copies a property onto a pin only while that pin is being created, and a pin that was already visible keeps whatever the previous pin held. The command now rebuilds the matching exposed pin's default from the property after the write, using the same conversion the engine performs when it creates such a pin, so a value written this way is spelled exactly as the same value typed into the editor. A connected pin is left alone, since its value arrives over the link and its default is never read. **If you were writing a property that is exposed as a pin by default — `LiveLinkSubjectName` on the Live Link Pose node, for instance — the value may not have been taking effect until now.**

- **Some capabilities could never be lifted, however correctly they were named in `AllowedCapabilities`** (`UAIPCore` module): the capability grant decision only ever considered names that a registered command provider had declared. A capability the property-write path asks for **dynamically, based on the shape of the value in hand** — `PropertyDefaultsOnlyEdit` and `PropertyStructuredEdit` — appears in no command's `RequiredCapabilities`, never entered the candidate set, and so was dropped no matter what the ini said. `ReloadCapabilities` reported `AddedCapabilities: []` with no error and no warning, which an operator cannot tell apart from "that was already the state". A DefaultDenied capability is meant to be a door an operator can open, and one with no opening mechanism removes the feature by the back door. A capability the ini names is now part of the decision as long as it is **registered** — an unregistered name is still left out, so a misspelling cannot turn into a grant. Revocation is symmetric: delete the line, call `ReloadCapabilities`, and the capability leaves the session. `ReloadCapabilities` also gained an **`UnknownCapabilities`** field, listing names the ini states that are not registered capabilities, so a misspelling is no longer indistinguishable from a no-op.

- **`PlaceActorInLevel` refused the spelling its own parameter description shows** (`UAIPEditorLevel` module): the `ClassName` description gives `/Game/Blueprints/BP_MyActor.BP_MyActor_C` as its example, but the implementation loaded the name it was handed as a Blueprint asset. A name ending in `_C` addresses the class the Blueprint generates rather than the asset itself, so **writing it the way the description shows always failed with `Blueprint not found`** — and that spelling is also what the content browser copies. The command now retries without the suffix, but only after the literal name has failed, so a **Blueprint whose own asset name ends in `_C`** still resolves to itself rather than to a neighbouring asset.

- ⚠️ **`ReparentBlueprintComponent` reported success for an attachment the engine undoes afterwards** (`UAIPEditorBlueprint` module): both this command's description and the contract behind it stated that, when a parent is named, the target and the new parent must each be a `USceneComponent` subclass — and **neither of them checked**. Handing it a plain `UActorComponent` subclass returned `Success` while the hierarchy stayed exactly as it was. The engine does not refuse the request; it accepts it and then takes it back. `USimpleConstructionScript`'s hierarchy fixup puts a non-scene node back in the root list, drops it from its parent's child list, and clears the children it had been given; a node parented to a non-scene component loses the relationship for the same reason. That pass runs on a full compile, on load, and on reinstancing, so **the relationship never survives** — and because it does not run right after the write, reading the value back immediately shows nothing wrong. The command now checks both classes before anything is written and refuses with `InvalidParams` otherwise, deciding the same way the engine's fixup does (the node's declared class first, its template's class only as a fallback). ⚠️ **Behaviour change — requests that used to return `Success` are now refused.** Only the answer was ever a success, though: the change itself never once lasted. **Moving to the root level (an empty `NewParentVariableName`) stays available to every component class** — that is where the engine puts a component without a transform anyway. The refusal also matches the Blueprint editor, which rejects the same drag at both ends.

- **`ReparentBlueprintComponent` wrote a parent link the engine treats as invalid** (`UAIPEditorBlueprint` module): separately from the item above, **an ordinary reparent between two scene components was affected too**. The command put the target into the new parent's child list and then also called `USCS_Node::SetParent()`. That function records an *inherited* parent — a node living in a parent Blueprint's construction script — and is not for parenting within one script. The result was a node that sat in a parent's child list while also naming that parent, and the engine's hierarchy fixup reported it on the next full compile as `Reparenting the '...' component (now nested under '...') - possible cyclic linkage? Please validate the component hierarchy and resave the Blueprint.` On top of that, **moving to the root level (an empty `NewParentVariableName`) passed `null` to that same function and tripped an engine check every single time**. Neither path calls `SetParent()` any more — within one script the command now just adds the node to the parent's child list, which is what the engine's own reparent path does. **The same misuse was present in `DuplicateBlueprintComponent`'s "duplicate a component that has a parent" path and is fixed alongside it.**

- **Automatic artifact cleanup could never run at all** (`UAIPArtifacts` module): the age and session-count rules had exactly one caller, a ticker on the configured interval (one hour by default). With this kind of ticker **the interval is also the wait before the first tick**, so any use that closes the editor sooner — a short run, a build check, a headless automation run — never reaches those rules even once. Nothing pointed at it: the feature was enabled, and the startup scan that re-indexes artifacts from previous runs was working (measured: `Saved/UAIP/` had accumulated three months of sessions — 366 directories, about 240MB). Cleanup on session end does not stand in for it, because it only reaches sessions the session manager can still name. The rules now also run once at startup; the ticker stays for editors that do run for hours. Retention settings themselves are unchanged. **On an installation with a backlog, the first start after this update may spend noticeable time deleting** (measured: three months of accumulation went from 366 directories, 5668 files and about 240MB down to 7 directories, 199 files and 26MB in a single start). Files and directories under `Saved/UAIP/` that this store did not write are never deleted.

- **`GetStackInputData` took the editor down on every call** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module): the command builds a headless Niagara system view model to read the stack, and initializing one builds the whole stack tree, whose entries subscribe to the engine's message manager using the key the view model was given. UAIP never set that key, so the entries subscribed with an unset one and the engine's subscribe path asserted on it — the editor died before the read returned anything. Running headless does not skip that work: the data-processing flag only suppresses the details panel and issue generation, both of which come later. The view model is now given the system's own asset guid, which is what every engine path that builds one without an editor window passes, and what the Niagara editor uses for the same system. The same guard is shared by the emitter, renderer, stack input and module topology reads, so all four were affected and all four are fixed.

- **`AddSetParameterEntry` and `AddSetParametersModule` reported success for a parameter type they had refused** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module): when the requested type did not resolve against Niagara's type allowlist, both commands logged the refusal, then created the entry as a `float` and returned success anyway. A caller asking for an object-typed parameter was told it had been created, and the only way to find out otherwise was to read the entry back and see a type it never asked for. An unresolvable type is now refused with `InvalidParams` naming it. `AddSetParametersModule` refuses before the module is created, so a request whose second parameter is unresolvable no longer leaves a module holding only the entries that resolved. The `ParsedAsDefault` / `ParsedAsDefaultArray` flags no longer fold in "the type did not resolve" and report only that a value fell back to its type's default.

- **`GetSystemTopology` reported every emitter's `Spawn`, `Update` and `Event` module lists as always empty** (`UAIPEditorNiagara` module): an earlier crash fix removed the code that walked each stack's modules, leaving these arrays permanently empty and pointing callers at `GetStackInputData` instead — which itself needs a module id `GetSystemTopology` could no longer supply. It now walks each script's graph the same crash-safe way `AddModule` / `RemoveModule` already do and reports each emitter's real modules (name plus the new `ModuleId`, see **Added**). ⚠️ **Still narrower than `GetEmitterTopology`.** Here `Spawn`/`Update` are the *emitter*-level stacks and `Event` is the event handler stack; a system's Particle Spawn / Particle Update modules are never listed by `GetSystemTopology`, at any point. Use `GetEmitterTopology` (`EmitterSpawn` / `EmitterUpdate` / `ParticleSpawn` / `ParticleUpdate`) when a complete listing matters.

- **Trace analysis reported a failed artifact write as a failed extraction** (`UAIPRuntimeInsights` module): when the analysis itself succeeded but a result section's artifact could not be written, `GetTraceAnalysisResult` reported the reason as `ExtractionFailed`. The symptom surfaced one layer away from the cause, sending callers to inspect the trace or the analyzer. A section that could not be written is now reported as an **artifact save failure** rather than an extraction failure.

- **References to artifacts that cannot be fetched are now removed from responses and scenario aggregates**: an artifact whose save failed carries an empty identifier. Left on the response, a caller treats it as fetchable, and in a scenario `${StepName.Artifacts[n]}` resolves by index — so **an empty entry occupies a position and shifts every reference after it**. The response-building layer now refuses these, and the same check runs again just before serialization, dropping any that still arrive and recording a warning. In the asset audit, validation and trace analysis jobs, a report that could not be saved drops out of the result list and is recorded with its reason as an unavailable report — the job itself does not fail.

- **`DumpOpenTabs` reported success when it had failed to write its own output** (`UAIPEditorObservation` module). The artifact is this command's entire response — there is no `Result` payload beside it — but the handler attached the artifact reference without checking that the store had actually saved anything. When a save failed (a full disk, a denied path, a name too long for the filesystem), the caller received `Success` together with an artifact entry whose id was empty, which resolves to nothing: the MCP bridge skips it silently, and a scenario spliced the empty string into whatever step referenced it. It now returns `ExecutionFailed` and no artifact. ⚠️ **Behavior change** — a caller that treated `Success` as proof an artifact exists will now see the failure instead of an unusable reference.
- **`NormalizeEditorLayout` attached an artifact reference that resolved to nothing** (`UAIPEditorWorkspace` module). Same cause as above, but the artifact here only records the editor state after a layout change that has already happened, so a save failure is not a failure of the command: it now returns success with no artifact rather than one the caller cannot read.

- **A rejected property write reported `ExecutionFailed` with an empty `ErrorMessage`.** `FObjectPropertyUtils::SetPropertyValue` (`UAIPEditorShared` module) is the shared write path behind every property-mutation command — `SetAssetProperty`, `SetActorProperty`, `SetWorldSetting`, `SetDataTableRow`, `SetBlueprintDefault`, `SetProjectSetting`, and several node/component property setters in other modules. Every failure branch below `ImportText_InContainer` returned its result code without ever writing to `OutError`, so a rejected write — an unresolved `PropertyPath` segment, a cross-object reference in the path, an out-of-range array index, or a property type/flag `SetPropertyValue` refuses to write (a whole array, `EditConst`, disabled-for-this-context, deprecated) — reached the caller as `{"Success": false, "ErrorCode": "ExecutionFailed", "ErrorMessage": ""}` with no way to tell what went wrong. `ErrorMessage` now names the property path, the resolved property's C++ type, and, for a type/flag rejection, the specific reason. (Writing a whole array is no longer one of those rejections — see the reference / struct / container entry under **Added** — but the same wording still covers the type / flag rejections that remain.) A failed `ImportText` (the value string does not match the target property's UE text-import format) now also names the property, its expected type, and the rejected value instead of a fixed generic sentence.
- **`DumpInputState` reported `ActiveMappingContexts` as an always-empty array** (`UAIPRuntimeInput` module). The field was declared in the response but nothing ever populated it, so a caller could never tell which Input Mapping Contexts were actually active on the local player. It now reports the real set — `{ Path, Priority }` per context, sorted by `Priority` descending then `Path` ascending. The command also gains `EnhancedInputState` (`"Available"` / `"Unavailable"`), which tells apart "queried and found none" from "could not query at all", and a new optional parameter `IncludeActionStates` (Boolean, defaults to `false`) that, when set to `true`, additionally returns `ActionStates[]` — per-action `ETriggerEvent` state sourced from the active mapping contexts — capped at 256 entries with `ActionStatesTotalCount` / `ActionStatesTruncated`. Required capability is unchanged (`RuntimeInspect`). ⚠️ **Behavior change** — a caller that treated the previously-empty `ActiveMappingContexts` as "no contexts are active" now sees the actual list.
- **`FocusEditorTab`'s `GraphName` was silently ignored on Blueprint-derived editors that report their own toolkit name** (`UAIPEditorWorkspace` module): the command told a graph-capable Blueprint editor apart from any other open editor by matching the toolkit's display name against a fixed string, so an editor that subclasses `FBlueprintEditor` but reports its own toolkit name — AnimBlueprint, WidgetBlueprint (UMG), GameplayAbility Blueprint, and the legacy Control Rig editor among them — never matched, and the requested `GraphName` was dropped without an error. The response still came back `Success: true` with the requested `GraphName` echoed in it, so a caller had no way to tell that no tab actually changed. The check now asks the editor's own toolkit interface directly instead of matching a name string. For a call that passes a non-empty `GraphName`, `Result` now reports `WindowFocusRequested` (whether window activation was requested — the OS actually bringing it to the foreground is never guaranteed), `GraphNameRequested` / `GraphNameApplied`, and `Reason` (`Applied`, `BlueprintEditorInterfaceUnavailable`, or `OpenFailed` — a closed set of three); a call that omits `GraphName` leaves `Result` unset, as before. Read these fields rather than `Success` alone whenever the tab switch itself matters. ⚠️ **Behavior change — two ErrorCodes changed with no compatibility window**, since retrying with the same parameters could never have succeeded either way: naming a `GraphName` on an asset that is not a Blueprint now returns `InvalidParams` instead of `ExecutionFailed`, and naming a graph that does not exist on the target Blueprint now returns `NotFound` instead of `ExecutionFailed`. A scenario step's `RetryCount` retries only `ExecutionFailed` and `PreconditionFailed`, so a caller relying on that no longer retries either case — that is the intent, since neither failure resolves by retrying unchanged. Separately, when `GraphName` matches more than one graph in the asset, one of them is opened and which one is not defined — see [Commands — UAIP.Editor.Workspace](commands.md#uaipeditorworkspace) for when that can happen.
- **A Blueprint node or pin that does not exist was reported as `ExecutionFailed` instead of `NotFound`** (`UAIPEditorBlueprint` module): `ConnectBlueprintPins`, `DisconnectBlueprintPins`, `DeleteGraphNode`, `GetPinDefaultValue`, `ListBlueprintPins`, and `SetPinDefaultValue` all answered `ExecutionFailed` for an unresolvable `NodeId` or `PinName`, which a scenario's `RetryCount` retries even though repeating the same request can never succeed. All six now answer `NotFound` for that specific failure. `GetPinDefaultValue` had a second, more serious defect alongside this one: when `PinName` matched no pin on the target node, the handler had no branch for that case at all and fell through to reading a pin that was never found — an unchecked access with undefined behavior. It now reports `NotFound` the same as the other five.
- **ControlRig's `GraphName` resolution reported the same error for "no such graph" and "the asset itself is unreadable"** (`UAIPEditorControlRig` module): every command that resolves a RigVM model by `GraphName` — `GetGraph`, `AddGraph`, `DeleteGraph`, `AddGraphNode`, `AddEventNode`, `AddVariableNode`, `FindNodes`, `GetConnectedPins`, `GetNodeInfo`, `GetNodePosition`, `GetPinValue`, `ListNodes`, `ListPins`, `SetNodePosition`, `SetPinValue`, `ResetPinValue`, `DuplicateNode` — answered `ExecutionFailed` with "Graph is null." in both cases, so the dedicated `NotFound` outcome the response schema already declared was never actually reachable. `GraphName` matching no model in an otherwise-valid asset now answers `NotFound`; the asset's own ControlRigBlueprint reference being unreadable still answers `ExecutionFailed`.
- **Repeatedly naming an unknown graph grew the engine's name table for the rest of the editor session** (`UAIPEditorBlueprint`, `UAIPEditorAnimBlueprint` modules): resolving a graph by name used to intern the caller-supplied string into an `FName` before searching for it, and `FName`s are never freed once interned, so a scenario or a misbehaving caller that tried many nonexistent graph names left the process's memory usage permanently higher for the session. Both modules' graph-name lookup now uses `FNAME_Find` (look up an already-interned name without creating a new one) instead, so a name that was never used elsewhere resolves straight to "not found" without growing the table.
- ⚠️ **A trace recorded with `StartTrace`'s default settings could never be analyzed, and nothing said so** (`UAIPRuntimeInsights` module): `MaxFileSizeMB` defaulted to 512, exactly the size `AnalyzeTrace` accepts. That limit is sampled once per second rather than enforced on every write, so a trace that stops itself on size is always **above** its limit by whatever was written before the next sample — which put every trace recorded with the defaults past what `AnalyzeTrace` takes. The `TraceTooLargeToAnalyze` warning that exists for exactly this could not fire either, because it compared the limit against the cap with a strict `>` and the two were equal. The path nobody has to opt into was the one path with no warning on it. The default is now **448 MB**, leaving room for the overshoot, and the warning now compares the size the file can actually **reach** against the cap, so any limit above 448 MB is reported when the trace starts. ⚠️ **A trace started without a `MaxFileSizeMB` is now 64 MB smaller than before.** Pass the value explicitly to record more, and expect the warning that comes with it.

- **A scenario that hit the 1800-second wall-clock cap was reported as a full success** (`UAIPScenario` module): `AllStepsSucceeded` only looked at whether every executed step had returned `Success: true`; a scenario cut off by the wall-clock watchdog — before any step ran, or between steps — had no failing step to report and so answered `AllStepsSucceeded: true`, indistinguishable from a scenario that actually finished. The response now carries `Status: "TimedOut"`, `AllStepsSucceeded: false`, and a top-level `ErrorCode: "Timeout"` for this case; the HTTP status stays 200, the same as every other scenario response, so branch on `Status` / `ErrorCode`, not the HTTP status. `StepResults` may still be empty for a timed-out scenario — steps can still be running in the editor in the background — so an empty array is not proof nothing ran. See [Scenario Execution → Response shape](scenario.md#response-shape).

- **Six `GetParameterSchema()` parameter descriptions said something the implementation did not do** (`UAIPEditorObservation` module), found while auditing the module's descriptions against their parsers: `CaptureGraphViewportImage`'s `TabId` described a content-browser path, but it is actually resolved the same two-step way every graph-editor `TabId` is (an exact Slate `FTabId` match, then a case-insensitive substring match against an open asset's name or path) and never accepts a `/`-bearing path; that command's and `CaptureEditorTabImage`'s `IncludeOverlays` said it defaults to `false` when the parser's actual default is `true`; `DumpSlateTree`'s `RootWidgetPath` said dot-separated when the parser splits on `/`, and its `MaxDepth` / `MaxNodes` stated defaults of 20 / 500 against actual defaults of 5 / 5000; and `DumpSelectionState`'s `TabId` implied that passing one includes graph node selection, when `SelectedNodes` is always returned empty regardless of `TabId` — that extraction path is not implemented. None of these six affected validation, parsing, or execution — only the text `UAIP.Core.DescribeCommand` returns was wrong. All six now describe what the parser and handler actually do.

- **Four more `GetParameterSchema()` parameter descriptions said something the implementation did not do** (`UAIPEditorExecution`, `UAIPRuntimeExecution`, `UAIPRuntimeObservation` modules), found by extending the same description-vs-parser audit to every module: `RunEditorPythonScript`'s `TimeoutSec` description promised the script would be cut off once the given number of seconds elapsed, but the value is parsed and range-checked and then never read anywhere else — Python always runs to completion on the game thread. The description no longer makes that promise; it now says the field is accepted for compatibility but currently has no effect, and points callers at the enclosing request's own `TimeoutSeconds` to extend how long they wait for the answer instead. `RunFunctionalTest`'s `TimeoutSec` stated a default of 60 against the parser's actual default of 120, and `RunGauntletTest`'s stated 300 against an actual default of 600. `DumpActorState`'s `IncludeComponents` said it defaults to `false` when the parser's actual default is `true`. None of these affected validation, parsing, or execution — only the text `UAIP.Core.DescribeCommand` returns was wrong. All four now describe what the parser and handler actually do.

- **`RemoveGameplayTag` wrote every non-default-source removal to the wrong file** (`UAIPEditorGameplayTags` module): a tag living in a project's own additional tag source (a `.ini` registered via `AddNewGameplayTagSource`, typically under `Config/Tags/`) was removed from the in-memory list correctly, but the write path always passed an empty config filename, which falls back to the engine's default gameplay tags list file — so the command reported success while `Config/DefaultGameplayTagsList.ini` gained a stray removal entry and the tag's actual source file was never touched. The write now targets each source's own config file explicitly, so a removal lands only in the file the tag actually came from and no defaulted-to file is touched. This also fixes removal when the same tag name is registered from more than one source: previously only the first source found was ever inspected, so the command could report success while the tag remained readable from a second source; all sources holding the tag are now removed together, or none are if any of them cannot be written (see **Fixed** below and **Changed** above for the source-control checkout this now performs).
- **`RemoveGameplayTag` could not remove a Restricted tag at all** (`UAIPEditorGameplayTags` module): the removal path only ever looked at the ordinary tag list, so a Restricted tag was reported as not found regardless of capabilities held. Restricted tags can now be removed the same way ordinary ones are, subject to `GameplayTagRestrictedEdit` (see **Changed** above).
- **`AddGameplayTag` and the Toolset `AddTag` bridge could crash the editor when pointed at a Restricted tag source** (`UAIPEditorGameplayTags` module): passing the name of a Restricted-tag source to the unrestricted add path reached engine code that assumes the source has an ordinary tag list, which a Restricted-only source does not have. Both commands now check the source's kind before touching it and refuse with `InvalidParams` naming the correct command to use (`AddRestrictedGameplayTag` for a Restricted source; conversely, naming a non-Restricted source to `AddRestrictedGameplayTag` is refused the same way).
- **The `UAIP.GameplayTagEditing.AT_GAMETAG_INTEGRATION_*` automation tests no longer write to the executed project's `Config/` directory.** All five tests used to add, rename, or remove tags directly in the project's own tag sources, leaving redirect and tag entries behind on every run — including a `UAIPTestTag.RenameOld` → `UAIPTestTag.RenameNew` redirect in `DefaultGameplayTags.ini`. They now register disposable tag sources outside the project for the duration of the run and verify the project's `Config/` directory is byte-for-byte unchanged afterward, failing the run rather than leaving residue if it is not. **If you ran an earlier version of these tests**, your project's `DefaultGameplayTags.ini` may still contain a `+GameplayTagRedirects=(OldTagName="UAIPTestTag.RenameOld",NewTagName="UAIPTestTag.RenameNew")` line and tag entries starting with `UAIPTestTag.`; if the project also has a Restricted tag source, that source's `.ini` may contain a `UAIPTestTag.Restricted` entry. These are safe to delete by hand — the new tests do not depend on them and do not clean them up automatically.
- **On UE 5.7, duplicating a Groom, Geometry Collection or Chaos Cloth asset through UAIP could crash the editor later** (`UAIPEditorAssets`, `UAIPEditorGroomAsset`, `UAIPEditorShared` modules): UE 5.7's `FDataflowInstance` copies its property-change delegate handle when it is assigned, so a duplicated asset that embeds one — a Groom, Geometry Collection, Chaos Cloth asset or Dataflow attachment — never unregisters its own listener. Once the duplicate was deleted or otherwise garbage collected, the next property change anywhere in the editor crashed inside `FDataflowInstance::OnOwnerPostEditChangeProperty`. `DuplicateAsset`, `CopyAsset`, `BakeGroomRBFDeformation` and property writes that copy an instanced subobject now remove that listener right after duplicating. The price is that, until the duplicate is reloaded, its Dataflow variable overrides no longer follow edits made to its own properties. Nothing changes on UE 5.8, where the engine no longer copies the handle. ⚠️ **Known issue on UE 5.7**: a duplicate made with the engine's own tools (the Content Browser's Duplicate, for example) still has the engine defect — see [Troubleshooting](troubleshooting.md).

#### MCP Bridge 1.2.0 — released 2026-09-29

The bridge is versioned and released independently of the plugin; see the version numbering above.

**Added**

- **`uaip_execute` accepts an optional `TimeoutSeconds` argument, so a call that legitimately runs longer than 120 seconds is no longer cut off.** Sibling to `CommandName` and `Params` (not inside `Params`), it is a JSON number from 1 to 1800 that overrides the editor's 120-second async command timeout for this one call, the same field `POST /uaip/commands` already accepted for HTTP (see the plugin's **Added** entry above). Omitting it keeps the 120-second default; a value outside that range or of the wrong type is refused before the command runs, with a JSON-RPC invalid-params error whose message never contains the words "execution timeout" — so a refusal cannot be mistaken for a command that may still be running. The bridge extends both its own read timeout and the interval during which it suppresses health polling to match the requested window, so a busy editor still working on a long call is not mistaken for an unresponsive one partway through it — including when `scenario_timeout_seconds` has been configured lower than the window a single call named for itself. See [Connections → Long-running commands](connections.md#long-running-commands-and-the-120-s-async-timeout) and [API Reference → Request format](api.md#2-request-format) (§2.4).
- **Progress notifications while a `uaip_execute` call is pending.** When the client attaches an MCP progress token to the call, the bridge now sends a `notifications/progress` update about every 5 seconds until the call returns, carrying the seconds elapsed and the editor's state (`STARTING` / `RUNNING` / `UNRESPONSIVE`). It reports nothing from inside the editor — a job's own progress is read with the command that knows it, such as `GetAssetAuditStatus` — and `uaip_run_scenario` is out of scope. With no token nothing is sent. ⚠️ **Whether the update is ever shown is the client's decision**, and a client that ignores `notifications/progress` displays nothing while the call is pending; Claude Code is one such client as of this writing. See [Connections → Progress notifications while a call is pending](connections.md#progress-notifications-while-a-call-is-pending).
- **`uaip_get_editor_status` now reports the lock file's state.** The response gains a `Data.Lock` object — whether a lock file exists, whether this bridge holds it, the port / project / timestamp it records, and whether that recorded port currently has something listening on it — so a launch blocked by another bridge's lock can be diagnosed without deleting anything. See [Connections → Lock diagnostics](connections.md#lock-diagnostics).
- **`uaip_get_editor_status` now reports how the bridge's own `config.json` load went.** The response gains a `Data.Config` object — the path the bridge read, whether the file existed and parsed (`Loaded` / `Absent`), the count and names of the top-level keys actually found in it (never their values), and whether the bridge's most recent `uaip_reload_config` attempt failed and why. `RecommendedAction` grows one extra sentence when either of those is worth flagging, so a caller that only ever parses `RecommendedAction` still learns about it. See [Connections → Config diagnostics](connections.md#config-diagnostics).
- **The bridge now verifies it is talking to this project's editor before sending any credential, on the first request of a new connection and again after any reconnect or restart.** Before attaching a role token's `Authorization` header, it calls the editor's new `GET /uaip/instance-proof` (see the plugin's **Added** entry above) with a fresh challenge and checks the signed response against the editor's own per-launch secret file. Verification is cached per connection and redone after a connection error, a 401, an explicit restart (`RestartEditor` / `restart_editor_and_wait()`), a config reload, or any other point where the process behind the port could have changed — never on the hot path of an already-verified, still-answering editor. A session with no role configured never verifies and never sends a credential, since it has none to protect. Requires **UAIP 1.2.0 or later**; the editor's own `uproject_path` in `config.json` was already unconditionally required, and is now also where the bridge reads the per-launch secret this check compares against. See [Connections → MCP Bridge](connections.md#mcp-bridge) and [Security → Instance proof](security.md#instance-proof).
- **The bridge can authenticate as one of the editor's roles, with `role_name` / `role_token`** (see the plugin's **Added** entry on roles above). `config.json` gains `role_name` — the bridge reads the matching token from `Saved/UAIP/Roles/<role_name>.token`, only after a request comes back `401` — and `role_token`, the token value itself for when it is provisioned some other way, which takes precedence. Environment overrides are `UAIP_ROLE_NAME` / `UAIP_ROLE_TOKEN`. Leaving both empty sends every request exactly as before. Requires **UAIP 1.2.0 or later**. See [Configuration → MCP Bridge config.json](config.md#mcp-bridge-configjson).

**Fixed**

- **A `config.json` saved with a UTF-8 byte order mark (BOM) was silently discarded, and the bridge quietly ran on built-in defaults nobody chose.** `json.load()` rejects a BOM-prefixed file, and that failure used to be swallowed as a single warning line — every setting in the file (timeouts, `enable_scenario`, the inline-artifact policy, roles, everything) reverted to its default at once, with no signal beyond a log line nobody was watching for. The BOM itself came from the installer: Windows PowerShell 5.1's `Set-Content -Encoding UTF8` writes a BOM by default, so every `config.json` the bundled installer wrote on Windows carried one — the one supported platform's own installer produced a file its own bridge could not read. Both halves are fixed together: the bridge now opens `config.json` as `utf-8-sig`, which reads a BOM-prefixed file exactly like one without, and the installer's config writer now writes UTF-8 **without** a BOM, so a freshly written or freshly updated file cannot reintroduce the problem.
- **A `config.json` that cannot be read no longer falls back to defaults silently — the bridge now refuses to start.** Continuing on defaults after a read failure meant running on a configuration nobody chose, with the symptom (some tool behaving unexpectedly) far removed from the cause (the file). At startup, an unreadable or malformed `config.json` — invalid JSON, non-UTF-8 content, over the 1 MiB size limit, a JSON value that is not an object — now prints the exact path and reason to stderr and exits with code 2 before any MCP tool call becomes possible; check your MCP client's **server log**, not the AI's context, if the bridge never becomes available. `uaip_reload_config` hitting the same problem behaves differently on purpose: it does **not** tear down the running session. It reports the failure (`ErrorCode: InvalidParams`) and leaves the configuration already in effect untouched, recording the reason in `Data.Config.LastReloadError` until a later successful reload clears it.
- **A shutdown the editor refused was reported as a success.** `ShutdownEditor` returned `{"Success": true}` without ever reading what the editor answered, so a refusal — a read-only safety policy declining the command, for instance — looked like a clean shutdown while the process kept running and kept holding its file locks. The bridge now forwards the editor's own error, and confirms the port has actually been released before saying the editor stopped; when it cannot tell, it says so rather than guessing.
- **A restart left the bridge unable to talk to the editor at all.** `RestartEditor` is the editor relaunching itself, so the successor is not a process the bridge started. The bridge went on holding the dead handle and refused every later command with "the port is occupied by a process that is not this project's editor" — permanently, because that refusal cleared no state. Recovering meant closing the editor by hand. The bridge now adopts the editor answering on the port, after checking it reports the same project.
- **A response cut short no longer restarts a healthy editor.** A connection that dropped mid-response and one that never opened were classified the same way, so the bridge could restart an editor that was working and resend a request it had already begun. The two are now told apart, and a dropped response only counts as a dead editor when the port has genuinely gone.
- **A long command no longer looks like a dead editor.** The bridge pings every 15 seconds and declared the editor stopped after 30 seconds of silence, but a command that holds the game thread for longer than that — `BuildMetaHuman` takes about 75 seconds — starved those pings. The bridge would then either launch a second editor against the same project or terminate the healthy one that was still working.
- **A scenario template with a leading slash no longer misses.** The pointer tokenizer rewrote every `.` to `/`, so a body written as `${S.Data./refPath}` became `//refPath` and matched nothing. A body opening with `/` is now read as a literal JSON pointer — the only way to reach a key that itself contains `.` — while any other body keeps `.` and `/` as interchangeable separators. A failed resolution also names which part failed instead of returning one fixed sentence for every cause.
- **A running editor could be judged stopped, handing its lock to a second bridge.** The bridge checked whether the lock holder's process id was alive with `os.kill(pid, 0)`. On Windows that call raises **the same error** whether the process does not exist or exists but cannot be queried, and the bridge read it as the former — so the process holding a running editor could be written off as gone. Two bridges holding the same lock drive the same editor at once, which crosses commands and can end the editor unexpectedly. The bridge now queries the process directly and reads its exit code, treating "cannot be queried" as proof that the process exists.
- **A bridge no longer driving an editor kept holding the project lock, blocking every other session from launching one.** The lock was only released on a clean shutdown; if the bridge was force-terminated, or if it simply stopped driving an editor without exiting itself (the editor was closed or restarted from elsewhere), the lock stayed marked as held, and every other bridge trying to launch that project's editor was refused with "another mcp_proxy instance is already managing this project" — with no way out short of deleting the lock file by hand. The bridge now releases the lock as soon as it notices it is no longer driving an editor (unless it is waiting out a restart it requested itself), and a force-terminated bridge no longer leaves the lock stuck either, since the OS releases the underlying file handle the moment that process ends. A lock file that is merely present but held by nobody is simply overwritten by the next bridge to start — it is not itself a problem anymore.

**Changed**

- ⚠️ **The bridge now requires UAIP 1.2.0 or later.** The UAIP floor in the bridge's `compatibility.json` moved from `0.9.1` to `1.2.0`: the bridge relies on `UAIP.Core.Artifacts.GetArtifact`, the `ListCommands` discovery parameters and instance proof, all added in UAIP 1.2.0. Connected to an older plugin, it reports that the plugin must be upgraded instead of silently losing artifact access. A project still on UAIP 1.1.x should stay on MCP Bridge 1.1.2.
- ⚠️ **One bridge process is now one UAIP session: the bridge overwrites any `SessionId` the caller supplies.** On the five tools it forwards to the editor (`uaip_execute`, `uaip_list_commands`, `uaip_describe_command`, `uaip_query_capabilities`, `uaip_run_scenario`), the bridge replaces the caller's `SessionId` with its own `MCP-<32 hex digits>`, minted once per process and kept across `uaip_reload_config`, and logs each replacement. The artifacts of a whole conversation now land in one session folder instead of a fresh anonymous session for every call that gave none, and a role binding holds for the life of the process. A client that used different `SessionId`s through one bridge to keep work apart must run one bridge per session instead.
- **Artifacts that will not be inlined are no longer downloaded.** The bridge fetched an artifact's bytes and then decided whether its MIME type was on the inline allowlist. It now reads the length and content type first and fetches the body only when it is going to be used, which matters most for the binary artifacts the allowlist excludes by design — a `.utrace` capture can be hundreds of megabytes.
- **The installer's own `config.json` defaults now match the ones documented here.** The installer template shipped `command_timeout_seconds: 60` (the documented default has always been `180`) and `inline_artifacts.json` / `inline_artifacts.text` at `true` (both are `false`, matching `inline_artifacts.image`). Running the installer over an existing install now moves a value onto the new default **only when it still equals the old default outright** — anything you set yourself is left untouched. See [Configuration → MCP Bridge config.json](config.md#mcp-bridge-configjson).
- **Non-Windows note:** the BOM problem above was specific to `Set-Content` on Windows PowerShell 5.1, so `install.sh` never wrote one — and the automatic migration described above, which only fires for a BOM-prefixed file left by an old installer, never applies to a `config.json` `install.sh` created. If you installed on macOS / Linux with an older bridge, your `config.json` keeps its old defaults (`command_timeout_seconds: 60`, `inline_artifacts.json` / `.text`: `true`) until you edit it by hand or delete it and let the installer recreate it.

#### Claude Code Plugin 1.0.0 — released 2026-09-29

First public release, `1.0.0`. Eight `/uaip:*` skills (`uaip`, `launch`, `health`, `diag`, `exec`, `capture`, `test`, `scenario`) that let Claude Code drive, observe, and test a UE Editor project over UAIP's HTTP API directly — no MCP server configured, no separate MCP Bridge install. Each skill runs one pre-approved, bundled Python script; the editor's own Capability grants and SafetyPolicy remain the boundary for what a command may actually do, the same as every other transport. Before sending its authentication token, it verifies the target port is genuinely this project's editor using the new [instance proof](security.md#instance-proof) mechanism above. Requires **UAIP 1.2.0 or later** with the HTTP API enabled (not MCP-only, not the demo build) and Python 3.10+. Distributed separately from both the plugin and the MCP Bridge, as `UAIP-ClaudeCodePlugin-<version>.zip` from this repository's Releases (tag `ClaudeCodePlugin-v<X.Y.Z>`), under marketplace `uaip-tools` / plugin name `uaip`. See [Claude Code Plugin](claude-code-plugin.md) for installation, verification, exit codes, and how it compares to the MCP Bridge.

---

### UAIP Plugin 1.1.0 — 2026-07-24

**UAIP 1.1.0 is now live on Fab.** [https://www.fab.com/listings/0eedf909-00ac-4d95-b109-8fda51800fff](https://www.fab.com/listings/0eedf909-00ac-4d95-b109-8fda51800fff)

[![Watch the update trailer on YouTube](https://markdown-videos-api.jorgenkh.no/youtube/0Z2MKvMQ0g8)](https://youtu.be/0Z2MKvMQ0g8)

A backward-compatible MINOR release. All changes below have shipped in the plugin repository (`.uplugin` `VersionName` is `1.1.0`) and passed Fab review.

> **Migration notes.** 1.1.0 is a backward-compatible MINOR release, with three narrow exceptions that may affect callers pattern-matching on specific behavior:
> - **Niagara `AddSetParameterEntry` / `RemoveSetParameterEntry` now require a `script_name` parameter** — calls without it return `InvalidParams`. See the *Changed* entry below.
> - **PIE/SIE rejection ErrorCode changed from `ExecutionFailed` to `NotAllowed`** for `SetActorTransform`, `PlaceActorInLevel`, `DeleteActorFromLevel`, and the WorldPartition / DataLayer / HLOD mutation commands. Callers that pattern-matched `ExecutionFailed` for the PIE-time case must switch to `NotAllowed`.
> - **Physics read-only commands no longer reject during PIE/Simulate** — the 10 read-only `UAIP.Editor.Physics` commands now return `Success: true` with a `PieInProgress: true` field instead of `NotAllowed`. Callers that relied on `NotAllowed` in that case must update.

#### UAIP Plugin

**Added**

- **Editor Toolset bridge commands** (`UAIPEditorAssets`, `UAIPEditorLevel`, `UAIPEditorObservation`, `UAIPEditorWorkspace`, `UAIPEditorEngineManagement`, `UAIPRuntimePIE`, `UAIPRuntimeWorld` modules): AI agents can now call Toolset bridge commands (`Toolset.*`) that delegate to the UE 5.8 official Toolset framework (`EditorAppToolset` + `LogsToolset`). All bridge commands require UE 5.8+ with the relevant Toolset plugin enabled; they are listed as `Available: false` on older versions. Native UAIP commands remain the primary path and continue to work on UE 5.7. Also adds 6 new native commands: `GetVisibleActors`, `ProjectWorldToScreen`, `ProjectScreenToWorld` (under `UAIP.Editor.Level`), `CaptureViewportImageAnnotated` (requires `ViewportAnnotationCapture`; under `UAIP.Editor.Observation`), and `GetLogVerbosity` / `SetLogVerbosity` (requires `LogVerbosityEdit`; under `UAIP.Editor.Engine.Log`). Adds 1 new capability `ViewportAnnotationCapture` (DefaultDenied). Also adds `CVarInspect` capability (DefaultDenied) gating the `Toolset.Editor.Toolset.World.SearchCVars` bridge command.
- **World Partition editing** (`UAIPEditorWorldPartition` module, **Pro only** — not available in the demo build): AI agents can now manage World Partition streaming, Data Layers, and HLOD workflows. Adds 34 commands under `UAIP.Editor.WorldPartition` — 12 World Partition commands (`GetWorldPartitionInfo`, `GetWorldPartitionStreamingGrids`, `GetRuntimeGridSettings`, `SetRuntimeGridSettings`, `GetActorWorldPartitionSettings`, `SetActorIsSpatiallyLoaded`, `SetActorRuntimeGrid`, `SetWorldPartitionStreamingEnabled`, `PinActorInWorldPartition`, `UnpinActorFromWorldPartition`, `DumpWorldPartitionCells`, `ListExternalActors`), 15 Data Layer commands (`ListDataLayers`, `GetDataLayerInfo`, `CreateDataLayerAsset`, `DeleteDataLayerAsset`, `CreateDataLayerInstance`, `DeleteDataLayerInstance`, `SetDataLayerType`, `SetDataLayerInitialRuntimeState`, `SetDataLayerIsLoadedInEditor`, `SetDataLayerVisibility`, `SetParentDataLayerInstance`, `GetActorDataLayers`, `AddActorToDataLayer`, `RemoveActorFromDataLayer`, `GetActorsInDataLayer`), and 7 HLOD commands (`ListHLODLayers`, `CreateHLODLayer`, `DeleteHLODs`, `SetActorHLODLayer`, `BuildHLODs`, `CancelHLODBuild`, `GetHLODBuildStatus`). Adds 3 capabilities: `WorldPartitionEdit`, `DataLayerEdit`, `HLODBuild` (all DefaultDenied). Observation commands (`GetWorldPartitionInfo`, `GetWorldPartitionStreamingGrids`, `DumpWorldPartitionCells`, `ListDataLayers`, `GetDataLayerInfo`, `ListExternalActors`, `GetActorWorldPartitionSettings`, `GetActorDataLayers`, `GetActorsInDataLayer`, `ListHLODLayers`, `GetHLODBuildStatus`) return `Success: true` with `IsWorldPartitionEnabled: false` when the current level does not use World Partition.
- **PCG extended commands and capabilities** (`UAIPEditorPCG` module): Adds 20 commands under `UAIP.Editor.PCG` — asset creation (`CreatePCGGraph`, requires `PCGGraphAssetCreate`), schema / description / parameter read-write (`GetPCGGraphSchema`, `GetPCGGraphDescription`, `SetPCGGraphDescription`, `SetPCGGraphParams`, `RemovePCGGraphParams`), instance management (`ListPCGGraphInstances`, `SpawnPCGGraphInstance`, `GetPCGGraphInstanceParams`, `SetPCGGraphInstanceParams`, `ResetPCGGraphInstanceParams`), subgraph / native-node inspection (`ListPCGAvailableSubgraphs`, `GetPCGNativeNodeSchema`, `AddPCGSubgraphNode`), graph structure editing (`RepositionPCGNode`, `AddPCGCommentBox`, `UpdatePCGCommentBox`, `RemovePCGCommentBox`), and data view / execution (`GetPCGNodeDataView`, `RunPCGInstantGraph`). Adds 5 DefaultDenied capabilities: `PCGGraphAssetCreate`, `PCGGraphExecute`, `PCGVolumeSpawn`, `PCGNodeInspect`, and `PCGToolsetUnsafeNodeAdd`. Do **not** add `PCGVolumeSpawn` or `PCGToolsetUnsafeNodeAdd` to `AllowedCapabilities` in `DefaultUAIP.ini`. `GetPCGNodeDataView` is only functional in builds with `PCG_PROFILING_ENABLED=1`. Also adds 31 Toolset bridge commands under `Toolset.Editor.PCG.*` (requires UE 5.8+ with the `PCGToolset` plugin enabled; listed as `Available: false` on older versions or when the plugin is disabled).
- **Plugin management** (`UAIPRuntimeEngineManagement` and `UAIPEditorEngineManagement` modules): AI agents can now inspect and manage UE plugins without leaving the editor. Adds 5 read-only commands under `UAIP.Runtime.Engine.Plugin` — `ListPlugins`, `GetPluginInfo`, `IsEnabled`, `GetPluginDependencies`, `GetPluginForAsset` — available in both editor and packaged builds. Adds 9 commands under `UAIP.Editor.Engine.Plugin` — 5 inspection commands (`GetPluginDescriptor`, `GetPluginDependents`, `GetPluginTemplateDescriptions`, `IsPluginCreationAllowed`, `IsPluginModificationAllowed`) plus 4 mutation commands (`SetPluginEnabled`, `UpdatePluginDescriptor`, `AddPluginDependency`, `RemovePluginDependency`). On UE 5.8+ with the `PluginToolset` plugin enabled, 15 additional bridge commands are available under `Toolset.Plugin.*` — `ListEnabledPlugins`, `ListDiscoveredPlugins`, `GetPluginInfo`, `IsEnabled`, `GetPluginDependencies`, `GetPluginForAsset`, `GetPluginDescriptor`, `GetPluginDependents`, `GetPluginTemplateDescriptions`, `IsPluginCreationAllowed`, `IsPluginModificationAllowed`, `SetPluginEnabled`, `UpdatePluginDescriptor`, `AddPluginDependency`, `RemovePluginDependency`. Adds 3 DefaultDenied capabilities: `PluginEnableToggle` (gates `SetPluginEnabled`), `PluginDescriptorEdit` (gates `UpdatePluginDescriptor`), `PluginDependencyEdit` (gates `AddPluginDependency` / `RemovePluginDependency`). Inspection commands require only `EditorInspect`. `UAIP.Core.ListPlugins` is deprecated; use `UAIP.Runtime.Engine.Plugin.ListPlugins` instead.
- **Foliage management** (`UAIPEditorFoliage` module): AI agents can now manage foliage types and instances in the editor. Adds 11 commands under `UAIP.Editor.Foliage` — 4 observation commands (`ListFoliageTypes`, `GetFoliageTypeInfo`, `GetFoliageInstanceCount`, `GetFoliageInstances`; require `EditorInspect`; available during PIE), 3 foliage type management commands (`AddFoliageTypeToLevel`, `RemoveFoliageTypeFromLevel`, `SetFoliageTypeSettings`; require `FoliageTypeEdit`), and 4 instance control commands (`AddFoliageInstances`, `RemoveFoliageInstances`, `DeleteAllFoliageInstances`, `ResimulateProceduralFoliage`; require `FoliageInstanceEdit` or `FoliageBulkDelete`). Instance placement is World Partition aware and routes each instance to the correct `AInstancedFoliageActor` cell. Adds 3 new DefaultDenied capabilities: `FoliageTypeEdit`, `FoliageInstanceEdit`, `FoliageBulkDelete`.
- **MVVM editing** (`UAIPEditorMVVM` module, **Pro only** — requires `ModelViewViewModel` plugin; not available in the demo build): AI agents can now drive the full MVVM setup of a WidgetBlueprint — wiring ViewModels, creating and updating View Bindings and View Events, and managing ViewModel properties. Adds 26 commands under `UAIP.Editor.MVVM` and 2 capabilities: `ViewModelBindingEdit` and `ViewModelSourceEdit` (both DefaultDenied). On UE 5.8+ with the `MVVMToolset` plugin enabled, 9 additional bridge commands are available under `Toolset.MVVM.*`.
- **Sound asset editing** (`UAIPEditorSound` module): AI agents can now read and set properties on `USoundClass`, `USoundAttenuation`, and `USoundMix` assets. Adds 13 commands under `UAIP.Editor.SoundSettings` and 3 capabilities: `SoundClassEdit`, `SoundAttenuationEdit`, `SoundMixEdit` (all DefaultDenied).
- **Sandbox editing integration** (`UAIPEditorSandbox` module, **Pro only** — requires `FileSandbox` plugin; not available in the demo build): AI agents can now stage asset changes inside a FileSandbox session and commit or revert them after human review. Adds 6 commands under `UAIP.Editor.Sandbox` — `BeginSandboxSession`, `EndSandboxSession`, `GetSandboxStatus`, `GetSandboxChanges`, `CommitSandboxChanges`, `RevertSandboxChanges` — and 4 capabilities: `SandboxObserve` (DefaultAllow), `SandboxSessionControl`, `SandboxPersist`, `SandboxRevert` (all DefaultDenied). All 6 commands — including the read-only observe commands — require the `FileSandbox` plugin and are not included in the demo module whitelist.
- **Semantic asset search** (`UAIPEditorAssets` module, requires `SemanticSearch` plugin UE 5.8+ and an OpenAI API key): AI agents can now search and compare project assets using natural language queries. Adds 5 commands under `UAIP.Editor.SemanticSearch` — `SearchAssetsSemantic`, `FindSimilarAssets`, `GetIndexStats`, `StartIndexing`, `CancelIndexing` — and 1 capability: `SemanticSearchEdit` (DefaultDenied; gates index rebuild operations). On UE 5.8+ with the `SemanticSearchToolset` plugin enabled, 2 additional bridge commands are available under `Toolset.Editor.SemanticSearch.*` (`Search`, `FindSimilar`). Note: `SearchAssetsSemantic` and `FindSimilarAssets` require an active semantic index; run `StartIndexing` once before using search commands.
- **ConfigSettings commands** (`UAIPEditorEngineManagement` module): AI agents can now inspect and modify Project Settings and Editor Preferences through `ISettingsModule`. Adds 8 commands under `UAIP.Editor.Engine.ConfigSettings` — `ListSettingsContainers`, `ListSettingsCategories`, `ListSettingsSections` (read-only, no capability required), `GetSettingsSchema` (returns a JSON artifact with property names, types, descriptions, defaults, and edit conditions; requires `EditorInspect`), `GetSettingsValues` (returns a JSON artifact with current values; secret fields masked with `***`; requires `EditorInspect`), `SetSettingsValues` (merges a `Properties` map into the settings object via `ImportText`; supports `DryRun`; requires `ConfigSettingsEdit`; blocked during PIE), `SaveSettings` (persists the section to its ini file; blocked during PIE and when `bDisableSave` is set; requires `ConfigSettingsSave`), and `ResetSettingsToDefaults` (reverts to class defaults; blocked during PIE; requires `ConfigSettingsReset`). Write operations are restricted to files under the project `Config/` directory (engine ini files are rejected with `PolicyViolation`). Also adds 2 commands under `UAIP.Runtime.Engine.Config` — `GetConfigValue` (read a raw ini key; no capability required) and `SetConfigValue` (write or delete a raw ini key; blocked in packaged builds; rejects ini injection characters `[` and `]`; requires `ConfigSettingsEdit`). Adds 3 DefaultDenied capabilities: `ConfigSettingsEdit`, `ConfigSettingsSave`, `ConfigSettingsReset`. On UE 5.8+ with the `ConfigSettingsToolset` plugin enabled, 8 additional bridge commands are available under `Toolset.ConfigSettings.*`.
- **CVar management** (`UAIPRuntimeEngineManagement` and `UAIPEditorEngineManagement` modules): AI agents can now read, search, set, and reset console variables. Adds 4 commands under `UAIP.Runtime.Engine.CVar` — `GetConsoleVariable` (requires `RuntimeCVarRead`), `SearchConsoleVariables` (requires `RuntimeCVarRead`), `SetConsoleVariable` (requires `RuntimeCVarWrite`), `ResetConsoleVariable` (requires `RuntimeCVarWrite`) — plus 1 Toolset bridge `Toolset.Editor.Toolset.EngineManagement.SearchCVars` (requires `CVarInspect`; UE 5.8+ with EditorToolset plugin). Adds 1 new DefaultDenied capability `RuntimeCVarWrite` (gates `SetConsoleVariable` and `ResetConsoleVariable`). CVars matching sensitive name patterns (`*password*`, `*token*`, `*secret*`, etc.) return `NotFound` to hide their existence. `ECVF_ReadOnly` CVars return `NotAllowed` on set/reset.
- **Log entry retrieval** (`UAIPEditorEngineManagement` and `UAIPRuntimeEngineManagement` modules): Adds `GetLogEntries` under `UAIP.Editor.Engine.Log` (retrieves recent editor output log entries with optional pattern-based filtering; no capability required) and `GetLogCategories` under `UAIP.Runtime.Engine.Log` (lists all registered log category names; no capability required).
- **`CreateAsset` self-description commands** (`UAIPEditorAssets` module): AI agents can now discover valid `CreateAsset` inputs before calling it, instead of trial-and-error via error messages. Adds 2 read-only commands under `UAIP.Editor.Assets` — `ListCreatableAssetClasses` (returns every UClass with at least one creatable factory, plus factory count and default factory class name; heavy call, requires `EditorInspect`) and `ListFactoriesForClass` (returns the factory candidates for a given `ClassName`, each with its `FactoryParams` JSON Schema; requires `EditorInspect`). Also adds `ICreateAssetInterceptor::GetFactoryParamsSchema()` so `DataTable` and `StateTree` asset creation now self-describe their required `FactoryParams` keys (`RowStructPath`, `SchemaClass`).
- **Data Registry observation** (`UAIPEditorDataRegistry` module, requires `DataRegistry` plugin): AI agents can now list, inspect, and read cached items from UE 5.8 Data Registries. Adds 9 commands under `UAIP.Editor.DataRegistry` — `ListRegistries` (with `StructFilter` and system diagnostics), `GetRegistryInfo`, `GetSchema` (returns `IsSecret` per property), `ListItems`, `ListDataSources`, `ListRuntimeSources`, `GetItems` (masks secret fields — name patterns, metadata, `FFilePath`/`FDirectoryPath`, including nested `FInstancedStruct` — and reports items not yet cached in `MissingItems` with a reason instead of silently dropping them), plus 2 native-only extensions with no Toolset equivalent: `GetAllCachedItems` (reads every cached item without naming items in advance, bounded to 1000 items / 1 MiB) and `AcquireItems` (triggers an asynchronous cache load for custom/Remote sources; DataTable sources precache automatically and rarely need this; times out after 30s by default and returns a partial result). All 9 commands are read-only and require only `EditorInspect`; no new capability is introduced. On UE 5.8+ with the `DataRegistryToolset` plugin enabled, 7 additional bridge commands are available under `Toolset.Editor.DataRegistry.*` — the bridge `GetItems` omits missing items silently and applies no masking, unlike the native command.
- **Asset audit, reference graph, and size map commands** (`UAIPEditorAssets` module): AI agents can now traverse asset reference graphs, aggregate disk/memory size, and detect unreferenced/circular/broken references without leaving the editor. Adds 8 read-only commands under `UAIP.Editor.Assets` — `GetAssetReferences` (traverses referencers/dependencies/both up to a given depth), `GetAssetSizeMap` (per-asset disk size, optionally including resident memory size for already-loaded assets), `GetAssetSizeMapByClass` (disk size aggregated per asset class), `FindUnreferencedAssets` (hard-reference-only heuristic; does not detect code/ini/PrimaryAssetRule references), `FindCircularReferences` (dependency cycle detection via depth-first search), `FindBrokenReferences` (dependencies pointing at unregistered packages), `GetAssetDependencyPath` (shortest path search between two assets), and `RunAssetAudit` (composite report combining the four analysis commands above). All 8 commands require only `EditorInspect` (no new capability). Each response includes a `TruncationReason` (`"OutputLimit"` or `"VisitedNodeCap"`) distinguishing an output-count cap from an internal traversal-cost cap (200,000 visited nodes). `FindUnreferencedAssets`, `FindCircularReferences`, `FindBrokenReferences`, and `RunAssetAudit` accept progress notifications, though because these commands run synchronously on the game thread, progress may only reach the caller alongside the final response rather than incrementally (transport-dependent).
- **AssetManager / PrimaryAssetType commands** (`UAIPEditorAssets` module): AI agents can now inspect and manage `UAssetManager` `PrimaryAssetType`s, `AssetBundle`s, and per-asset rules. Adds 15 commands under `UAIP.Editor.Assets` — 10 read-only commands (`ListPrimaryAssetTypes`, `GetPrimaryAssetTypeInfo`, `ListPrimaryAssets`, `GetAssetBundle`, `GetAssetTags`, `GetPrimaryAssetIdForPath`, `GetPrimaryAssetRules`, `GetManagedPackageList`, `GetPrimaryAssetLoadList`, `GetLoadedPrimaryAssets`; require only `EditorInspect`) and 5 mutation commands: `AddPrimaryAssetType` / `RemovePrimaryAssetType` (persist to `PrimaryAssetTypesToScan` in `DefaultGame.ini`; rejected during PIE, Live Coding compilation, or engine teardown), `SetPrimaryAssetRules` (in-memory-only rule override, not persisted; allowed during PIE), `LoadPrimaryAsset` (non-blocking explicit load; allowed during PIE), and `UnloadPrimaryAsset` (explicit unload; rejected during PIE). Adds 5 new DefaultDenied capabilities: `PrimaryAssetTypeAdd`, `PrimaryAssetTypeRemove`, `PrimaryAssetRulesOverride`, `PrimaryAssetLoad`, `PrimaryAssetUnload`. `GetPrimaryAssetIdForPath` returns `Success: true` with `Found: false` (not an error) for syntactically valid but unmanaged paths, matching `GetAssetBundle`'s `Success: true` with an empty `BundleEntries` array for assets with no bundles defined.
- **Chaos Cloth Asset editing** (`UAIPEditorChaosClothAsset` module, **new**, requires the `ChaosClothAsset` plugin family, UE 5.8, Experimental): AI agents can now bind/unbind Clothing Assets to Skeletal Mesh sections, create and convert Chaos Cloth Assets, read/write Weight Map vertex data, and set the imported mesh reference on Import Dataflow nodes. Adds 10 commands under `UAIP.Editor.ChaosClothAsset` — `CreateClothingAsset`, `AssignClothingToSection`, `RemoveClothingFromSection` (destructive, irreversible), `ListClothingAssets`, `GetSectionClothing`, `ConvertClothingAssetCommonToChaosClothAsset` (Experimental, LOD0 only), plus 4 native-only commands with no Toolset equivalent: `GetClothAssetInfo` (LOD count, Sim/Render Mesh vertex counts, referenced `UDataflow` asset path, Weight Map attribute names), `SetClothWeightMapVertexValues` (destructive direct write of a Weight Map node's per-vertex weight array), `SetClothMeshImportSource` (sets the imported SkeletalMesh/StaticMesh reference on a `SkeletalMeshImport`/`StaticMeshImport` node; the node kind is auto-detected, and overwriting an existing reference requires `AllowOverwrite`, a destructive operation), and `CreateLegacyClothingAsset` (creates a new legacy `UClothingAssetCommon` by extracting the simulation mesh from an existing SkeletalMesh render section, via `UClothingAssetFactory::CreateFromSkeletalMesh`; useful for producing legacy clothing test fixtures). Adds 1 new DefaultDenied capability: `ClothAssetEdit`. On UE 5.8+ with the `ChaosClothAssetToolset` plugin enabled, 6 additional bridge commands mirroring the same operations are available under `Toolset.Editor.ChaosClothAsset.*`.
- **Dataflow node property Get/Set** (`UAIPEditorDataflow` module): AI agents can now read and write a Dataflow node's `EditAnywhere` property value directly, without a domain-specific command for every node type. Adds 2 commands under `UAIP.Editor.Dataflow` — `GetDataflowNodeProperty` (primitives / enum / `FName` / `FString` / simple structs) and `SetDataflowNodeProperty` (reuses the existing `DataflowGraphEdit` capability; no new capability introduced). Container properties (`TArray`/`TMap`/`TSet`) and object references are rejected with `InvalidParams`. This is domain-agnostic — it is the mechanism used by `UAIPEditorChaosClothAsset` to edit Cloth Weight Map and simulation config node properties, but has no module dependency on Cloth.
- **Asset redirector listing and project-wide fixup** (`UAIPEditorAssets` module): AI agents can now inventory and bulk-fix stale asset redirectors after a refactor. Adds 2 commands under `UAIP.Editor.Assets` — `ListAssetRedirectors` (read-only; lists redirectors under a folder, `/Game` project-wide by default, resolving each `SourcePath`/`DestinationPath` from the AssetRegistry `DestinationObject` tag without loading assets; requires only `EditorInspect`) and `FixAssetRedirectors` (always targets `/Game` project-wide and recursive — it does not accept a `FolderPath`; to fix up a specific folder instead, use the existing `FixUpRedirectorsInFolder`; requires `RedirectorFixup`, the same DefaultDenied capability as `FixUpRedirectorsInFolder`). Both commands, along with `FixUpRedirectorsInFolder`, now share a common fixup implementation with two improvements: failure detection uses a set-difference against the pre-fixup redirector snapshot (rather than a raw before/after count) so redirectors created elsewhere mid-call are not miscounted, and the response reports `RequiresSave` (fixing up a redirector always dirties its referencer packages — call `SaveAllPackages` afterward) plus `ReferencerPackageCount` / `ReferencersOutsideGameCount` (fixup rewrites referencer packages, which are not necessarily under `/Game`). No new capabilities are introduced.

**Changed**

- **`UAIP.Runtime.World.GetConsoleVariable` and `UAIP.Runtime.World.SearchConsoleVariables` deprecated**: Use `UAIP.Runtime.Engine.CVar.GetConsoleVariable` and `UAIP.Runtime.Engine.CVar.SearchConsoleVariables` instead. Both commands remain functional and return `Success: true` with a `DeprecationWarning` in the response `Data`.
- **`UAIP.Core.ListPlugins` deprecated**: Use `UAIP.Runtime.Engine.Plugin.ListPlugins` instead. The original command remains functional but will be removed in a future release.
- **Niagara module now supports UE 5.7**: All commands under `UAIP.Editor.Niagara` and `UAIP.Runtime.Niagara` (36 UAIP + Toolset bridge commands) are now available on UE 5.7. Previously the entire module was unregistered on UE 5.7 and every command returned `CommandNotFound`.
- **Niagara `default_value` applied**: `AddSetParametersModule` and `AddSetParameterEntry` now parse and apply the `default_value` field for common types (float, int, bool, `UScriptStruct`). Previously entries were always created with type defaults regardless of the supplied value.
- **`script_name` now required for `AddSetParameterEntry` / `RemoveSetParameterEntry`** *(breaking)*: Both commands require a new `script_name` parameter (e.g. `Spawn`, `Update`, `Particle Spawn`, `Particle Update`). This parameter routes the call to the correct script stack and is necessary for the UE 5.8 External Edit API. Existing calls without `script_name` will return `InvalidParams`.
